1 · Lessons ledger — the codified-problem catalog

CLAUDE.md's rule is invariants over memory: every hard-won problem becomes a row the moment it's learned, then climbs an enforcement gradient. UNENFORCED rows are the standing work queue; ledger-only is an honest terminal state (venue behavior / methodology judgment that can't be asserted). Click a status to filter.

UNENFORCED

learned the hard way; nothing stops the next variant yet

protocol

encoded in a charter / probe precedent

test

a regression test pins the exact fix

invariant

a CI assert fails the build on recurrence

IDDateLessonEnforcementSource
L1 2026-06-18 A synthetic/model price is NEVER a fill price. The pt1 trade lost 9.6% partly by treating synthetic prices as fillable. Every persisted number carries a source tag; untagged defaults to synthetic. Invariant CLAUDE.md prime directive; findings/2026-06-18-codebase-money-map.md
L2 2026-06-18 pstdev over ensemble members without member_count >= 4 produces garbage spread estimates. Invariant arb-bot post-mortem, inherited
L3 2026-06-18 ncep_gefs025 is byte-identical to gfs_seamless — including it double-counts a model and silently narrows ensembles. Invariant arb-bot post-mortem, inherited
L4 2026-07-03 Cloud sessions cannot git push origin main (permission boundary, not a race — proven by two clean rebases that still fell back). Assuming otherwise caused Q1 to be built twice from scratch. Protocol LOOP-QUEUE run log 2026-07-03 reconciliation
L5 2026-07-04 Kalshi's maker fee is 0.0175, taker 0.07 — a 4x difference. A first S13 draft charged every maker fill the taker rate; at mid-range prices the maker fee alone is ~1¢/contract, enough to consume a 1¢ edge entirely. Always take fees from core.pricing.fee_per_contract, never hand-roll. Test-pinned findings/2026-07-04-sports-maker-s13-verdict.md
L6 2026-07-04 Bootstrap the independent unit, never the row: outcomes within one game are correlated draws — resampling by outcome fakes n. Same for strikes within a release, hours within a session. Protocol findings/2026-07-04-sports-clv-s7-verdict.md (block-bootstrap by GAME)
L7 2026-07-04 Never hardcode a bracket/strike width: BTC hourly brackets are $50-spaced (fine bands $100), ETH $10/$20. A fixed-$100 half-band check silently mis-scored every ETH hour. Derive spacing from the ladder itself. Test-pinned findings/2026-07-04-crypto-basis-s8-verdict.md (latent bug fixed)
L8 2026-07-03 A "paired" reference price lagging the event by collector cadence is a confound, not a basis: S8's spot lagged settlement by mean 29 min, fully explaining the observed gaps. Align timestamps to lag 0 (historical minute candles) before measuring any basis. Protocol findings/2026-07-03-crypto-basis-s8-q5.md → fixed in findings/2026-07-04-crypto-basis-s8-verdict.md
L9 2026-07-03 Before joining two datasets, verify their date windows actually overlap: S7a's ESPN pull (Jun 15–21) had zero overlap with the Kalshi tape (Jun 26–Jul 02) and would have joined nothing — or worse, joined wrongly. Protocol findings/2026-07-03-sports-history-s7b.md
L10 2026-07-04 Kalshi's open-market universe is 10,000+ markets; an unbounded /markets pull grew RSS past 3GB. Cap pulls and carry an honest markets_truncated flag — never silently claim full coverage. Test-pinned LOOP-QUEUE Q6 entry, 2026-07-04 run log
L11 2026-07-04 Kalshi purges settled markets ~60 days after close (NFL fully purged by discovery time). Un-collected history is lost forever — collection starts the day a strategy is conceived, not the day it's tested. Protocol findings/2026-07-03-sports-history-s7a.md
L12 2026-07-04 Fine-band ladders near Kalshi's 1¢ minimum ask produce enormous *apparent* overround (KXBTC bracket_sum 7.78 across 188 members) that is NOT an arb — the fee floor and unfillable wings absorb it. Any arb claim must clear core.pricing.true_arb_edge's real fee-floor math. Test-pinned LOOP-QUEUE Q2/Q5/Q6 entries; findings/2026-07-03-crypto-basis-s8-q5.md (overround decomposition)
L13 2026-07-05 A membership-diff over accumulated tape has a startup artifact: everything "appears" at the first capture. Exclude the first capture window before calling a membership change an event. Test-pinned findings/2026-07-05-polymarket-leadlag-s9-first-cut.md, scripts/s9_shock_eventstudy.py
L14 2026-07-05 The sandbox's local main ref can silently lag origin/main by days — every diff against it lies. Always git fetch origin main and diff against the remote tip before trusting any line-set comparison. Protocol LOOP-QUEUE run log 2026-07-05T00:14Z (step-0b sweep caught its own bug)
L15 2026-07-06 Kalshi lists no hourly BTC/ETH group during the 20 UTC hour — a recurring venue-side hole (12/12 incomplete crypto passes 07-03→07-05 are exactly the 20:2x/20:5x captures, both collectors). Expect 2 incomplete passes/day; a probe over hourly tape must treat that hour as structurally missing, not as collector failure. Ledger-only findings/2026-07-06-tape-audit.md
L16 2026-07-06 Match cross-venue/cross-series markets by their own title/rules text, never by ticker arithmetic: KXFEDDECISION uses "26" as a stand-in for ">25bps", and ticker-suffix matching would mispair it. Test-pinned findings/2026-07-06-fed-decision-macro-pairs-q12-first-cut.md
L17 2026-07-06 The hourly collector's push to main fails intermittently and strands tape on tape/hourly-* branches — tape that never reaches main on its own. Every research run must union-append the line-set diff (30-min freshness rule) or the canonical tape silently under-counts. This run swept 1,158 stranded lines. Protocol LOOP-QUEUE step 0b; findings/2026-07-06-tape-audit.md
L18 2026-07-06 Kalshi fee-schedule rates live solely in core/pricing.py as TAKER_FEE_RATE (0.07) / MAKER_FEE_RATE (0.0175) / SP500_NDX_FEE_RATE (0.035); no module may hand-roll a fee coefficient. Supersedes L5's UNENFORCED static-check clause. Honest scope: name-bound fee/rate/coeff constants and positional literal args into fee_per_contract() are statically caught; a from-scratch re-implementation of the fee formula that reads an imported rate is NOT statically catchable and stays protocol. Invariant this run (escalate L5/L7/L17); supersedes L5; findings/2026-07-04-sports-maker-s13-verdict.md
L19 2026-07-06 L7 (never hardcode a bracket/strike width) is genuinely NOT statically assertable: a numeric width literal is indistinguishable from any other constant at regex level, and the one live site (scripts/s8_basis_probe.py BAND_WIDTH_DOLLARS_BY_SYMBOL = {"BTC": 100.0, "ETH": 20.0}) is a deliberate documented schedule map — a name-anchored ban would either false-positive on it or have to sanction it, and the next hand-rolled width would just carry the sanctioned-looking name. Residual hazard this review found: s8_basis_probe.py lines 162/284 use .get(symbol, 100.0) — a silent $100 fallback that repeats the exact ETH-class bug for any NEW symbol (e.g. SOL). S8 is dead and the script historical so it is NOT edited; any future crypto probe (Q7/S10) MUST derive spacing from the ladder itself or fail loudly on an unknown symbol. Records L7's terminal state. Protocol this run (escalate L5/L7/L17); records L7; findings/2026-07-04-crypto-basis-s8-verdict.md
L20 2026-07-06 scripts/invariants.py --full now surfaces locally-known stranded tape/hourly-* refs as a non-gating stderr warning: offline-safe (any git failure yields no refs and can never poison the gate), reports LOCAL refs as of the last fetch only, and never flips the exit code — 35 such refs print on today's tree and the gate still exits 0. Supersedes L17's UNENFORCED-automated-check clause. LOOP-QUEUE step 0b (fetch + union-append line-set sweep) remains the binding reconciliation. Invariant this run (escalate L5/L7/L17); supersedes L17; findings/2026-07-06-tape-audit.md
L21 2026-07-07 When a new sub-pass needs "the set of tickers an upstream sub-pass already discovered this pass" (e.g. S6's depth collector needs sports_pairs/crypto_hourly's tickers), don't re-run discovery and don't thread a new return field through every sibling collector's run() (would change every existing return shape and break their tests) — read the tickers straight back out of the freshly-written tape, filtered by this pass's own capture_id. Zero extra API calls, no shape change to existing collectors. Protocol collection/hourly_pass.py::_gather_depth_tickers/_tickers_from_tape, LOOP-QUEUE Q16 (2026-07-07)
L22 2026-07-07 core/source_tag.py's VALID_SOURCE_TAGS (real_ask/midpoint/synthetic/broker_truth, per CLAUDE.md) has no slot for a resting bid's own genuine fillable price — a live order book has two real sides, not one. collection/orderbook_depth.py tags the ask ladder real_ask and the bid ladder real_bid, a value outside the canonical enum; harmless today (JSONL tape, not a DB row the invariant scans) but would trip scripts/invariants.py's source-tag check the moment a real_bid-tagged value lands in a DB column. UNENFORCED kb/lessons/00-lessons.md L22 (this run); findings — see LOOP-QUEUE Q16 (2026-07-07)
L23 2026-07-07 An empty yes_bids/no_bids ladder on one side of a Kalshi order book is normal for a far-from-strike wing market (real one-sided book), not a capture failure — a depth collector must record it as valid data (depth=0 on that side, ticker still counted captured), never as a DROP, or genuine market shape will spuriously tank completeness_ok on every wing. Test-pinned collection/orderbook_depth.py, tests/test_orderbook_depth.py::test_run_missing_book_still_captures_empty_ladders, confirmed against a live Kalshi pass (LOOP-QUEUE Q16, 2026-07-07)
L24 2026-07-07 L22 asked whether to add real_bid to VALID_SOURCE_TAGS or keep it a separate tape-only namespace. Decision: keep it separate — VALID_SOURCE_TAGS mirrors CLAUDE.md's own literal four-tag trust-taxonomy contract, and widening a project-contract enum is outside a single research-loop milestone's authority (the same class of decision as S9's sub-hourly-automation call and the PreToolUse-hook registration, both left for Ryan). The "harmless today" half of L22's claim is no longer just inspection — a new regression test proves the existing DB-side enum check already rejects real_bid (or any non-canonical tag) the moment one reaches a price_source_tag column, so no live gap exists to close. core/source_tag.py's docstring now cross-references this decision so the next reader doesn't reopen it as a bug. Test-pinned this run; supersedes L22; core/source_tag.py docstring; tests/test_invariants.py::test_db_real_bid_tag_is_caught_as_invalid_enum
L25 2026-07-10 A queue day-count check (e.g. Q7's "≥7 distinct days of tape/crypto_hourly/") must verify a dt=<date> entry is the expected file shape, not just that a same-named path exists. The 2026-07-08 main-rewind caused the collectors to briefly run a different (post-reset) code version that wrote tape/crypto_hourly/dt=2026-07-10/ and tape/sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-10/ as directories of raw per-market API blobs, not the canonical dt=2026-07-10.jsonl line format — a naive ls tape/<family>/ | grep dt= day-count would have miscounted Q7 as newly eligible on unreadable data. 23 hourly passes (2026-07-10T00:26Z–19:24Z) affected; self-corrected the moment collectors picked up the post-PR#35-reconciliation code (confirmed via the first pass after merge, stranded branch tape/hourly-20260710T1955Z). UNENFORCED findings/2026-07-10-tape-format-regression-crypto-sports.md
L26 2026-07-11 Kalshi's 1¢ minimum YES tick mechanically mirrors into a \$1.00 NO ask on any genuinely-far / floor-pinned bracket: yes_ask at the 1¢ floor ⇒ yes_bid = 0 ⇒ no_ask = 1 − yes_bid = \$1.00. A taker "fade the rich tail" trade therefore has NO fillable positive-EV price on that side — profit is capped at exactly \$0 before fees (and fee_per_contract(\$1.00) = 0). In S10's tape only 4/18,992 (0.02%) far observations had any no_ask < \$1.00 room, 3 of the 4 from a single hour. Any real tail-fade edge must live on the maker side (rest a NO offer / sell the rich YES *at* the elevated ask), which is S6/S11 territory (maker fee 4× cheaper) and needs L2 depth + a fill-sim — not falsified by a taker cut. Generalizes L12's fine-band floor-artifact caution to the NO / fade direction. Ledger-only findings/2026-07-11-crypto-reachability-s10-firstcut.md (S10 structural DEAD, verifier-CONFIRMED)
L27 2026-07-11 core.pricing.fee_per_contract(1.00) == 0 is correct, not a bug (a \$1.00 fill has no fee), but a block-bootstrap dominated by ~\$1.00-priced legs can yield a 95% CI whose lower bound is a floating-point +0.000000 — technically "not negative" yet economically meaningless. S10: mean +\$0.000008, 95% CI [+\$0.000000, +\$0.000024] — three orders of magnitude below the 1¢ minimum tick (~0.001¢), i.e. unfillable rounding residue of a few floored trades. A sign-only CI check lets a mechanically-floored population masquerade as "alive"; every CI-based verdict must ALSO clear an economic-significance / magnitude gate (compare the estimate and CI bounds against the 1¢ tick), not just strictly-positive-on-sign. UNENFORCED findings/2026-07-11-crypto-reachability-s10-firstcut.md
L28 2026-07-11 Before running a decay/CI pipeline to satisfy a gate like "clear the artifact floor," first verify the floor is even observable in the data: check what fraction of the candidate population is ALREADY floor-pinned at the earliest observation. S10's far brackets sat at the 1¢ YES floor ~40 min before close (mean early→late Δyes_ask +0.00014 across 18,992 obs) — there was no decay window beneath the floor to measure, a fact the probe surfaced only after building the whole EARLY/LATE-capture pipeline. A cheap early floor-pinned-fraction precheck would have surfaced this before the full build. Same discipline family as L6 (bootstrap the right unit) and L13 (exclude the startup artifact): descriptive sanity checks precede the verdict machinery. UNENFORCED findings/2026-07-11-crypto-reachability-s10-firstcut.md, scripts/s10_reachability_probe.py
L29 2026-07-11 L25's candidate (an invariants/tape-audit check that a dt=<date> path is a regular file, not a directory) is now built: scripts/invariants.py's _tape_dir_shape_issues()/tape_dir_shape_warning(), wired into --full's stderr output alongside the L20 stranded-tape advisory (same non-gating pattern — offline-safe, never flips the exit code). Live-validated against the real committed tree: it correctly flags the 4 stray directories the 2026-07-08 regression left behind (crypto_hourly/dt=2026-07-10, sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-02, sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-09, sports_pairs/dt=2026-07-10) that L25 first documented — those directories were never cleaned up after the regression self-corrected, only the newly-written data went back to the canonical format. Cleaning up the stray directories themselves (or reprocessing their raw blobs) is separate follow-up work, out of scope for this milestone. Invariant this run; supersedes L25; scripts/invariants.py; tests/test_invariants.py::test_tape_dir_shape_warning_none_when_empty, ::test_tape_dir_shape_warning_message_content, ::test_tape_dir_shape_issues_finds_directories, ::test_tape_dir_shape_issues_clean_tree_is_empty, ::test_tape_dir_shape_issues_missing_tape_root_is_empty, ::test_tape_dir_shape_warning_never_gates_exit_code
L30 2026-07-11 Kalshi's maker fee is a flat $0.01/contract at every interior price, not price-scaled: fee_per_contract(P, MAKER_FEE_RATE) = ceil(0.0175·P·(1−P)·100)/100 = 0.01 for all 0<P<1, because max P(1−P) = 0.25 ⇒ 0.0175·0.25·100 = 0.4375 always ceils to 1. Consequence: a maker must net more than a full cent of half-spread-minus-adverse-selection to break even, so the modal 1–2¢-spread Kalshi book (0.5–1¢ half-spread) is structurally unprofitable to quote *before* adverse selection is even counted — this is the structural killer of S6 (spread-capture) and the same fee-floor that killed S13 (the maker fee ate the assumed 1¢ bid-under-fair margin). Sharpens L5/L18's "4× cheaper than taker" framing into a hard 1¢ floor. Test-pinned findings/2026-07-11-mm-spread-s6-firstcut.md (S6 first-cut DEAD, verifier-CONFIRMED)
L31 2026-07-11 A wide (>30¢) bid-ask spread on a far / one-sided Kalshi bracket is a nominal, not maker-capturable, spread — it is wide *because* there is no two-sided interest (e.g. a 0.03 bid against a 0.89 ask). A naive maker-spread P&L that books the half-spread as income lets these unfillable wings dominate the mean: S6's >30¢-wing population showed +$0.339/contract (99.9% "profitable") vs −$0.002 on realistic ≤10¢ two-sided books, and the naive "ALL two-sided" mean (+$0.06928) was *entirely* this wing artifact. Any maker-P&L calc must cap the spread / exclude the wing before a verdict — declaring the wing "alive" repeats the L12 (arb-direction) and L26 (taker fade / $1.00-NO-ask direction) floor-artifact errors, now generalized to the spread-capture direction. Ledger-only findings/2026-07-11-mm-spread-s6-firstcut.md
L32 2026-07-11 For a maker-spread proxy built over hourly BBO snapshots, a *frozen* consecutive pair (bid AND ask unchanged between two captures) is a no-fill, not free spread income — booking its nominal half-spread as riskless profit (dmid=0 ⇒ net = half_spread − fee) is the naive error that inflates a phantom maker edge. Report the frozen fraction as an L28-style observability precheck (S6: 25,618/36,738 = 69.7% of ≤90-min consecutive two-sided pairs frozen) and bracket every verdict with BOTH a frozen-inclusive cut (maximally generous — counts unrealized spread as income) AND a movement-conditioned cut (the only population where a fill plausibly occurred and adverse selection is real). S6's DEAD is robust precisely because both ends came out strictly negative (≤10¢ frozen-inclusive CI [−$0.00297,−$0.00094]; movement-conditioned CI [−$0.02271,−$0.01759]) — the kill survives either fill assumption. UNENFORCED findings/2026-07-11-mm-spread-s6-firstcut.md, scripts/s6_maker_firstcut.py
L33 2026-07-11 L27's (magnitude-vs-tick CI gate) and L28's (floor-pinned-fraction precheck) own candidate notes said each was "likely terminal as protocol... once a probe-precedent encodes it" rather than a statically-assertable invariant — but every probe to date (s6_maker_firstcut.py, s10_reachability_probe.py, s7c_sports_clv_bootstrap.py, ...) still hand-rolls its own block-bootstrap loop from scratch, so the fix has to be re-remembered per script instead of reused. Encoding the protocol as an importable helper (not a memory) is what turns "likely terminal as protocol" into something a future probe actually gets for free. Protocol this run; generalizes L27/L28; queue drained (Q1 claimed by open PR #4, Q7/Q16 DONE, Q13/Q14/Q15 BLOCKED) — drawn from the lessons ledger's own standing UNENFORCED queue, same pattern as L25→L29
L34 2026-07-11 L33 built the importable bootstrap-helper module but left the actual "probe-precedent encodes it" half of L27/L28's own candidate wording undone — the module existed, but nothing yet told a future probe to reach for it instead of hand-rolling again, so the next probe was still one memory-lapse away from repeating L27/L28. Closed that gap in the one place every probe is required to read before writing code: .claude/agents/edge-prober.md's house-style section now names core.bootstrap.block_bootstrap/clears_tick_magnitude/floor_pinned_fraction explicitly and folds the tick-magnitude gate into the three-outcome verdict rule itself (a CI>0 that fails the magnitude gate is DEAD, not merely "worth flagging"). Docs-only — no probe re-run, no verdict re-opened. Protocol this run; supersedes the "encode it" half of L27/L28 and closes L33's remaining gap; queue drained (Q1 claimed by open PR #4, Q7/Q16 DONE, Q13 BLOCKED — 8 of ≥10 valid tape/sports_pairs/ days, eligible ~07-13, Q14/Q15 data-adequacy BLOCKED) — drawn from the lessons ledger's own standing UNENFORCED queue
L35 2026-07-12 L34 closed L27/L28's "probe-precedent encodes it" gap but left L32 (frozen-pair no-fill precheck + dual-cut bracketing) as the one still-open UNENFORCED methodology candidate from the S6 lineage — core/bootstrap.py had block_bootstrap/clears_tick_magnitude/floor_pinned_fraction importable, but nothing importable for L32's own pattern, so a future maker/spread-style probe would still hand-roll the frozen-vs-movement split from scratch (exactly the class of duplication L33 was built to stop). Protocol this run; generalizes L32; queue drained (Q1 claimed by open PR #4, Q7/Q16 DONE, Q13 BLOCKED — 9 of ≥10 valid tape/sports_pairs/ days, eligible ~07-13, Q14/Q15 data-adequacy BLOCKED) — drawn from the lessons ledger's own standing UNENFORCED queue, same pattern as L25→L29 and L33→L34
L36 2026-07-12 L7 ("never hardcode a bracket/strike width... derive spacing from the ladder itself") stayed UNENFORCED for over a week: scripts/s8_basis_probe.py's actual fix only swapped a single fixed-$100 half-band check for a per-symbol dict (BAND_WIDTH_DOLLARS_BY_SYMBOL = {"BTC": 100.0, "ETH": 20.0}) — still a hardcoded guess two symbols wide, not a value read off the ladder's own strikes, so a third symbol or a mid-series spacing change would silently mis-score exactly like the original bug. No importable helper existed for the thing L7's own wording asked for. Protocol this run; generalizes L7; queue drained (Q1 claimed by open PR #4, Q7/Q16 DONE, Q13 BLOCKED — 9 of ≥10 valid tape/sports_pairs/ days, eligible ~07-13, Q14/Q15 data-adequacy BLOCKED) — drawn from the lessons ledger's own standing UNENFORCED queue, same pattern as L25→L29, L33→L34, L32→L35
L37 2026-07-12 scripts/invariants.py's inv_no_yes_ask_arithmetic (Hard Rule #3) scans raw text, including comments and docstrings, for (yes|no)_ask immediately adjacent to an arithmetic operator. A test docstring's plain-English prose "kalshi.yes_ask / polymarket.best_ask" (describing two fields, not dividing them) tripped it — a false positive, not a real Hard Rule #3 violation. When naming a sanctioned real-ask field next to another field in a comment or docstring, use a word ("and"/"vs") instead of /, +, -, *, or % as the separator. Ledger-only this run (Q12/S17 lead-lag probe build); tests/test_s17_leadlag_probe.py docstring (fixed before commit)
L38 2026-07-12 The step-0b stranded-tape sweep's raw total-lines-swept number is NOT a health metric on its own: full chronological series 2,076→223→1,936→873→872→1,708→2,632 is noisy/non-monotone, not climbing. orderbook_depth alone runs a flat ~1,100-1,280 lines/hour (not growing) but is ~3-4x every other family's per-hour volume combined, so whether a sweep window happens to catch 0/1/2 orderbook_depth passes swings the total by ±1,200-2,400 lines regardless of any real trend. A weekly retro (PR #46) read a local 4-sweep upswing as a possible regression before checking the fuller series. Ledger-only findings/2026-07-12-stranded-tape-sweep-growth-diagnosis.md (tape-auditor diagnosis)
L39 2026-07-13 When a bracket-ladder P&L is a small net of two large legs (Σ collected premium ≈ the \$1 payout — S14's mean +\$0.0925 sits on top of a ~\$0.51–\$0.97 winner-\$1 loss), a candlestick/volume fill proxy (high ≥ ask AND volume > 0) that credits the income leg without queue position is biased UPWARD: a bar only proves the price *printed*, not that a resting offer ahead of the whole book would have filled. The loss leg is NOT so biased (heavy volume sweeps the offer — S14 winner filled 96.7%), so the bias is asymmetric and inflates the net. A per-leg volume gate (require the income leg to carry volume ≥ X) is necessary-but-INSUFFICIENT — it caps thin-print optimism but cannot model the adverse-selection correlation between *which* income strikes fill and *which* strike wins (which the queue-blind proxy assumes independent). Before claiming fillability, decompose the edge as a fraction of the thinnest income legs: S14's \$0.072 of \$0.093 (78%) came from sub-100-contract-volume legs, and the "complete fill" gate term was exactly \$0 (0.0% complete-fill) — the fat nominal overround never underwrites; the edge is entirely near-money partial pass-through. Family L27 (magnitude gate) / L30 (flat 1¢ fee annihilates 30.9% of the nominal overround) / L31 (nominal ≠ capturable) / L32 (frozen-vs-movement dual-cut). UNENFORCED findings/2026-07-13-ladder-underwriting-s14-firstcut.md (S14 first cut, verifier CONFIRMED-WITH-CAVEAT)
L40 2026-07-13 validation.v3_market's Kalshi get_* retries on HTTP status codes (429/5xx) but NOT on transport-level ConnectionError — a long candlestick sweep (S14 fetched 6,524 per-ticker summaries) that hits a mid-stream connection drop will raise rather than retry. The S14 script wrapped its own ConnectionError retry (self-contained, resumable via the cached tape/s14_ladder_fillsim/dt=*.jsonl), so no data was lost; recording so the next large-sweep script does the same rather than re-discovering it. Ledger-only findings/2026-07-13-ladder-underwriting-s14-firstcut.md; scripts/s14_ladder_fillsim.py (self-contained retry)
L41 2026-07-13 Degenerate bootstrap: when every resample cluster resolves the same direction (zero losing clusters), a one-sided cluster-bootstrap p is MECHANICALLY 0 and carries no evidentiary weight — no resample can produce a pooled mean ≤ 0 if no unit is ≤ 0, so p=0.0 regardless of the population's true edge. S20's sole BH-FDR survivor (afkpnlucl, mean e +6.6¢, boot CI90 [+1.3¢,+9.3¢], n=174) drew all 174 fills from 8 market clusters, every one of which resolved to 0 (the wallet sold longshots that all lost) — the "significance" was a construction artifact, and the catastrophic leg (a sold longshot that *wins*, −89¢ at this median) was structurally absent because the eval set conditions on markets already RESOLVED during the sample, and in a long-horizon winner ladder the early resolutions are disproportionately longshot eliminations. Root-cause family = resolution-conditioning bias (conditioning long-horizon skill evaluation on already-resolved markets truncates the tail that hasn't resolved yet — the wallet's still-alive book, Spain/England/France, was exactly the unpriced tail). Any positive-edge bootstrap claim requires ≥1 losing (opposite-sign-mean) cluster in the resample unit AND a minimum cluster count (8 is below any comfortable floor — cf. S19's 2-event-hour filled population, L-family: a positive magnitude-gate-clearing CI over a below-floor unit count is a resampling artifact, not an edge). Test-pinned findings/2026-07-13-polymarket-wallet-forensics-s20-dossier.md (Result 2, verifier finding; §Peer review); findings/2026-07-13-polymarket-wallet-forensics-s20-prereg.md (C1 cluster-bootstrap spec)

L43 2026-07-13 L9 (verify two datasets' date windows overlap before joining) recurs at the COLLECTOR level, and there it is permanently fatal, not just a probe-time caution. Q24's binding probe needs tape/sports_clv/ (fair-anchored longshots) joined to tape/orderbook_depth/ (the no_bids resting queue) — but the two collectors ran over disjoint game windows (sports_clv fair anchors cover kickoffs ≤07-03; sports orderbook_depth began 07-07), so every fair-anchored game had already SETTLED before any depth was captured → 0/81 joinable (0.00%), zero event- AND outcome-ticker overlap. Because the calendar date is embedded in the Kalshi ticker string, the non-overlap is STRUCTURAL — no probe-time join-window relaxation can manufacture a match (the verifier reproduced 0 by bypassing the probe's join code). The generalization beyond L9: when a future probe will need two tape families JOINED, the fix is not a careful probe-time check (L9) but collector alignment — the two capture passes must fire concurrently over the same *upcoming* (not-yet-settled) games, or the join is permanently empty regardless of how carefully the probe is written. Q24 is re-testable only on a fresh collection where sports_clv and orderbook_depth run concurrently over the same future window (e.g. a re-collected WC-final). Q25 treated sports HHMM as UTC per its own spec's contract but flagged the caveat explicitly; a wrong assumption only shifts near-close bucket boundaries by a few hours in an hourly-cadence descriptive scan, not structurally, but any consumer wanting < few-hour precision on a sports close time needs an independent source (e.g. matched odds-api kickoff time, L18) to confirm the zone. L47 2026-07-13 collection/orderbook_depth.py's persisted book-side sizes (yes_bids/no_bids price+size ladders) are stored as floats and can be fractional (Q25 observed a real KXWCGAME median best-level size of 91,316.82 contracts) — a consumer assuming integer contract counts (e.g. int(size) truncation, or an equality check against a whole-number queue position) will silently corrupt real queue-depth reads. L48 2026-07-13 A resting-order turnover proxy computed from consecutive same-ticker, same-best-price snapshot pairs (max(0, size_prev−size_now)/size_prev) is a strictly looser, order-of-magnitude observable than a queue-aware fill rate — it is snapshot-sampled (intra-hour round-trips invisible, undercounted) and a best-price move excludes the pair entirely (also undercounted, differently). Q25 used it only to orient cells against the S19 0.45%/S14 2.5% fill-rate anchors and explicitly could ONLY rule a cell OUT (dead-thin, e.g. KXBIG3GAME's 0.48% sitting on the S19-dead line) — never rule one IN as fillable; the highest-turnover cells (WNBA 11.06%, KBO 8.35%) are candidate-generation signal for a *future* queue-aware fill-sim, not evidence a fill-sim would succeed. Generalizes L39's "necessary-but-insufficient proxy" caution from candlestick fill-proxies to turnover-style observability proxies. L50 2026-07-14 The fix for L43 (a two-family join permanently empty because the two collectors ran over disjoint windows) is to source the join's SECOND leg from a same-venue outcome/settlement endpoint retrievable ex-post over the PRIMARY tape's OWN window (within Kalshi's ~60-day purge, L11), rather than from a separate pre-computed anchor collected on a different, earlier schedule. S21 died because sports_clv fair anchors (kickoffs ≤07-03) were disjoint from orderbook_depth (≥07-07) → 0/81 joinable; S22/S23 (Q21 2026-07-14) instead pull realized settlement from Kalshi's free settled-markets endpoint (fetch_kalshi_settled) over the SAME depth-window games, so the join is non-empty by construction. At idea stage, this is the test that distinguishes a registrable candidate from an S21-class permanently-empty join: before registering any two-family-join probe, confirm the second leg is retrievable ex-post over the first leg's own window. Protocol Q21 2026-07-14 idea-gen round (verifier-surfaced); generalizes L43/L9, cites L11
L51 2026-07-14 A "does signal X beat the mid" calibration precheck run on a DISAGREEMENT subset (both directional, X's call ≠ mid's call) produces two hit rates that are mechanically COMPLEMENTARY on a two-way market (hit_X ≡ NOT hit_mid, sum to exactly 1.0) — they are not two independent measurements. Q26/S22's disagreement-subset hit rate (imbalance 27.9% vs mid 72.1%) looked suspicious ("far below 50%, is the sign flipped and this a hidden contrarian edge?") until the verifier confirmed the arithmetic identity: X can only "beat the mid" on this subset if the mid is <50% accurate exactly where they disagree — a bar a liquid, calibrated market essentially never fails, and sign-flipping X on this subset just reproduces betting the mid (zero independent edge either direction). Any future "feature beats the market price" probe on a 2-way market should report the disagreement-subset result as "mid accuracy where they disagree = X%", not as two independent hit rates, to avoid the illusion of an extra data point. UNENFORCED findings/2026-07-14-ofi-depth-imbalance-s22-verdict.md (Q26/S22 verdict, verifier-surfaced)
L52 2026-07-14 Kalshi sports settlement results are not always binary: Q26's live pull of fetch_kalshi_settled over 458 settled markets in 7 sports series returned 8 with result:"scalar", not result ∈ {"yes","no"}. A binary-outcome probe must filter explicitly on the result field's actual value, never assume "settled" implies "yes/no" — an unfiltered join would silently inject non-binary rows into a yes/no hit-rate or P&L calculation. L53 2026-07-14 A queue-aware fill-sim's fill-rate KILL gate (the S19 0.45% floor, Q27 gate G3) does NOT bind when the resting window is long: over a median ~37 hourly pre-close snapshots a cumulative-departures model clears almost any queue (S23 fill rate 95.83%, 23/24), so a high fill rate is NOT evidence of a real edge — the strategy must still die or live on the EDGE (win-rate vs fill_price+fee), which is exactly where S23 died (DEAD-by-fee, not by fill or adequacy). Complements L39/L48's caution that a generous fill proxy orients but never substitutes for the edge test. L54 2026-07-14 Favorite-longshot bias does NOT manifest as a fillable maker-BID edge on Kalshi two-sided sports: buying the favorite YES (normalized ask/bracket_sum ≥0.65) at the resting bid, favorites were marginally RICH — win-rate 0.6957 < breakeven fill_price+fee 0.7361 (mean fill $0.7261 real_bid + $0.01 flat maker fee) — the OPPOSITE of the textbook bias's prediction. With S13 (bid side vs devig, null), S21 (ask side, data-adequacy dead) and now S23 (bid side vs realized settlement, DEAD-by-fee), the entire favorite-longshot / S7-family maker lens is closed DEAD on Kalshi sports at real fills. L55 2026-07-14 An entry-time favorite filter (normalized ask/bracket_sum ≥0.65 chosen strictly from pre-close snapshots, with NO settlement lookahead) is small by construction — S23 yielded only 24 rested markets from 207 joinable games — because a no-lookahead pre-close favorite population is inherently thin. This is the honest cost of an anti-lookahead design (contrast a lookahead filter that would inflate n by selecting on the outcome); a thin-but-honest population that still clears the ≥10-game floor is the correct trade. L57 2026-07-14 A burst-resolution cross-venue lead-lag correlation can be DOMINATED BY A SINGLE SHOCK TICK: on the June-CPI burst tape, Kalshi<->Polymarket rho(polymarket-leads) on the two CPI-sensitive July fed buckets was 0.902/0.777, but removing the single 12:30:13Z release-instant capture collapsed it to noise (0.196/0.037) AND the residual sign is unstable (flips toward Kalshi on leave-one-out) — the "lead" was that one snapshot expressed as a Pearson rho over ~100 near-flat steps, not a persistent relationship. Any burst-window lead-lag figure MUST be reported WITH the release-tick-removed recompute before any directional claim. Two corollaries confirmed this run: (i) the WIDTH x DURATION cross cleanly discriminates a real shock dislocation from a stale-quote nominal basis and generalizes L31 from single-venue wings to CROSS-VENUE dislocations — the two largest gaps ($0.079/$0.06 net of both fees) were single-capture release-instant transients while every durable gap was small ($0.01-0.04), the S6/L31 signature; (ii) tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/ is SIZE-BLIND (only best_ask/best_bid, no depth) and captured_at is a single pass-level timestamp shared by all records in a capture, so it can neither prove venue simultaneity nor claim fillability — every cross-venue dislocation over it is a price observation, never a fill. Protocol findings/2026-07-14-s17-burst-cpi-q19.md (Q19 CPI-burst per-event, verifier CONFIRMED-numbers / REFUTED-both-tradeable-claims)
L58 2026-07-14 A behavioral signal can be directionally real yet un-tradeable by an order of magnitude — distinguish "the effect exists" from "the effect is fillable." S24's near-close mid genuinely mean-reverts after a jump (~0.7¢ net, De Bondt-Thaler confirmed as a synthetic mid-price observation) but a taker round-trip must pay 2× the 0.07 fee + 2× the half-spread (~6-7¢ on a ~3.7¢-overround two-sided book), turning the confirmed signal into a strictly-negative real_ask/real_bid CI (mean −$0.029, 95% CI [−0.052,−0.006], n=123 games/739 trades). Same family as L31/L57 (nominal ≠ capturable) and L39/L48 (a signal orients, the fill/cost test binds) — this is the round-trip-cost instance of that pattern. Ledger-only findings/2026-07-14-nearclose-fade-s24-verdict.md (Q28/S24, verifier-CONFIRMED)
L59 2026-07-14 **Reversal *frequency* and reversal *magnitude* can disagree in sign of implication — a momentum/reversal precheck must report BOTH, not pick one.** S24's raw continuation frequency was 0.454 (a slight MAJORITY of jumps kept going, which alone would read as momentum), yet the sign-conditioned mean next-step pointed the opposite way (post-jump-up mean −$0.0061, post-jump-down mean +$0.0087) — a minority of large reversals carried the mean. Classifying on frequency alone would have mislabeled this DEAD-by-momentum and skipped the real (round-trip-cost) kill entirely. UNENFORCED findings/2026-07-14-nearclose-fade-s24-verdict.md (Q28/S24 Gate 2 direction precheck, verifier-CONFIRMED)
L60 2026-07-14 The S6/L32 frozen-vs-movement maker dual-cut does not apply to a taker round-trip — it has a different boundary. L32 exists because a maker's resting order can go UNFILLED when the book is frozen (no fill, no loss); but a TAKER round-trip fills both legs by crossing the spread regardless of whether the book was "frozen" at entry, so a frozen exit is a real (losing) fill, not a no-fill — bucketing taker trades by frozen-vs-movement would misapply L32's rationale. The correct place for a frozen guard on a taker jump-detection probe is at DETECTION (a zero-Δmid pair is never counted as a jump event in the first place), not at the fill-outcome layer. Records the boundary of where L32 applies (maker resting fills) vs where it doesn't (taker round-trips). Ledger-only findings/2026-07-14-nearclose-fade-s24-verdict.md (Q28/S24 design note, verifier-CONFIRMED)
L61 2026-07-15 **A scheduled-macro Kalshi ladder settles AT the print, so its resolving-month event has no post-release live quotes at all — the tradeable window ends *before* the outcome becomes public.** On the June-CPI burst tape (tape/econ_prints/dt=2026-07-14.jsonl) the resolving events KXCPI-26JUN/KXCPICORE-26JUN/KXCPIYOY-26JUN have close_time 12:25:00Z/12:29:00Z — *ahead* of the 12:30Z release — and vanish from the tape at 12:28:59Z; there are zero post-12:30Z records of the resolving ladder. The 100 "post-print" records in the 12:30–12:49Z window are all FORWARD months (-26JUL/-26AUG/…), a *different object* whose outcome the June print does not determine, and they carry ~0.88 median yes-spreads. Any "post-print staleness pickoff" (buy the now-certain bucket) or "print-overshoot fade" thesis on econ tape is therefore structurally DOA — Kalshi closes the market early precisely to deny the stale-order pickoff. Verify the resolving event's close_time against the release instant before proposing any post-print econ trade. Ledger-only Q21 idea-gen round 2026-07-15, verifier KILL of S25 & S27 (findings/2026-07-15-q21-idea-gen-round.md)
L62 2026-07-15 A cross-venue ask-to-ask "gap" is not an edge when one venue's own bid-ask spread exceeds the gap. Scoring the S26 entry condition |kalshi_yes_ask − poly_best_ask| > overround_share + fee on tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/dt=2026-07-14.jsonl cleared for 19.8% of records — but on these thin far-dated KXFEDDECISION buckets the Kalshi yes bid-ask spread is median 9¢ (mean 11¢, up to 30¢) and the Polymarket anchor sits INSIDE Kalshi's own bid-ask in 62.6% of the entry-met records. So the "divergence" is Kalshi's half-spread against Poly's tight top-of-book ask — Kalshi's true mid already agrees with Poly, and there is nothing to converge (the size-blind top-of-book illusion, one layer worse than L39). Any Kalshi↔Polymarket divergence claim must first check whether the anchor falls inside the Kalshi bid-ask, and must name a realizable exit (round-trip the Kalshi spread vs hold-to-settlement) before calling a gap an edge. Ledger-only Q21 idea-gen round 2026-07-15, verifier KILL of S26 (findings/2026-07-15-q21-idea-gen-round.md)
L63 2026-07-15 A cross-venue laggard trade is market-neutral only if BOTH legs are held; a single-venue "take the laggard toward the anchor" is a directional bet, not an arbitrage. S26 tried to escape S17's cross-venue rail-risk caveat by executing only the Kalshi leg (Polymarket as a free anchor, never held) — but dropping the hedge leg removes the *hedge*, not the *risk*: "convergence-hold" on a single macro bucket to its FOMC/CPI resolution is unbounded directional event risk (the S2/S16 family), and it re-inherits the exact "Kalshi is the laggard" premise that S17-burst already REFUTED as sign-unstable (residual lead flips toward Kalshi on leave-one-out). Escaping S9's data-adequacy death and S17's rail caveat only re-lands the idea on the directional-macro-bet objection. Before proposing any "single-venue convergence" trade, state whether "convergence" is defined anywhere but at settlement — if not, it is a directional position. Ledger-only Q21 idea-gen round 2026-07-15, verifier KILL of S26 (findings/2026-07-15-q21-idea-gen-round.md)
L64 2026-07-15 A "post-close" definition anchored on a ticker's HHMM token, not the settlement close_time (broker_truth), silently mislabels the majority of a population as post-close when it is actually pre-close. Q25's post_close sports-depth bucket (n=2,478, ticker-HHMM-as-UTC) turned out to be 2,860/2,864 (99.86%) still pre-close under the real settlement close_time — median understatement +7.07h, max +24.33h, concretizing L46's ~13h league-local tz uncertainty into a measured number. The genuinely-post-close population was only 4 captures / 2 games. Any liquidity or fill-plausibility claim keyed off a ticker-embedded time token must be re-verified against broker_truth settlement timestamps before being trusted as a population definition. UNENFORCED Q29/S28 probe, verifier-CONFIRMED (findings/2026-07-15-q29-settlement-lag-s28-verdict.md)
L65 2026-07-15 Kalshi empties and settles a sports book AT close — there is no post-close resting-quote window to pick off. Across the entire committed tape/orderbook_depth/ history the maximum observed gap between a capture and its market's real settlement close is 0.024h (~1.4 min), and the 4 genuinely-post-close captures found (L64) all have a fully empty book (both BBO sides None, empty ladders) — confirmed by hand against the raw tape (e.g. KXNPBGAME-26JUL120500YAKHAN-HAN: full two-sided ladder at 11:24:58Z, fully empty at 11:55:18Z, ~30min after close). The sports-analogue of L61's "econ ladder closes before the print": Kalshi structurally denies the stale-quote pickoff by pulling the book at settlement, not by keeping asks near $1. UNENFORCED Q29/S28 probe, verifier-CONFIRMED
L66 2026-07-15 "Buy the ex-post known winner" constructions are near-L41-degenerate by design — record the boundary precisely, don't overstate it. A trade defined as "lift the winner-side ask after the outcome is already public" has edge = 1 − ask − fee, so a losing cluster requires ask to already be priced within the fee of $1 (or a settlement correction/void after entry) — both are real but rare, so while not perfectly degenerate, in practice this trade shape structurally starves the block-bootstrap of the opposing-sign clusters L41 requires. State this precisely as "a genuine but thin losing tail" rather than "provably degenerate," since an adversarial verifier can construct a synthetic counterexample (as done here) even when the real tape never realizes it. UNENFORCED Q29/S28 probe, verifier-CONFIRMED (minor overstatement caught, did not change the verdict)
L67 2026-07-15 Median total-ladder depth on an illiquid two-sided book is not evidence of a capturable spread — decompose the resting size by price band before believing a wide two-sided spread is tradeable. A maker-spread candidate (S30, foreign-sports wide-spread capture) rested on "the wide KBO spread is backed by thousands of two-sided contracts (4,601 yes / 10,556 no)." That figure is the *total ladder summed across all price levels*; the top-of-book at the 27¢ spread was 10 yes / 26 no contracts, and 98.83% of the resting yes-ladder size sat at price ≤ 0.10 (deep-OTM lottery bids, e.g. 4,000 contracts at 6¢). Capture happens at the top of book, so the fillable number is the 10-contract one, not the 4,600 total — the wide spread is an L31 wing with a two-sided tail, not a competition gap. Always split ladder depth into "size within ~5¢ of top" vs "lottery tail" before quoting a two-sided-liquidity claim. Generalizes L31/L39 to the two-sided-depth-illusion case; sibling of L62's anchor-inside-spread check. Test-pinned Q21 idea-gen S30 verifier-KILL (findings/2026-07-15-q21-ideagen-edge-hunter.md)
L68 2026-07-15 A maker-spread-capture idea evaluated over tape/orderbook_depth/ alone is toxicity-untestable by construction — kill it at the idea stage rather than register it as "untestable." orderbook_depth carries resting-depth snapshots only (best_*_bid/ask, yes_bids, no_bids) — no trade / volume / last-price fields — and the only sports executed-volume tape anywhere is tape/sports_history_s7/worldcup2026.jsonl (WC-only, L44). A maker's survival hinges on whether fills are adversely selected (does resting size get lifted disproportionately on the side that then loses?); with no trade prints that correlation cannot be measured, so the L41 adverse-modeled block-bootstrap CI is unconstructible. A wide spread on a thin book is equally consistent with "absent competition" (good) and "adverse-selection compensation" (fatal), and the depth tape cannot adjudicate. Such a candidate has no path to the registration bar and is a clean idea-stage KILL — do not register it as data-collecting/untestable (that would burn a research-loop probe). UNENFORCED Q21 idea-gen S30 verifier-KILL (findings/2026-07-15-q21-ideagen-edge-hunter.md)
L69 2026-07-15 A queue-aware maker fill-sim that rests at the EARLIEST pre-close snapshot systematically enters on thin, often one-sided early books — the fillable-entry restriction (two-sided book / near-close) must be the PRIMARY population, never a robustness footnote applied after a positive headline. Q30's spec population (earliest-entry, median 65.6h pre-close, p90 entry spread 86¢) cleared every binding gate (+9.03¢, CI [+0.021,+0.163]) on nickel bids against 87-94¢ asks days before kickoff — nominal lottery-ticket placeholders the generous fill-sim (L48: a cancel ahead counts as advancing us) still marks FILLED. Both honest fillable-entry cuts (two-sided book ≤10¢, near-close ttc≤24h) failed to reproduce the edge; near-close went negative. This is the pt1 "synthetic price treated as fillable" mistake one abstraction up — a nominal ONE-SIDED quote, not a synthetic midpoint. Sibling of L67's two-sided-depth-illusion (that one attacked a static depth CLAIM; this one attacks a fill-SIM's default entry rule). UNENFORCED Q30/S29 probe, verifier-REFUTED the ALIVE framing (numbers CONFIRMED, interpretation overturned)
L70 2026-07-15 Draw-aversion IS directionally present on Kalshi 3-way soccer, but is not a fillable maker edge — record the outcome-type bias as real-but-unfillable, distinct from L54's absent/reversed favorite-longshot closure. Empirical record (157 games, 19 pooled -TIE series): draws settled 28.0% of the time against resting draw-YES bids priced (nominally) at ~18¢ — a genuine ~9¢ gap in the direction the literature predicts. But under the only defensible fillable-entry populations (two-sided book, near-close) the edge collapses to a zero-straddling CI or goes negative (L69). Distinguishes "bias absent/reversed" (S23/L54) from "bias present but only visible in unfillable prices" (S29) — both are DEAD verdicts but for different reasons, and a future literature-driven candidate should check which failure mode it's walking into. Ledger-only Q30/S29 probe verdict, verifier-CONFIRMED
L71 2026-07-15 The gate-4 "power floor" for a hold-to-settlement Bernoulli P&L is sqrt(p(1-p)/n) — a one-sigma standard error, NOT a 1.96-scaled 95% half-width. Q30's milestone spec stated "~$0.09 at n≈24, ~$0.044 at n≈100," which matches sqrt(0.1875/n) (p=0.25) exactly, not 1.96*sqrt(p(1-p)/n). Pinning this so the next settlement-Bernoulli probe (any "buy the outcome, held to settlement" trade shape) doesn't reach for the wrong CI scaling by reflex when computing its own power-floor commentary. L77 2026-07-15 Forward-filling each market's last-seen snapshot into a JOINT multi-leg ladder state manufactures phantom cross-sectional arbs. This is the intra-ladder instance of L8 (a cadence-lagged reference price is a confound, not a basis). Any joint-state cross-market incoherence must survive an INDEPENDENT re-capture of the binding leg before it counts as real. L86 2026-07-16 The winner/catastrophic-leg measurability asymmetry. When a net-P&L contains a leg that is a large FIXED LOSS (here the winner's \$1 payout), the S19 discipline "unmeasurable → no-fill" is NOT symmetric: applied to the LOSS leg it fabricates a free win and biases the result POSITIVE. Correct handling = DROP the whole unit on that leg's measurability (measurability is exogenous to settlement), never count it with the loss zeroed; AND verify the drop moves the result the conservative direction — here, counting the 290 dropped event-hours as payout=0 moved −0.0453 → −0.0152 (still negative), confirming the drop is honest, not a thumb on the scale. UNENFORCED scripts/s14_queue_fillsim.py; verifier counterfactual; findings/2026-07-16-q34-s14-queue-fillsim-verdict.md (Q34/S14)
L87 2026-07-16 Kalshi crypto-hourly near-money brackets are overround-RICH — the net-buying-pressure / variance-risk-premium thesis is REVERSED on this venue. Last pre-close capture (real_ask) joined to previous_settlement broker_truth realizes BELOW its ask *before* fees: BTC band win-rate 0.140 vs mean ask 0.167; ETH 0.101 vs 0.164; BTC near-money [0.03,0.97] 0.258 vs 0.277; ETH 0.421 vs 0.447. Near-money sum-of-yes-ask runs a median 1.120 for BTC (+12¢, worse than S1's +9.84¢ overround). So the literature thesis (retail lottery demand overprices the tails → fillable near-money left cheap; Bollen & Whaley 2004 / Coval & Shumway 2001, kb/quant-finance/net-buying-pressure-implied-distribution.md) does NOT hold here: the overround is spread ACROSS the near-money region, not concentrated in the wings, and the directional skew that does exist is one-sided (upside rich, downside fair) and lives inside the spread (maker-only, cross-refs L26/L31). Any future held-to-settlement single-near-money-leg crypto candidate is presumptively S1-on-crypto and must clear a win-rate-above-ask-plus-fee calibration cut before registration. L88 2026-07-16 A Kalshi crypto_hourly "between" range-ladder has NO fillable ATM up/down directional instrument. To bet direction you must buy the STRIP of all brackets above spot, which inherits the ladder's ~189% bracket_sum overround (2.89) plus a per-leg 0.07 taker fee floor plus each leg's own half-spread. Any directional-crypto proposal must name at proposal time whether it trades the multi-leg strip (and then price the overround + per-leg fee floor) or a single bracket (which is a pin bet, not a direction bet); absent a fillable directional instrument, kill at idea. L89 2026-07-17 A clamp-vs-rounding discriminator (dead-band vs quantization) must test the GAP relative to the data's OWN granularity — never against an absolute threshold. The two hypotheses for a zero-heavy series are: (a) a dead-band clamp — rates inside a ±band forced to *exactly* 0 while the surviving nonzeros stay continuous/unquantized; vs (b) a symmetric-rounding/quantization artifact — the "zeros" are a lattice bucket straddling zero and the nonzeros sit on that *same* tick grid. The decisive test is therefore (i) are the nonzeros continuous or lattice-quantized, and (ii) is there a hard gap in the open interval just above zero. Q42: the Kalshi perp finalized funding backfill (1,447 broker_truth prints, 2026-06-03→07-16, 13 contracts) has a pooled exact-zero fraction 0.762 with a hard gap in (0, 1e-4) — 1,102 exact zeros, 0 nonzeros in (0, 1e-4), 186 in [1e-4, 1.5e-4) — and the smallest nonzero |rate| *varies* per contract (BTC 1.0004e-4 … SUI 1.0560e-4, a floor near ~1e-4, not one shared value). Because the nonzeros are continuous rather than on a 1e-4 lattice, the zeros are a genuine ±1bp clamp, not a rounding bucket — confirmed on 12/13 contracts (KXLINKPERP undecidable, 1 nonzero print). An absolute-threshold test ("call it a clamp if zeros dominate below some fixed ε") would misclassify EITHER a fine-tick series (real clamp hidden under a coarse ε) OR a coarse-tick series (rounding mistaken for a clamp). Do NOT headline an inferred single tick / ticks-from-zero figure — it is a sample-dependent proxy, not a physical venue tick. Same per-probe-methodology-gate family as L6 (bootstrap the right unit) / L27 (magnitude-vs-tick) / L28 (observability precheck). Test-pinned findings/2026-07-17-q42-funding-clamp-characterization.md (Q42 part 1, verifier-CONFIRMED); scripts/q42_funding_clamp_probe.py
L90 2026-07-17 Kalshi's settled /markets objects carry numeric settlement fields under suffixed names, not the bare ones a naive reader would reach for: settlement_value_dollars, volume_fp, open_interest_fp (all wire-format strings, sometimes blank). A collector that reads settlement_value/volume/open_interest directly silently gets None for every row instead of erroring — a completeness-invalidating bug that looks like clean output. collection/settlement_ledger.py parses the _dollars/_fp variants explicitly and treats a blank/unparseable string as an honest None, never a fabricated 0. Test-pinned Q45 build (collection/settlement_ledger.py:143-146, _to_float); findings/-class discovery, no dedicated finding filed (collector build)
L91 2026-07-17 /events?status=settled&with_nested_markets=true returns empty nested markets arrays platform-wide on Kalshi's public API — the per-market settlement fields (result, settlement_value_dollars, volume_fp, open_interest_fp) live only on the /markets object, not the event. A systematic settled-market harvest that follows the events-endpoint route literally would need one extra /markets?event_ticker=... call per event (~1.7k calls/day at Q45's discovery volume) — exactly the unbounded-pull expansion L10 warns against. The bounded alternative is to paginate /markets?status=settled directly (the same endpoint the four legacy qNN_settlement_cache probes already called, just without a series filter) — one capped, cursor-paginated sweep returns every field needed in place. L96 2026-07-18 Before proposing (or building) any tape/universe_sweep/ strategy that differences a market across sweeps or joins its entry-BBO depth to settlement, verify the tape actually supports a per-market panel — schema presence ≠ signal presence, and a cross-section ≠ a time series. Two failures found the same run (S39/S38 idea-stage kills): (a) volume_24h is persisted 0.0 on 100% of the 100,000 committed census rows (the field exists, volume/open_interest are populated, but the specific signal is identically zero — likely a volume_24h_fp source-field-name bug in collection/universe_sweep.py), so a "volume-surge" trigger is uncomputable; (b) the 20k-call-cap (PAGE_LIMIT=1000 × MAX_CALLS=20) truncates the >80k-market cursor mid-stream, so each pass paginates a disjoint slice — 0 ticker overlap across all 5 committed passes — meaning universe_sweep is a bag of one-shot cross-sections, NOT a re-observation of the same markets. Any consecutive-sweep delta is structurally impossible, and per-market entry-depth→settlement joins get only the trickle of tickers that recur by luck. Check volume_24h!=0 count AND cross-capture ticker-overlap > 0 on the real tape before the mechanism is even conceivable. Same "attack the data claim, don't trust the proposal" family as L9/L43/L50 (join reality) and the general L68 (name+verify your data leg at proposal time). L97 2026-07-18 A Kalshi incentive-program parameter (e.g. discount_factor_bps=5000 from /incentive_programs) is synthetic for economic modeling until its mechanism is pinned by a real fee-paid-on-a-fill check or vendor confirmation — never treat a 4-digit bps field as a settled fee/rebate fact. The Q37 probe (scripts/weather_fee_schedule_probe.py) found the *actual* weather fee schedule is standard (fee_type=quadratic, fee_multiplier=1, empty /series+/events/fee_changes); the "50%" lives ONLY inside a separate incentive_programs object whose payout mechanics (discount_factor_bps vs the separate period_reward centi-cent pool) kb/kalshi-api/03-fees-and-breakeven.md explicitly flags as undocumented beyond field names. A Liquidity Incentive Program is structurally a reward *pool*, so the parameter is at least as likely a reward weight as a maker-fee discount. Reading it as "5000 bps → 50% off my fill's fee" and sizing a strategy on it is the pt1 plausible-unattacked-number failure mode (CLAUDE.md prime directive #1). Any candidate whose viability rests on such a parameter must carry a mechanic_unverified block on graduation, not a broker_truth fee — and idea-stage proposals citing it as "confirmed-live" overstate the source. L110 2026-07-19 Supersedes L109's enforcement column (lesson content unchanged: L25's shape-check flags a directory-shaped dt=<date> entry as wrong-shape but never says what to DO about one). L130 2026-07-22 A temporal-derivative (rate-of-change) reformulation of a depth feature that is ALREADY integrated into the displayed mid does NOT escape the disagreement-subset complementarity trap that killed S22. Verifier reproduced the L50 ex-post settlement join over tape/orderbook_depth/ (197 games ≥ the 10-game floor): on the disagreement subset the growth-side hit-rate is 0.15–0.20 vs the mid's 0.80–0.85 across four charitable constructions, with exact arithmetic complementarity (signal_hit ≡ NOT mid_hit) — identical to S22. Tightening the growth threshold LOWERS the disagreement hit (0.154), a spoof/noise tell, not a stronger signal. The only diagnostic that matters for any "buy the depth signal, not the mid" proposal is whether the signal beats 0.5 ON THE DISAGREEMENT SUBSET; the level (S22) and its derivative (S46) both fail it. L131 2026-07-22 two_sided_share as computed by the Observatory (analysis/observatory/features.py: size-blind, counts a market "two-sided" whenever 0<yes_bid<yes_ask<1 with no size threshold) is NOT a proxy for maker-capturable two-sidedness — on tape/orderbook_depth/ it is monotonically ANTI-correlated with capturable edge. L132 2026-07-22 A sequence gap in a streaming feed is DATA, not a drop — generalizes L23 (empty ladder ≠ drop) to the streaming tier. collection/ws_depth.py tracks one last_seq per market; when a delta skips a seq it emits an explicit ws_depth.gap.v1 line (expected vs got) and forces a resync (reconnect → fresh snapshot) so the seq chain re-anchors — a gap NEVER passes silently as a clean chain, and the collector never trades/rebuilds off an unverified post-gap book. A snapshot re-anchors the chain (no false gap); a missing seq is not a false gap. L138 2026-07-22 Closes L136's candidate. core.timeutil already had a private tolerant ISO-8601 parser (_parse_iso, built for parse_kalshi_ts's epoch-vs-ISO branch) that fixes exactly L136's Python-3.9 fractional-second gap — it just had no public entry point for a caller parsing a standalone ISO string directly, so scripts/s17_leadlag_probe.py (L136's live-symptom file) still called datetime.fromisoformat raw in two places (parse_capture_time, parse_window_bound). Added core.timeutil.parse_iso_utc(s) as the sanctioned public wrapper and repointed both call sites at it. This sandbox runs Python 3.11 (where fromisoformat already tolerates short fractions), so the fix could not be proven against the literal 3.9 failure here — verified instead by construction: parse_iso_utc's fraction-padding regex is version-independent (it normalizes the string BEFORE calling fromisoformat, so 3.9 and 3.11 see byte-identical input), and new tests pin the exact L136 malformed-input shape (...04.7Z, single fractional digit + trailing Z) end-to-end through both parse_iso_utc and s17_leadlag_probe.parse_capture_time/parse_window_bound. The other ~30 files still calling datetime.fromisoformat directly (collection/, other scripts/) were left untouched — out of scope for one milestone; converting them is a separate future pass, optionally backed by a static invariant forbidding raw fromisoformat outside core/timeutil.py. L146 2026-07-22 A vendor's "public market data needs no key" documentation is not load-bearing — probe the live API before designing a public-mode collector. Polymarket US's docs promise keyless market-data reads; a live signed probe (Ryan-interactive session, real POLYMARKET_US_* creds on the VPS) FALSIFIED this — every /v1/ data endpoint 401s unauthenticated, only /v1/health is open, and the block is app-level (returned by the gateway to a signed-but-wrong-scope request), NOT a geo-block. The collector was therefore built gated on POLYMARKET_US_API_KEY with a blocked_key no-op. The flip side is a *feature*, not a limitation: a credential-gated leg with a proven no-public-mode is a structural cloud-safety property — blocked_key (zero network, zero files) is the CORRECT steady state on every non-credentialed box, mirroring CLAUDE.md's "the gate is structural (missing credentials), not behavioral." L148 2026-07-24 Generalizes L142 (the conflict-marker-only committed-tape gate) into a full JSON-per-line validity gate — BUT scope matters more than leniency. Extending L142's check to "every line of every committed tape .jsonl must parse as JSON" is correct, yet a raw rglob filesystem walk also scans UNTRACKED/in-flight files, so a collector mid-append (a torn final line, no trailing newline) would false-fail a GATING invariant and wedge the autonomous loop on data that was never committed. The fix is SCOPE, not torn-line tolerance: restrict the scan to git-TRACKED tape only (git ls-files -- tape, filtered to .jsonl), with an offline-safe empty-set fallback (any git failure → skip, never wedge). A committed torn/truncated line IS real corruption and must STILL be caught — leniency there would defeat the purpose. The new gate explicitly does NOT double-report L142's own conflict-marker lines (those keep L142's dedicated diagnostic). Verifier-CONFIRMED: independently re-scanned the real committed tape (212 .jsonl files, ~1.06M non-empty lines) → 0 malformed; genuinely gating; correct *.jsonl scope; no double-report. L151 2026-07-24 A runtime-version-conditional hazard is structurally invisible to a test suite that only ever runs the permissive version — the enforcement has to be a static ratchet, not a test. pyproject.toml declares requires-python = ">=3.9", but CI and every sandbox run Python 3.11.15, where all three hazardous ISO shapes (...04.7Z short fraction, bare Z, 9-digit nanosecond fraction) parse FINE under datetime.fromisoformat. So the 1,612-test suite is green no matter how many raw datetime.fromisoformat( call sites exist, and can never catch a NEW one — exactly how the debt grew to 33 files / 39 sites while L136/L138/L141 were open (L141's advisory could see them but, being non-gating, could not stop the next one). The generalizable rule: when a hazard is conditional on a runtime/config the test environment does not exercise, a passing suite is not evidence — convert the hazard to a SOURCE-level assert, and where the existing debt is too behaviour-sensitive to migrate in one pass, ratchet it (pin today's count per file; pins may only shrink) so the debt is frozen instead of growing while the migration waits. Invariant this run (idle-run policy (a)); L136 (the 3.9 rejection), L150 (scripts/tape_timestamp_parseability_audit.py: 38.27% of committed ISO values, 638,730/1,669,146, would crash a strict 3.9 consumer); scripts/invariants.py; measured 33 files / 39 sites on the 2026-07-24 tree

L154 2026-07-25 A float-size contract that is only DOCUMENTED gets violated by the very lane that most depends on it: the paper fill model. L47's rule ("never coerce a ladder level's size to int without an explicit, justified rounding rule") was written into collection/normalize.py::normalize_snapshot's docstring at the shared normalizer — and yet execution/fill_models.py::_taker_depth, the code path that produces every taker_depth paper fill, carried a bare take = min(remaining, int(size)) with no stated rounding rule, no L47 citation, and no test pinning the intent. The violation is REAL but numerically BENIGN and I am not inflating it: int(size) on a non-negative float IS a floor, which is the conservative direction (it under-states liftable depth, never manufactures it), the real tape carries no negative or NaN sizes, and a full replay of real committed ladders through the old and new code produced byte-identical fills — zero paper P&L moved. The residual exposure was sub-contract per level (max 0.999 contracts of unlifted depth) plus the latent crash/negative-depth path on a malformed size. The generalizable point is about ENFORCEMENT PLACEMENT, not about this one truncation: a docstring at the producer (collection/) does not bind a consumer in another lane (execution/), and a rule with no single callable home gets re-implemented inline at every consumer — the same re-derive-per-site pattern L33/L35/L36/L49 closed for the bootstrap, magnitude-gate, floor-precheck and close-time helpers. Real-tape census supporting L47's premise, re-derived independently this run over all 17 files of tape/orderbook_depth/dt=*.jsonl (yes_bids+no_bids levels; these are DESCRIPTIVE level COUNTS, not prices — the ladders come from records tagged price_source_tags={"asks":"real_ask","bids":"real_bid"}): 14,756,132 levels · 747,412 fractional (5.07%) · 5,832 with 0 < size < 1 · 0 zero-size · 0 negative · max 13,209,380.2. An audit of every int-coercion site across execution/, core/, collection/, scripts/, analysis/, validation/ found this to be the ONLY one — the queue-aware fill-sims (S14/S19/Q24/Q27/Q30) already keep queue-ahead depth as a float (queue_ahead_at, bid_size_at_or_above, core.depth.capturable_depth all return float and compare with a - 1e-9 tolerance), so no published verdict carries an optimistic queue-truncation bias. L155 2026-07-25 A lexical advisory reporting 0 issues on a clean tree is evidence of PRECISION only, never of RECALL. "0 hits" answers "does this rule fire on correct code?" and says nothing about "would it fire on the bug?" — recall can only be established by a CONSTRUCTED-NEGATIVE corpus: a set of realistic violation shapes asserted to FIRE, checked in alongside the rule. It follows that an enforcement's ledger row must state the tested shape set, not the intent; otherwise the kb records coverage the code does not have, and a future run reads the row, believes the class is guarded, and stops looking. Instance from this run: L47's new advisory (scripts/invariants.py::_ladder_size_coercion_issues) reported 0 sites on the clean tree and its L47 row claimed it "flags any NEW int()/round()/floor()/ceil() on a size-ish name". A verifier probe of 15 realistic shapes refuted that in 10 of the 13 genuine-violation cases — including int(no_bids[0][1]), the double-subscript form analysis/observatory/features.py:160-161 ALREADY writes for touch_queue (i.e. the single likeliest reintroduction path was invisible), plus int(float(size)), math.trunc(size), bare int(depth), int(row[1]), renamed intermediates, size-ish prefixes, multi-line calls, size // 1 and '%d' % size. This generalizes L151 ("when a hazard is conditional on a runtime the test environment does not exercise, a passing suite is not evidence") from runtime-conditional hazards to LEXICAL-PROXY coverage claims: L151's suite was green because the hazardous input was never presented; this advisory was green because the hazardous SHAPE was never presented. Same failure mode, different axis — absence of a signal from an instrument nobody probed is not evidence of absence. Corollary, applied here: where widening a lexical rule to catch a shape would false-positive on legitimate code (depth is an already-integer level COUNT in the orderbook_depth schema; row/pair/entry are ordinary iteration names), the honest terminal state is a DOCUMENTED, regression-tested recall hole, not a guessed-at wider regex. Test-pinned this run (2026-07-25 idle-run enforcement pass, verifier attack on the L47 milestone); the verifier's 15-shape adversarial probe (13 genuine-violation shapes, 10 missed pre-fix, 3 widened post-fix, 7 recorded as deliberate holes); L47 and L154 enforcement columns corrected in the same pass; generalizes L151
L156 2026-07-25 A detector with no scheduled caller is not enforcement — it is documentation that happens to be executable. scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py is a *correct* detector: it names collector_diagnosis="vps_dead" on six families (sports_pairs, crypto_hourly, orderbook_depth, weather_books, polymarket_macro_pairs, perp_tape; 10 families alerting in total) and would have named it at ANY moment across the 61.7h window in which the VPS :23 leg was silent and nobody noticed. It named it to no one. The precise, non-inflated framing (an earlier draft said "the detector worked for 61h and paged nobody", which the verifier REFUTED): a whole-repo grep finds the monitor has no scheduled caller at all — no cron, no workflow, no collection/ call site — and its only escalation path is a documented no-op, [tape_gap_monitor] alerts present but no NTFY_TOPIC_URL / --ntfy-url supplied — ntfy POST skipped. So the failure is not "the alarm rang into an empty room"; it is "the alarm was never wired to a clock." The generalizable rule: a monitoring capability only counts as enforcement once it is attached to the one command every run is REQUIRED to run and to read — in this repo that is scripts/invariants.py --full (LOOP-QUEUE step 4), not a standalone script whose invocation depends on somebody remembering. Corollary for severity-vs-gating: attaching a check to the gate command does NOT require making it gating; a dead VPS cron is severe AND physically un-fixable from a cloud sandbox, so gating on it would convert one silent failure into an indefinite loop halt. Non-gating-but-always-printed is the honest middle. L158 2026-07-25 Commit-author archaeology is UNSOUND on a squash-merge repo — collector-liveness must be derived from tape content, never from authorship. git log HEAD --author=vps-collector returns ZERO commits, despite ~330 such commits genuinely existing on side refs; main squash-merges, so the author identity is erased on the mainline, and the newest VPS commit is unreachable from any ref in a fresh clone. Any liveness/attribution claim built on git log --author, commit messages, or committer timestamps will therefore read a live collector as dead (or, on a differently-shaped ref set, a dead one as live) purely as an artifact of merge policy. The sound method is per-line captured_at minute-of-hour bucketing (vps = minutes 20-29, cloud = minutes 50-59) restricted to kind=="hourly-dual" families, per scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py's COLLECTOR_MINUTE_BUCKETS — tape content is the durable artifact; git topology is not. Same durability principle applies to citing WHERE a tape line came from: cite its captured_at (and the UTC-hour gate it implies), never "the commit that added it" — that answer varies by ref set. Invariant findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md; verifier fetched all 487 remote heads to establish the zero-reachable-commit fact; extends L118's minute-bucket calibration
L159 2026-07-25 The 09-UTC econ slot is a PERMANENT false :2x liveness signal — a raw minute-bucket count will report a dead VPS leg as alive once a day, forever. econ_prints/anomalies land at 09:20-09:29 (an earlier draft of this row narrowed the span to 09:25-09:29; the true observed span inside the bucket is 09:20-09:29 — e.g. econ_prints dt=2026-07-19 at 09:20 and 09:21, dt=2026-07-21 at 09:23/09:24/09:28/09:29), squarely inside the VPS :20-29 bucket, so any raw :20-29 line count forges a live-VPS reading. This is not hypothetical drift: the artifact was present through the ENTIRE first outage (07-19T09, 07-20T09, 07-21T09 — i.e. on every single day the leg was confirmed dead) and again on 07-23T09 during the second. The fix is to restrict liveness counts to kind=="hourly-dual" families, which are the only ones actually written by both staggered legs. Generalizes past the econ case: a minute-of-hour signature is only an identity for legs that share the same schedule shape; mixing a differently-scheduled family into the count destroys the signature. L160 2026-07-25 Tree-hash equality is the cheap, authoritative stranded-tape containment test; git merge-base --is-ancestor must NEVER be used for it. git rev-parse <branch>:tape vs git rev-parse HEAD:tape settles "is any tape line stranded on this branch?" in ONE command with no per-file work (this run: origin/tape/hourly-202607250406Z:tape == HEAD:tape == 23b44416b723b76fca0b130feba53f719d2b5676 → zero missing lines, sweep complete). Per-file comm is only needed AFTER a hash mismatch, to localize WHICH lines differ. The anti-pattern is specific and costly: because main squash-merges, a tape branch is *never* an ancestor of HEAD even when its content is fully contained — so git merge-base --is-ancestor produces a false "stranded" signal every single time, sending step-0b sweeps chasing tape that is already merged. (Same root cause as L158: squash-merge destroys topology-based reasoning; content-based reasoning survives it.) Test-pinned step-0b stranded-tape sweep by a separate tape-auditor, 2026-07-25 run; findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md (§3 + "Reproduce"); relates to L158
L161 2026-07-25 Malformed tape-branch names are a RECURRING defect, not a one-off — and a naive lexical "newest branch" sweep silently mis-triages them, which is a data-loss risk in step-0b. 44 of 192 remote tape/* branches fail the canonical name regex ^tape/hourly-[0-9]{8}T[0-9]{4}Z$, measured 2026-07-25 ~08:5xZ by the exact command this row ships (an earlier draft of this row said "roughly 13-20 of 192" with no command and no definition of "malformed" — unfalsifiable, and re-derived to 44-45 of 192-193 minutes later; an independent verifier measured 45 of 193 in the same hour, i.e. the count DRIFTS as branches accumulate, which is precisely why a row asserting a count over refs must inline its command rather than freeze a number): git ls-remote --heads origin 'refs/heads/tape/*' | awk '{print $2}' | sed 's|refs/heads/||' > /tmp/br.txt then wc -l < /tmp/br.txt (192 total), grep -cE '^tape/hourly-[0-9]{8}T[0-9]{4}Z$' /tmp/br.txt (148 well-formed), grep -vcE '^tape/hourly-[0-9]{8}T[0-9]{4}Z$' /tmp/br.txt (44 failures). The dominant malformed shape is ~22 branches of the form hourly-YYYYMMDDHHMMZ — the T separator missing — including this run's own newest branch tape/hourly-202607250406Z. The substance is unchanged and still verified: tape/hourly-Z and tape/hourly- (no timestamp at all) both exist. Because Z and the empty suffix sort AFTER every dated tape/hourly-* branch lexically, a sweep that picks "the newest-looking name" picks one of these degenerate branches, concludes it has already swept past everything, and leaves real unswept tape behind — silently. Rule: any branch failing the name regex must be triaged by COMMIT DATE, never by name order, and the count of name-regex failures should be reported by the sweep rather than skipped quietly. Test-pinned step-0b stranded-tape sweep by a separate tape-auditor, 2026-07-25 run; findings/2026-07-25-vps-collector-second-death-and-cloud-slot-attrition.md (§3)

L164 2026-07-25 Chunking a one-shot burst-capture window to bound sandbox-death data loss introduces its own new failure mode: a chunk seam (the commit+push+verify pause between two invocations) can land directly on top of the single most decisive instant in the window. Built (this run) a chunked-commit mitigation for Q19's FOMC burst leg with a UNIFORM 20-minute/14-tick chunk plan; an independent verifier caught that this plan's first seam falls at ~17:59:30Z-18:01:00Z, straddling the FOMC statement's 18:00:00Z release exactly. L57 (findings/2026-07-14-s17-burst-cpi-q19.md) already established that an entire burst's signal can live in ONE release-instant capture (removing the June-CPI print's single 12:30:13Z capture collapsed rho from 0.902/0.777 to 0.196/0.037) — a seam-induced gap at the FOMC release risks losing exactly the tick the whole chunking exercise exists to protect, the opposite of its intent. Fixed for this instance with a hand-verified non-uniform first chunk ([16, 14, 14, 14, 14, 12] instead of uniform [14]*6, sized so 18:00:00Z falls safely inside chunk 1). Rule: no chunk boundary may fall within one capture interval of a one-shot's decisive release instant — a uniform chunking tool cannot know this on its own; it must be checked by hand (or by a future tool that accepts a protected-instant parameter) for every event with such a moment. L168 2026-07-26 A 200-OK fetch is not a captured book. tape/orderbook_depth/'s crypto (KXBTC/KXETH) records go completely HOLLOW (yes_bids=[], no_bids=[], depth=0, yet still tagged real_ask/real_bid) whenever the fetch reaches a ticker at or after its own close — 15,175 of 339,947 committed lines through dt=2026-07-25 (4.46%), rising to 100% on dt=2026-07-23/dt=2026-07-25 — and completeness_ok (computed in collection/orderbook_depth.py, never persisted to the tape line) cannot see it, because an empty book IS a valid HTTP 200 response. Two compounding causes, both distinct from ordinary pass-duration overrun: (1) a pass's ticker *discovery* step can run before the hour rolls, so the fetch targets an already-closed contract from the start (789 records, verified: capture 20260723T005605Z stamped 00:56:05Z fetched 488 records for markets closing 01:00:00Z, i.e. *before* its own stamped time by the naive reading — see L169's sibling finding); (2) captured_at is a PASS-START timestamp replicated onto every record in a multi-minute capture (1 distinct value per capture_id, 425/425 captures verified), so a late-pass fetch's true fetch time can trail its recorded timestamp by the whole pass duration — proven on the same capture, whose 488 "00:56:05Z" records were provably fetched >=235s later, at/after their ticker's 01:00:00Z close. For any deadline-bearing market family, completeness must be measured relative to the ticker's own deadline, not merely to whether the HTTP call succeeded.
