L169 2026-07-26 When a suspected failure mechanism is itself a function of minute-of-hour, a minute-of-hour collector-leg attribution is collinear with the mechanism and cannot be tested against it. The first-pass tape-auditor finding attributed L168's hollow-ladder defect to "the cloud leg degrading" (a hand-rolled minute-bucket split gave cloud 52,477/28.9% hollow vs VPS 64,274/0.0%) — an independent verifier REFUTED the causal claim two ways: (a) using the project's OWN scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py::collector_bucket instead of the hand-rolled rule changes the cloud figure to 54,055/28.0% (the hand-rolled version had silently dropped 1,578 clean off-slot records from its own denominator, inflating the rate); (b) the discriminating case is an off-schedule cloud-hour capture at minute :41 (20260721T224112Z, ~18.8min runway) that is 488/488 CLEAN — a cloud-leg capture with enough runway is fine, so "cloud" was never the operative variable, "runway to close" was (see L168). Rule: any collector-leg split MUST (i) use the repo's own collector_bucket, never a re-hand-rolled minute rule, and (ii) be checked against an off-schedule/off-slot capture in the SAME leg as a discriminator before a leg-identity causal claim ships — a same-schedule-only split can never distinguish "this leg is worse" from "this leg's slot has less of whatever the real variable is." L181 2026-07-26 Tightening one gate can LOOSEN another — re-derive BOTH after any change to the population. Adding MIN_ENTRY_EDGE (a correct fix, L176/L179) removed the float-dust legs that had been holding the fabricated-release attack's bootstrap CI below one tick, so clears_tick_magnitude flipped False → True and the adversarial case got *stronger* on that gate while getting weaker on the one that was actually fixed. The general rule: L27's magnitude gate filters ECONOMIC INSIGNIFICANCE, never POPULATION LEGITIMACY — a fabricated population of large edges sails through it. Whenever two gates are cited as JOINTLY guarding an output, any change to the population that feeds them invalidates the joint claim, and the adversarial fixtures must be re-run, not assumed. Test-pinned Verifier round 2, Q48/S55 prep, 2026-07-26; the concrete instance is L178's row (with its exact re-derivation command). Generalizes the L27/L34 magnitude-gate discipline from "is this edge big enough?" to "big enough AND drawn from a population the caller could not choose".
L182 2026-07-26 Do not edit an artifact while a verification run against it is in flight — a PHANTOM defect is the result. The producer edited scripts/q48_s55_fomc_lag_probe.py at 22:33:49Z while a verification run was mid-execution; inspect.getsource re-read the changed file with line numbers captured before the edit and returned the WRONG function body, producing a spurious failure of test_one_liner_provenance_is_rendered_from_the_observed_tags. Nothing was wrong with the code or the test — a less careful verifier would have reported it as a real defect, and a producer "fixing" it would have damaged working code. Source-introspecting tests (inspect.getsource, docstring pins) are the specific class that fails this way, because they read the file at assert time rather than at import time. Protocol Verifier round 2, Q48/S55 prep, 2026-07-26 (phantom failure observed and correctly diagnosed by the verifier, not reported as a defect).

L185 2026-07-27 A capped, newest-first-paginated harvest with no time-window parameter has a coverage ceiling of cap / event_rate, independent of how often the leg runs — and it does not improve as the ledger accumulates more days. collection/settlement_ledger.py's daily pull (MAX_SETTLED_MARKETS=5000, no min_close_ts/max_close_ts) reaches back only ~1.3-3.8 hours of close_time per pass (independently re-derived: 800 rows/1.26h, 4,200/3.83h, 5,000/3.25h) against a platform settling ~1,100-1,540 markets/hour — a ceiling of ~13.5% of any day's true settlement population, non-randomly biased to the few hours before the 10:00 UTC cron fire, and NOT fixed by the two committed live-day files having zero close_time overlap (each day just restarts the newest-first cursor from "now"). Consequence traced end-to-end: the family's one documented join-rate result (Q21/S52's 605/10,605 = 5.7%, findings/2026-07-25-q21-idea-gen-round.md) turns out to be entirely the 605 migrated legacy rows — zero of the 10,000 live-harvested rows are joinable — so "collect more days" will not raise that rate; the cap/window shape is the binding constraint, not sample size. Generalizes beyond this one collector: any future capped-pagination collector must reconcile its cap against the observed per-hour event rate for what it's enumerating, not choose the cap as a bare memory guardrail. L190 2026-07-27 A queue that only a machine can close needs a MACHINE-READABLE close marker — enforcement status flipped in prose is unparseable — and a precision requirement has a measurable recall cost that must be published alongside it. Twenty-one ledger rows were closed in substance by later runs, but each closure lived only as prose ("built by a later run", "supersedes L…"), which no scanner can distinguish from a mention; consequently the rows stayed on the queue forever and the detector had no way to stop re-reporting a row a human had already adjudicated. The fix is ONE canonical marker in the ENFORCEMENT column only — case-sensitive literal DISPOSES:, then L<digits> tokens separated by commas and/or spaces, terminating at the first non-separator token (a . ends the list); a prose mention of an ID NEVER suppresses. The second half of the lesson is the cost: making the agent_charter matcher precise (it only fires when the named charter file actually cites the row's own ID) means it MISSES rows whose charter does not self-cite — measured: 22 of the 164 non-UNENFORCED rows name a charter that does not cite their own ID. That number is published with the matcher, because an unpublished precision/recall tradeoff reads to the next agent as a bug rather than a decision. Invariant findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md (2026-07-27 idle run, policy (a)). L192 2026-07-27 An acceptance test that asserts a LIVE-DOCUMENT advisory FIRES on the real tree binds the gate to that document's current content — and the advisory exists to CHANGE that content. Same family as L191: there a statistic was pinned to a live-GROWING tape population; here a warning's presence is pinned to a live-EDITED document. The property those two tests genuinely own — *the advisory can never touch the exit code* — is independent of whether it fires. L194 2026-07-27 A markdown ledger row is NOT str.split on a bare pipe character — and cols[-2], the obvious fix, is also wrong. scripts/invariants.py::_parse_lesson_rows split rows on a naive bare-pipe split, so any row whose prose contains a pipe shifted its enforcement column: 14 of 190 rows misparsed — L25, L37, L62, L89, L109, L145, L147, L161, L173, L177, L179, L180, L183, L184. The trap that makes this expensive: the seductive repair "just take the second-to-last cell" passes a spot-check on 13 of those 14 and fails only on L147, whose embedded pipe sits INSIDE the enforcement cell itself — so a fix validated on a handful of examples looks total and is not (L186's negative-control rule, recurring in a parser). Only a delimiter-aware splitter is right for 190/190: a backslash-escaped pipe is a literal, and a pipe inside a backtick code span is a literal. The general form: when a format has escaping and quoting rules, a regex over the delimiter is a guess; the parse must honour the format, and the acceptance test must be the WHOLE corpus, not a sample. Sibling of L161 (a count over a growing corpus needs its command); this is the layer below — a count whose EXTRACTOR is wrong is wrong at every measurement time. Test-pinned This run's verifier round (REFUTED the census that L188 rested on), 2026-07-27; findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md. Reproduction (measured 2026-07-27, repo root): python3 -m pytest -o addopts="" -q tests/test_stale_unenforced_advisory.py::test_naive_pipe_split_really_does_break_those_rows — the counterfactual test that asserts, for each of the 14 IDs, that the OLD naive rule disagrees with the correct column AND that it agrees on every other row (so the pin cannot decay into a tautology). The 14 IDs are a named constant, MISPARSED_14_ENFORCEMENT_PREFIX.
L195 2026-07-27 A status-marker detector must match the marker's SHAPE, not one literal spelling. Even once the columns were parsed correctly, the membership test startswith("UNENFORCED") still missed L145, whose real enforcement cell reads UNENFORCED — UNRESOLVED COLLISION, flagged to parent/Ryan (NOT touched this docs-only pass). — the qualifier and the em dash sit INSIDE the bold span, so the closing never lands where the literal expects it. The row was maximally visible to a human (it shouts UNRESOLVED) and completely invisible to the scanner. The rule: a status token in prose is a prefix with optional qualifiers, never an exact string — match UNENFORCED and let the rest of the span be free text, or normalise the cell before testing. Corollary for every future status vocabulary in this repo: the detector and the writing convention must be introduced together, and the detector must be run against the existing corpus at the moment it is written (L198). Test-pinned This run's verifier round, 2026-07-27; findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md. Measured 2026-07-27, repo root: the strict-literal test x[2].startswith('UNENFORCED') yields 22 rows and omits L145; the shape test x[2].lstrip().startswith('UNENFORCED') yields 23 and includes it (python3 -c "import sys;sys.path.insert(0,'scripts');import invariants as I;r=I._parse_lesson_rows();print(len([x for x in r if x[2].startswith('UNENFORCED')]), len([x for x in r if x[2].lstrip().startswith('UNENFORCED')]))").
L196 2026-07-27 Two independent invisibilities can hide the SAME row twice — so verify a count's own extraction path BEFORE quoting the count as evidence that a queue is empty. L145 was missed by the column parser (L194) and, independently, by the marker literal (L195). Either defect alone would have hidden it; fixing only one would have left the audit still wrong while looking repaired, and the fix would have shipped with a green spot-check. This is the shape that turns a measurement into a false conclusion: the audit re-read 21 rows very carefully and never asked whether 21 was the right denominator. The rule: an emptiness claim is a claim about the rows you did NOT see, so it is exactly the claim your extractor cannot support — before writing "the queue is empty", re-derive the population by a second, independent path (a raw grep of the marker text, a hand count, another agent) and reconcile the two numbers. A single-path census can only ever prove a lower bound on what is open. Test-pinned This run's verifier round, 2026-07-27; findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md. Independent cross-check available at any time (measured 2026-07-27, repo root): grep -c "UNENFORCED" kb/lessons/00-lessons.md returns a deliberately OVER-broad count (it also matches prose such as "the UNENFORCED backlog is empty" in L163/L166/L167), which is the point — a second path that errs in the opposite direction bounds the parsed figure from above; the parsed shape-matched figure is 23 with 2 open (command on L193).
L197 2026-07-27 A detector's emitted EVIDENCE must claim only what the matcher actually showed — a NAME match is not an enforcement match. The stale-marker advisory's original wording implied that a hit meant the lesson's enforcement was already built. The worked counterexample is L76: the detector's hit was the RIGHT verdict cited to the WRONG artifact. It matched tests/test_probe_ladder_coherence.py::test_runs_single_deep_snapshot_fails_duration_gate, whose NAME contains "duration gate" but whose BODY asserts a snapshot COUNT (MIN_SNAPS=2) — precisely the mechanism L76's own text says is NOT a duration gate. L76 genuinely IS stale, but for an unrelated reason: L93 later built core.bootstrap.collapse_duration_gated_runs. A reader who trusted the emitted evidence would have flipped the row's status on a string coincidence. This is L165 (a citation is not a verification; only reading the cited artifact is) recurring INSIDE the fix for L152 — the tool built to stop stale statuses was itself about to certify one on a name. Rule: every automated evidence string states its own epistemic ceiling in the text it emits ("the cell NAMES an artifact that EXISTS in the tree — a name match, never proof the enforcement is built"), and a hit is a prompt to READ, never a licence to flip. Test-pinned This run's verifier round (REFUTED the L76 evidence line), 2026-07-27; findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md. L198 2026-07-27 A supersession grammar that matches ZERO existing rows is a claim about the future, not a measurement — report coverage of the convention already in use before adding a new one. The DISPOSES: marker (L190) was introduced with n_disposed = 0, which was read as "no row has ever been closed". It was nothing of the kind: it was the arithmetic consequence of a brand-new grammar matching a corpus written before the grammar existed. The ledger already HAD a supersession convention, in prose, and L107 enumerates the closure map (L22→L24, L27/L28→L33+L34, L32→L35, L39→L73/L98, L45→L49, L51→L103, L59→L72/L94, L64→L101, L65→L104, L68→L106, L76→L93, L86→L99, plus L66→L116, L69→L112, L105→L107, L117→L118, L119→L121, L162→L163, L164→L166, L165→L167); L163/L166/L167 each already state in prose that the backlog is empty. So the "zero closures" reading inverted the truth. Rule: when you add a machine-readable form of a convention that already exists in prose, the FIRST number you publish is its coverage of the existing corpus (how many prose closures the new grammar can see, and how many it cannot), not its own hit count — otherwise the new grammar's emptiness gets read as the domain's emptiness. Same class as L155/L189 (a low count is precision evidence, not recall) applied to a WRITING convention rather than a detector. L201 2026-07-27 A regression test may pin a LIVE append-only document's STRUCTURE; it may never pin that document's ENUMERATION. "Rows X..Z have property P and no other row does" is an assertion about PROSE — it makes the document's author a hostage of the gate. This run's own fix for L152 shipped exactly that shape: a test asserting, against the LIVE kb/lessons/00-lessons.md, that a named set of rows contains pipe characters and that no other row does. It went red twice in one day and forced a kb-distiller to REWORD ITS OWN LESSON TEXT to get the binding gate green — the gate editing the knowledge it exists to protect. The repair: every content-enumerating assertion was moved onto a frozen fixture, and the live-tree coverage was replaced by a purely STRUCTURAL contract (nothing dropped, split lossless, every enforcement cell starts at the head of the 5th cell and runs to end-of-row, n_rows >= 190), which is true of ANY prose a future row may contain. Structure is an assertion about CODE and survives arbitrary appends; enumeration is an assertion about WORDS and cannot. This is the operational form of L192/L200, and it fired inside this run's own fix for L152. Test-pinned This run's final code fix, 2026-07-27; findings/2026-07-27-stale-unenforced-queue-audit-and-tape-pinned-gate-repair.md; frozen fixture tests/fixtures/lessons_pipe_split_2026-07-27.md (49 rows, grep -c "^| L" tests/fixtures/lessons_pipe_split_2026-07-27.md → 49, measured 2026-07-27 19:18Z); live ledger grep -c "^| L" kb/lessons/00-lessons.md → 198 rows at the same instant. Operationalises L192 and item (2) of L200.
L202 2026-07-27 When freezing a counterfactual, freeze the NEGATIVE population too. A snapshot containing only the rows that BREAK the naive rule proves "the naive rule breaks these" while silently dropping the anti-tautology half of the claim — "and it agrees with the correct parser everywhere else". A fixture of only-failures cannot detect an over-eager replacement that misparses rows the old code got right, so the differential test degenerates into a restatement of its own selection. tests/fixtures/lessons_pipe_split_2026-07-27.md was therefore built as all 14 misparsing rows PLUS a 35-row representative sample of correctly-parsing rows: every immediate neighbour of a misparsing row (adjacency is where an off-by-one in a line-oriented parser shows up), the L188 DISPOSES: row, a 1-in-20 spaced sample across the ledger, and the first and last rows. 49 rows total, copied byte-identically. Test-pinned This run's final code fix, 2026-07-27; tests/fixtures/lessons_pipe_split_2026-07-27.md; extends L194 (which found the 14) and the fixture practice established this run by tests/fixtures/lessons_unenforced_21_2026-07-27.md. Row count command and time as in L201: grep -c "^| L" tests/fixtures/lessons_pipe_split_2026-07-27.md → 49, 2026-07-27 19:18Z (14 misparsing + 35 agreeing).
L203 2026-07-27 Do not assert a check STRICTER than the parser it guards. The structural ledger contract was first written with len(fields) == 7 (the canonical column count). That would have gone red on a row containing a BARE unescaped pipe — a shape scripts/invariants.py::_parse_lesson_rows handles correctly BY DESIGN, by re-joining any residual field beyond the 5th column. A guard stricter than its subject re-creates the very defect it was written to prevent, one abstraction down: the gate would again dictate what a lesson row may SAY, while the code under test was already fine. Pin the parser's OWN admission threshold instead — here len(fields) >= 6, the exact condition _parse_lesson_rows uses to accept a row — so anything the parser accepts, the guard accepts. Test-pinned This run's final code fix, 2026-07-27; scripts/invariants.py::_parse_lesson_rows (admission threshold if len(cols) < 6: continue); sibling of L201; the bare-pipe case is L194's.
L204 2026-07-27 Parameterise a live-tree assertion body on its PATH, so "would a future append red this?" becomes an executable proof instead of a reasoning claim. A live-tree test written inline can only be reasoned about; the same body factored into a helper taking a path can be (a) re-run over a scratch copy with hypothetical future rows appended — turning the safety argument into a test — and (b) re-run over the frozen fixture, which is what demonstrates the live check is not vacuous. Proven here by mutation: five rows covering every pipe shape (backslash-escaped, code-span, escaped INSIDE the enforcement cell, bare unescaped, and a DISPOSES:-carrying mixed row) were appended to a scratch copy of the ledger; the new structural check stays green on all five, whereas the enumerating check it replaced fails on all five. Test-pinned This run's final code fix, 2026-07-27; helper _assert_ledger_is_wholly_parsed_and_never_truncated(path) in tests/test_stale_unenforced_advisory.py; the mutation run was executed against a scratch copy, not committed tape or kb/. Companion to L201.

Ledger rows cite tests by node id as their enforcement evidence, and the agent that owns test files is forbidden to edit kb/ — so a rename made in the test lane creates a dangling citation that the renaming agent cannot repair, and the kb lane cannot even see. Four node ids in tests/test_stale_unenforced_advisory.py are load-bearing in ledger row text as of this run. A citation that no longer resolves silently downgrades a test-tier row to an unverifiable prose claim, which is the failure mode this whole ledger exists to prevent. L208 2026-07-27 A per-window density statistic computed only over windows that produced ≥1 observation is a survivorship statistic, not a coverage statistic. scripts/q42_funding_estimate_path_inference.py's min/median/max_samples_per_window is built from the set of *observed* funding windows, so a window with ZERO capture passes can never enter it — of 33 8h funding windows in tape/perp_tape/ (07-17→07-27), 4 have zero passes and are structurally invisible to this statistic (12/33 = 36% path-inadequate once 1-sample windows are counted too). The honest denominator for any window-bucketed tape metric is the expected window grid (derived from the collector's own cadence), never the observed-windows set. L209 2026-07-27 Faithful numeric coercion is not validation — a venue's int64-max no-quote sentinel survives every null check and every > 0 guard as a plain (enormous) float. tape/perp_tape/dt=2026-07-23.jsonl carries ask = 922337203685477.6 (9223372036854775807 / 1e4) on 5 non-BTC/ETH contracts in one pass; collection/perp_tape.py's _f() coerces it with no magnitude guard, and scripts/q43_perp_binary_consistency_probe.py's guard (bid <= 0.0 or ask <= 0.0) only screens zero/negative placeholders, not sentinel magnitudes — a BTC/ETH occurrence would silently wreck the lead-lag correlation (mid ≈ 4.6e14) instead of being dropped. Sentinel-magnitude bounds belong next to null/zero checks whenever a venue's no-quote convention is a huge number, not a null. L210 2026-07-27 A second-granularity capture_id is not a unique key when a one-shot backfill can land in the same wall-clock second as a scheduled pass. tape/perp_tape/'s 20260717T010032Z maps to two distinct captured_at values (a --backfill-funding one-shot and a regular pass sharing that second) — this is the previously-unreconciled discrepancy between findings/2026-07-23-q43-capture-density-advisory.md (31 passes on 07-17) and the 07-23 edge-hunter LOOP-QUEUE entry (30 passes): both were right, counting by different keys. Join/dedupe on (capture_id, record_type, mode) or on captured_at directly, never on a bare capture_id. L212 2026-07-28 A collector that computes completeness_ok and does not persist it makes its own gaps unfalsifiable — and a zero-line pass is indistinguishable from a non-run. collection/polymarket_pairs.py::run_fed_decision (:634-657) builds a full honesty summary (completeness_ok/unmatched_kalshi/unmatched_polymarket/ambiguous_kalshi/book_errors/polymarket_discovery_error) then writes ONLY the tape lines and returns the summary in-process — collection/hourly_pass.py folds it into an in-memory total (:463-470) and drops it, so nothing under tape/ records it. Consequence: a pass where Polymarket discovery raises produces lines == [] → zero bytes written → permanently indistinguishable from "the collector never fired." tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/ is the only actively-collected family this audit has found where a completeness_ok false-rate cannot be recomputed from committed tape (every sibling audit — perp_tape, settlement_ledger, hyperliquid_funding — could quote one). L213 2026-07-28 A recurring collector's own historical cadence at a specific wall-clock slot is a check-able predictor of whether a one-shot burst trigger's fallback will work — and for polymarket_macro_pairs at the FOMC release slot, it says the fallback won't. Over the 10 days preceding this audit (07-18→07-27), the recurring 3-hourly kalshi-collector leg landed zero captures in the 17:40-18:30Z window that a 2026-07-29T18:00Z FOMC statement needs; since 07-23 the nearest slot (18Z) fired on only 2/5 days, both ~55min late. Before relying on any time-gated burst trigger as the sole data source for an event, audit the recurring leg's own cadence AT that event's specific wall-clock slot (not just its daily average) to know whether a burst failure is silently recoverable or a total loss. The load-bearing design finding is what NOT to check. The obvious rule — one capture_id must map to one captured_at — is WRONG and would fire a false positive on hf_burst, whose single committed capture_id legitimately spans 10 strikes over 1.3s because one ladder round stamps each row as it is fetched. The correct discriminator is whether the same logical item repeats under one id: a pass walking a ladder visits each item once, two colliding passes revisit every item they share. Burst families are additionally exempted structurally, on the presence of a within-pass sequence field (capture_seq/capture_mono_ns/round_index), never on a family name-list, so a future burst collector inherits the exemption with no edit to the detector. L228 2026-07-29 A per-venue fee must be a SCHEDULE FUNCTION of the crossed leg's own price, never a flat per-contract constant — and a fee fix applied to one probe MUST be propagated to its sibling schemas in the same run. scripts/s17_leadlag_probe.py's burst dislocation scan charged the Polymarket leg a flat --poly-fee defaulting to 0.0 for 16 days after scripts/s9_leadlag_probe.py (the WC-schema sibling) was corrected to core.pricing.polymarket_fee_per_contract on 2026-07-15 (Q31) — and the WC-semi2 finding's own lesson candidate had already written the rule ("must be a rate, not a flat constant"). The stale flat parameter is wrong in BOTH directions: 0.0 under-charges and manufactures phantom dislocations (the prime-directive failure mode), while a naive flat 0.05 over-charges ~4x at mid prices (the real schedule 0.05·p·(1−p) is $0.0125 at p=0.50, no cent round-up — the L5 maker/taker error mirrored). It was load-bearing on the verdict, not cosmetic: on one identical tape the three views give 0 / 34 / 49 fee-clearing captures (flat 0.05 / schedule / free), i.e. the two mis-specified views disagree completely. Corollary: a silently-ignored fee flag is how this survived, so passing --poly-fee under a non-flat model is now a hard CLI error, not a no-op. L229 2026-07-29 L27's magnitude gate applies to a CORRELATION and to a stability RATIO, not just to a P&L: a ratio-based robustness check is meaningless against a near-zero denominator. leadlag_stability() labelled KXFEDDECISION-26SEP-H0 stable on rho_full = -0.004471504399603508 (|ρ| = 0.0045, pure noise) *because* the leave-one-out recompute moved it FURTHER from zero (−0.040873388781476276), giving retention = 9.14 — and the printed summary then read "1 of 6 leads survives leave-one-out", promoting the artifact to the headline a future run would quote. Fixed with a named LEADLAG_RHO_MAGNITUDE_FLOOR (= the module's own LEADLAG_SIGNED_LEADER_MARGIN = 0.05, so the gate is derived from the code, not fitted to the data: per_ticker_leadlag already refuses to name a leader unless the two lag-ρ *differ* by 0.05, so accepting a smaller ABSOLUTE magnitude as a stable lead is internally incoherent) and a distinct UNSTABLE_below_magnitude_floor verdict checked BEFORE sign and retention, because magnitude decides whether those two measure anything at all. The inflated ratio is still recorded so the pathology stays legible. Honest headline after the fix: 0 of 6, not 1 of 6. Generalises: any ratio-based robustness check needs an absolute-magnitude precondition on its denominator, and the rejected ratio should be published beside the rejection. L230 2026-07-29 L164 needs an interruption clause: it constrains where a seam is PLANNED, not where one LANDS — and a healthy median gap HIDES seams, so a cadence report must ENUMERATE them. L164 was written on 2026-07-25 about exactly this event: a uniform chunk plan put a seam on 18:00:00Z, a verifier caught it, and the fix shipped as the hand-verified non-uniform sequence [16,14,14,14,14,12]. Seam-safe *planning* is necessary and not sufficient; the recipe needs a restart policy that re-anchors relative to the protected instant, and every burst finding needs the mechanical bracketing check rather than a median-gap glance. L231 2026-07-29 A decision-event capture window must BRACKET the release, because the venue DELISTS the decided market at the decision — and the control that proves it is the venue's own universe count, not the matcher. All five KXFEDDECISION-26JUL-* buckets (the meeting this statement decided) are present at all 7 pre-release in-window captures and absent from every capture after 18:02:42Z: 15 pair records/capture pre-release, 10 post, ZERO post-release observations of the decided market. So a window that starts at the release, or that loses the release instant to a seam, can *never* observe the decided market's reaction — the observation window for a Kalshi decision market closes at the decision itself. The competing explanation for five vanishing pairs is always that the PAIR broke (Polymarket book gone, or the ticker↔slug matcher stopped resolving), which would be a fixable collection defect rather than a permanent foreclosure. Ruled out from the capture_summary rows: n_kalshi_markets drops 65 → 60 while n_polymarket_markets stays 15, and the five Polymarket ids 1654956-1654960 — confirmed to be exactly the 26JUL pairs' own market_ids — move into unmatched_polymarket on all 15 post-release captures. Polymarket kept the market listed; Kalshi delisted it. No matcher improvement recovers an observation Kalshi does not publish. Generalises beyond FOMC to every settle-on-event family. L233 2026-07-29 A hand-derived exhaustiveness bound is a SCREEN, not a proof, unless every minimum in it was READ OFF THE DATA — and the agreement between a hand derivation and execution is evidence about the ARITHMETIC, never about the CANDIDATE SET. Round 1 presented an exhaustiveness proof for the dislocation census that smuggled in two fee minima that do not hold on this tape: a minimum Polymarket fee of ≈$0.0004 (true minimum here ≈$0.00005, at pb = 0.001) and a minimum Kalshi fee of $0.01 on both crossed legs — but core.pricing.fee_per_contract returns $0.00, not $0.01, on a leg priced 0.00, and four buckets quote a 0.00 yes_bid. The result survived only because an independent verifier ran a fully exhaustive 96-distinct-quote-tuple census requiring no bound at all and landed on the same floats. A fee floor that "can never be zero" usually can. Second half, equally load-bearing: execution reproduced the hand-derived headline EXACTLY (34 captures / 5 episodes / max +$0.008220000000000035 vs a hand derivation of 34 / 5 / +$0.00822), which is real evidence about the arithmetic and none whatever about completeness — a hand derivation and the script it mirrors can share the same missed region, and here they did. L234 2026-07-29 pytest -q on top of a pyproject addopts that already carries -q doubles to -qq and SUPPRESSES the summary line — in a repo whose gate IS that count. L235 2026-07-29 An argmax over a SIGNED statistic can crown a negative one, and a magnitude floor only catches that by luck — a sign precondition and a magnitude gate are independent gates, neither implying the other. per_ticker_leadlag's signed_leader maximises the signed lag-ρ, so when both directions are negative it names the less negative one the leader: KXFEDDECISION-26SEP-H0 got leader = polymarket on ρ = −0.0045 over Kalshi's −0.2536, because −0.0045 > −0.2536 as a signed comparison. "Less negative" is not a lead in any economic reading. L229's magnitude floor rejected this row only incidentally (|−0.0045| happens to fall below 0.05); the verifier's counterexample makes the residual gap concrete — rho_k = −0.30 / rho_p = −0.20 clears a 0.05 magnitude floor comfortably and would be labelled stable, shipping a directional-lead claim built entirely on the less-negative of two negative correlations. PRE-EXISTING, not introduced by this milestone (git show confirms this round's diff only renamed the inline 0.05 to LEADLAG_SIGNED_LEADER_MARGIN; the comparison is unchanged context), and it cannot flatter the verdict: it yields MORE directional leaders than a sign-gated test would, on a headline that came back 0 of 6 survive. L236 2026-07-29 An "N of M are artifacts" decomposition must be computed PER-OBSERVATION, not read off per-episode maxima — and a float residue belongs to the one subtraction that produced it, never to the group. The generous free bracket reported 49 fee-clearing captures / 10 episodes, of which 3 episodes / 5 captures carry an edge that is pure floating-point residue (2⁻⁵⁸ multiples in the 10⁻¹⁷-dollar range, fifteen orders of magnitude below a tick). Two distinct errors, both caught by the verifier round on the very bullet that was drawing the lesson: (i) the exact float 1.734723475976807e-17 was asserted of all three episodes when it belongs to exactly one (26JUL-H25's 0.26 − 0.24 − 0.02 = 5·2⁻⁵⁸); the 26SEP-H25 pair are 0.61 − 0.59 − 0.02 ≈ 3.955e-17 and 0.62 − 0.60 − 0.02 ≈ 2.776e-17 — residues from different subtractions are different residues, so quote the magnitude CLASS for the group and the exact float only for the case it belongs to; and (ii) the residue count was read off episode maxima, which hides a fifth residue capture (26JUL-H0 at 17:46:12Z, whose episode max is +0.003 while that individual capture's own edge is residue-level) — so the honest complement is 44 of 49, not 45. An artifact share computed at the wrong granularity is always understated in the flattering direction. (NUMERIC CORRECTION, 2026-07-30, research loop idle-run policy (a) — produced BY building this row's enforcement; EXECUTED by the producing research-loop session (the independent verifier re-derived the same three floats by exact IEEE-754 integer arithmetic — its harness denied code execution — and CONFIRMED): repr(0.26-0.24-0.02), repr(0.61-0.59-0.02) and repr(0.62-0.60-0.02) ALL evaluate to EXACTLY 1.734723475976807e-17 = 5·2⁻⁵⁸ in IEEE-754 double. So clause (i) above is wrong on its own facts: the three residues are the SAME float, and round 2's shared-float claim — which the verifier round "corrected" to ≈3.955e-17 / ≈2.776e-17 — was RIGHT. The PRINCIPLE stands unchanged (residues from different subtractions are in general different, and you only know by evaluating); this particular trio coincides. Clause (ii) and every COUNT reproduce exactly on the committed tape this run: per-observation 5 residue captures, per-episode-max 3 episodes / 4 captures, complement 44 of 49 — note the text's "3 episodes / 5 captures" mixes the two views, the honest pairing is 3 episodes / 4 captures (max view) vs 5 captures (per-observation). Pinned by tests/test_bootstrap.py::test_l236_the_three_fomc_residue_subtractions_are_the_SAME_float.) L238 2026-07-30 A window-grid denominator is defined by TWO numbers — width AND anchor — and getting the anchor wrong produces a confident, precisely-wrong answer that the width alone cannot expose. L208's own worked example (findings/2026-07-27-perp-tape-audit.md PERP-F1) named four permanently-lost 8h funding windows in tape/perp_tape/ — 2026-07-23T08Z, 07-24T08Z, 07-25T08Z, 07-25T16Z. Those are 00Z-anchored CALENDAR bins over captured_at. Kalshi perps' actual funding boundaries, read off the collector's own next_funding_time field, land on the 04/12/20Z grid: 1,534 of 1,534 committed funding_estimate rows are on that anchor and zero on 00/08/16. On the same tape and the same 33-window span the honest grid has THREE zero-pass windows — 07-24T04Z, 07-25T04Z, 07-25T20Z — a set disjoint from the four reported (not one instant in common), and 10/33 = 30.3% path-inadequate rather than 12/33 = 36%. The width (8h) was right in both readings, so nothing about the count, the cadence or the span looked wrong; only re-deriving the anchor from the collector's own boundary field exposed it. Rules: (1) a window grid must be enumerated from the field the VENUE publishes the boundary in, never from captured_at binned to a wall-clock multiple; (2) a boundary value that is off the assumed grid must be COUNTED AND REPORTED, never snapped to the nearest slot — snapping is exactly what converts a mis-anchored grid into a plausible wrong answer; (3) a defect report naming specific lost windows must cite the boundary field it read them from, because the instants are the claim. Test-pinned This run's build of L208's own enforcement candidate: scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py::expected_window_grid_coverage (executed against real committed tape 2026-07-30, and independently re-derived beforehand by a from-scratch scratch script that reproduced BOTH readings — the audit's four 00Z bins exactly, and the three 04Z-grid windows); findings/2026-07-30-l208-window-grid-coverage.md.
L239 2026-07-30 An auditability predicate must key on the EXACT field the deriving function reads — keying on a superset manufactures a "checked" that was never checked. The L214 census asked "does this record carry the evidence its verdict was derived from?" with group_item_title or question, while collection.polymarket_pairs.fed_bucket_terms's Polymarket side reads group_item_title and NOTHING ELSE. A record with a null group_item_title and a rich question therefore reported has_terms: True sitting right beside polymarket_basis: None — the census would have certified as "terms present and checked" exactly the records whose basis the deriving function could not parse, i.e. it would have over-counted auditability in the flattering direction. Fixed by making the predicate field-exact: classify_record's poly_text_ok keys on group_item_title only, and poly_question_present is reported for visibility and feeds no count. General form: "has evidence" is not a semantic question about the record, it is a mechanical question about the READER — write the predicate against the reader's own field list, and if the two ever diverge the census is measuring a different thing than the function. L240 2026-07-30 Never pool "never asked" with "asked, and undecidable" — they are opposite kinds of ignorance, and one name for both hides which one you have. The census first reported a single n_terms_equivalent_null = 9,555, a name that reads as *9,555 records were examined and came back undecidable*. In fact all 9,555 are pre-v2 records carrying no bucket_terms FIELD AT ALL: they were never asked, so they cannot have answered "unknown". The counters are now split — n_no_terms_block (no block at all) vs n_terms_verdict_null (block present, verdict None) — and the report publishes the partition identity n_pair_records == n_terms_equivalent_true + n_terms_equivalent_false + n_terms_verdict_null + n_no_terms_block so any future miscount is arithmetically visible rather than merely mis-named. Measured: python3 scripts/polymarket_pair_terms_audit.py (over tape/polymarket_macro_pairs/ in this working tree, run 2026-07-30T20:22:13Z) → 12 + 8 + 0 + 9,555 = 9,575, and the same identity holds per bucket (5 buckets × 4 + 0 + 0 + 1,911 = 1,915). Sibling of L86 (an unmeasurable observation is dropped and reported, never booked as a zero) and of L212 (a check that dies before it reaches tape). L241 2026-07-30 A public verdict function must VERIFY the join key it presupposes, not inherit it from its only caller — and when it cannot verify it, it must say so in the record rather than claim a silent pass. fed_bucket_terms compares two legs' bps regions. It was safe only because its one in-repo caller, match_fed_pairs, joins on meeting_key first — but it is an importable public function, and called directly with a Sept-2026 Kalshi title ("Will the Federal Reserve Hike rates by 25bps at their September 2026 meeting?") against a Jan-2099 Polymarket leg it returned terms_equivalent: True: identical bps regions at two different meetings, which settle on different days and are not the same contract. Fixed three ways, all persisted: the function compares meeting_key explicitly (mismatch ⇒ False with a note naming both keys), it emits meeting_key_checked: True/False so a caller that supplied no meeting keys is never told the join was verified, and the block names its own scope with compares: "bps_region+meeting_key". The scope string is load-bearing: text equivalence is not settlement equivalence when the adjudicators differ (kalshi_rulebook vs uma_oracle, persisted per leg), so even a True leaves adjudicator risk unmeasured and no text comparison can settle it. L243 2026-07-30 Two reporting rules a "schema bump + tape append" pass must follow, because the two effects of such a diff are always confounded in the default invocation. (i) "No probe output changed" is only checkable TAPE-CONSTANT. The honest claim is: held tape-constant (old code vs new code on a frozen tape, via a git worktree differential), q48 / q31 / s17 outputs are byte-identical. It is FALSE to say "no probe's numeric output changed", because the same diff also appends 20 records, which moves every default invocation — q48 n_fed_records 9,555 → 9,575, n_passes 640 → 641, mean_abs 0.0206128 → 0.0206215, headline all +2.061¢ → +2.062¢; q31 obs 15,905 → 15,925, pooled mean net −0.03244 → −0.03246, 95% CI [−0.04006,−0.02540] → [−0.04007,−0.02541]; s17 contemporaneous ρ 0.43582 → 0.43724, kalshi-leads ρ 0.019706 → 0.015051 (all prices price_source_tag: real_ask, 9,575/9,575 records; DESCRIPTIVE/diagnostic statistics, not a strategy CI and not a P&L). State the two deltas separately, with numbers, or the code claim silently absorbs the data claim. (ii) When a verdict is a deterministic function of a parsed label, the RECORD count is not a sample size. The 20 v2 records look like n=20 but come from ONE capture pass, and terms_equivalent is a pure function of the parsed threshold-basis pair (kalshi_basis, polymarket_basis) — NOT of the literal (title, label) pair, which is distinct on all 20 only because the Kalshi title embeds the meeting month. Collapsing on the parsed bases gives 5 bucket classes (3 equivalent, 2 provably not), n=4 each: 20 = 5 classes × 4 meetings × 1 pass. And the two non-equivalent classes are the SAME >25bps-vs->=50bps rule mirrored hike/cut, so the number of independent asymmetry mechanisms is 1, not 2 — say so, or a reader takes "2" as corroboration. Reporting either number without its collapse is pseudo-replication (L6's unit rule, applied to a parser instead of a bootstrap). Protocol findings/2026-07-30-l214-crossvenue-terms-provenance.md; independent verifier round 1 defect 2 (probe-impact wording) + round 2 (unit-count/pseudo-replication wording), this run
L244 2026-07-31 A self-activation gate that counts DAYS must bound its window at BOTH ends and whitelist the instrument grammar it means — an unbounded season predicate plus an unfiltered family tape lets markets that can never settle in the study window buy the gate days it did not earn. Q37's _summer_contract_days_available() reported 19 summer daily contract-days where only 17 existed. Two compounding defects, each individually harmless-looking: is_summer(d) was d >= SUMMER_START with no upper bound (so a 2028-12-31 contract day is "summer 2026"), and load_daily_snapshots() had no series whitelist (so tape/weather_books/, which is captured by weather FAMILY not by ladder SHAPE, fed KXARCTICICEMIN-26OCT01-* and KXTXURI-28DEC31-* into a daily-temperature-ladder population). Each contaminant bought exactly one phantom gate day — enough to fire the >=21 gate two calendar days early on 19 real days of tape, a 10% shortfall against the gate's own design, silently. The population contamination was small (10 of 2,289 trades, 0.44%); the GATE contamination was the damage. Generalization for every gated probe in this repo: a day-count gate is an argument about which days COUNT, so it must be tested against rows that are the right shape on the wrong day AND the wrong shape on the right day, not just the happy case. Corollary that made this findable at all: fix by TIGHTENING only — both guards can solely remove rows, so re-running can never admit something the old code rejected. Test-pinned findings/2026-07-31-weather-gate-preflight-audit.md; scripts/weather_revival_gate_preflight_audit.py (re-derives 19-vs-17 under both admission rules after the fix landed); measured on committed tape/weather_books/ through dt=2026-07-31
L245 2026-07-31 A fix that ENUMERATES where it should DERIVE reproduces, one layer up, the exact defect it was written to remove — and the second instance is harder to see because a check now visibly "fires". L216 found that oversized tape day-files were size-guard-SKIPPED and the skip was misread as "checked and clean", missing 21,303 stranded lines. Its disposition L217 fixed that by adding a capture_id-set fallback — gated on a hard-coded BULK_CAPTURE_ID_FAMILIES allowlist of the four families it could then NAME. L217's own text even flagged the smell (it had to add weather_books as a fourth the same day it shipped), and still shipped the enumeration. Measured on this run's full 218-branch sweep: 126 branches (57.8%) were still reported no problem found but NOT FULLY VERIFIED — no containment check of any kind — solely because their only oversized files were crypto_hourly (121 branches), econ_prints (5) and anomalies (1), each of which carries capture_id and answers the check perfectly (verified live before the fix: crypto_hourly/dt=2026-07-14.jsonl 116 branch ids vs 143 HEAD ids, econ_prints/dt=2026-07-14.jsonl 137 vs 137, anomalies/dt=2026-07-21.jsonl 20 vs 21 — all 0 missing). The general rule: when a guard's applicability is a property of the data (does this blob carry the key the check needs?), ask the data, never a list of names. A name-list is a snapshot of what was true on the day it was written and silently rots as families grow past the threshold or gain fields; worse, the presence of a partially-firing check makes the remaining hole look covered. The honest-skip semantics L217 already relied on (capture_ids_in_blob returning empty == "no signal" == skip) is exactly what makes the derivation safe to apply universally — the extraction itself is the capability test. Post-fix on the same 218 branches: unverified 126 → 0, capture_id-verified 48 → 174; the genuinely-missing set was unchanged at 14, so this closed a blind spot rather than uncovering new loss (a null result that is itself the point — the hole had to be measured, not assumed empty). Sweep cost 5m09s wall-clock for all 218 branches. L248 2026-08-01 A single redundancy percentage cannot accuse anything until it is decomposed by SCOPE, and a duplicate-capture detector that does not excuse DECLARED re-capture manufactures its own incidents. Building L221's measurement half, a first draft reported one whole-slice redundant_line_fraction — the number L221 itself quoted. On real tape that lump conflated three mechanisms with three different culprits: duplicates written INSIDE one pass (the collector, not the gate), duplicates across passes in the SAME day (the gate — the only share L221 is about), and the same payload re-reported on a LATER day (a legitimate retrospective family doing its job). The nesting n_lines >= D_pass >= D_day >= D_all makes the three deltas an exact partition, so redundancy_decomposition costs nothing and the alternative — quoting the lump — would have booked weather_actuals' cross-day re-reports as gate leakage. Two sibling errors surfaced in the same build and are pinned as tests, not prose: (a) the headline pass-count measure was first keyed on captured_at.hour == gate_hour, which is WRONG for a late leg — the gate is evaluated on pass-start ts and econ_prints is leg #10 landing ~40 min later (L222's 380-542s spread), so tape/weather_actuals/dt=2026-07-17's 12:29Z + 13:02Z pair is almost certainly ONE pass, not a leaked gate; the verdict moved to max_passes_per_day, which the drift cannot touch. (b) Un-excused, the check read econ_prints at 137 passes/day and polymarket_cpi_pairs at 101 — both the sanctioned 2026-07-14 CPI burst, i.e. a Ryan-approved trigger doing exactly what it was built to do; reusing BURST_TRIGGER_WINDOWS/BURST_CAPTURE_KEY_TO_TAPE_FAMILY with a ±900s pad drops those to 85 and 2. Every one of these three fixes moves the reported number DOWN — the discipline is that a detector's errors should all point toward under-reporting, so a hit it does report is worth acting on. Test-pinned this build (scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py::single_hour_leg_idempotence, scripts/invariants.py::single_hour_leg_idempotence_warning); kb/00-LOG.md 2026-08-01 entry
L249 2026-08-01 A bootstrap verdict ladder whose ALIVE branch is structurally unreachable is not a test — when an entry gate arithmetically guarantees the sign of the P&L object being bootstrapped, bootstrap_verdict_admissible's L41-inadmissible result on that object carries no evidentiary weight and must not be reported as a verdict. Q49/S68's "spread ≥ two maker fees" entry gate, combined with best_yes_ask ≡ 1 − best_no_bid by collector construction (collection/normalize.py:35), makes every double-fill's gross capture ≥ the two fees BY DEFINITION — net P&L can never be negative, so no bootstrap resample of that object can ever produce an opposing-sign cluster, and admissible=False on it is a property of the gate, not new information about the strategy. The real, non-degenerate kill objects in that probe were the per-observation sub-tick magnitude decomposition and a strategy-level P&L that includes the unhedged single-side legs (which IS genuinely admissible and straddles/goes negative). Test-pinned Q49/S68 verifier pass, 2026-08-01 (scripts/q49_s68_bothside_maker_fillsim.py, findings/2026-08-01-q49-s68-bothside-maker-fillsim-verdict.md)
L250 2026-08-01 The Q27/S19 "turnover" queue-departure fill proxy (departures at ANY price level ≥ our resting price) saturates on multi-day-hold populations, and a HIGH fill rate under it is not itself evidence of a fill — L48's "rules a population OUT, never IN" needs the explicit corollary that a high turnover-rule fill rate proves nothing either way. On Q49's ~65-snapshot, multi-day-hold candidates, the turnover rule read 98% both-fill (cumulative departures in the tens of thousands vs queues of tens to hundreds) purely because the book migrates away from a stale resting price over that many hours — every departure at a level our price no longer competes with is counted as advancing us. A stricter touch rule (departures at our OWN price level, only while a snapshot's best bid is still at or below our price — i.e. we are genuinely at the touch) read 42% on the identical tape. Test-pinned Q49/S68 build, 2026-08-01 (scripts/q49_s68_bothside_maker_fillsim.py::simulate_leg_fill vs simulate_leg_fill_touch, regression-tested in tests/test_q49_s68_bothside_maker_fillsim.py::test_touch_model_is_strictly_tighter_than_turnover_regression)
L251 2026-08-01 A queue-aware fill-sim entry rule of the form "earliest pre-close snapshot, then filter to ttc≤H" silently selects on TAPE START DATE for any family still young, not on genuine near-close timing — the honest at-T-minus-H rule is "first snapshot with ttc≤H." Q49's primary fillable_entry cut (L69's discipline: restrict to a realistic fillable population) used earliest-then-filter and produced 20 candidates whose EVERY entry shared one identical entry_captured_at — the depth tape's first-ever capture pass, which happened to fall within 24h of several games' close. "5 game-series / 14 games" implied temporal breadth a single capture instant does not carry. An independent verifier's alternative "first snapshot with ttc≤24h" rule over the SAME tape produced 176 candidates / 17 series / 131 games spanning the full collection window — the more defensible near-close sample, and (in this instance) still DEAD, so the verdict didn't change, but it could have on a less structurally-doomed candidate. UNENFORCED Q49/S68 verifier pass, 2026-08-01
L252 2026-08-01 A wide-spread maker gate defined as "spread ≥ sum of the round-trip fees" selects, at its tightest and most realistic population, books sitting almost exactly on the zero-profit boundary — the gate captures the fee wall, not an edge, unless it is set at fees-plus-a-target-tick. Q49's entry gate (yes-spread ≥ two flat maker fees) is, on a mirrored binary book, IDENTICAL to "gross capture ≥ two fees" by construction (L249). At the realistic fillable-entry population every one of the 11 double-fills netted between $0.00 and one cent below a tick — the gate's own boundary condition, not a sampled range around it. UNENFORCED Q49/S68 build, 2026-08-01
L253 2026-08-01 A fill proxy that cannot observe trade direction CANNOT exhibit adverse selection — so a non-negative post-fill markout measured off a depth-only tape is the expected output of a blind instrument, never evidence that adverse selection is absent. Q50 instrumented the S68 fill-sim with a post-fill markout (the book's own mid k snapshots after the simulated fill, minus our fill price) and measured it *positive and non-decaying* — H=24/N=1 gives k=0 +0.0543, k=1 +0.0562, k=5 +0.0610, k=10 +0.0618. Read naively that says "no adverse selection", which would be a remarkable claim. It is an artifact of the instrument: tape/orderbook_depth/ carries no trade or volume field (L68/L106), so the touch rule counts a CANCEL at our price identically to a TRADE against us, and a rule blind to the direction of the crossing flow has no channel through which adverse selection could appear. The +5.4c at k=0 is approximately the half-spread of these wide books — a restatement of "we bought at the bid", not a measured informational cost. The companion measure that IS informative is the break-even haircut: how large a per-filled-leg charge flips the verdict. On Q50's ten CI>0 cells it is 0.5c-4.0c per filled leg, i.e. one-to-four times Kalshi's flat 1c maker fee — so the entire verdict is decided by a term the tape cannot measure. Any maker probe on a depth-only tape must publish that break-even and must NOT publish a markout as reassurance. Test-pinned Q50/S68 build, 2026-08-01 (scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::leg_markout docstring + ::adverse_selection_breakeven; findings/2026-08-01-q50-s68-tighter-gate-rederivation.md)
L254 2026-08-01 An apparent maker edge that is MONOTONE INCREASING in the entry horizon and INVERTS to negative in the only genuinely near-close window is a fillability artifact, not an edge — so a maker probe must report its verdict as a function of entry horizon, never at a single H. Q50 swept 7 entry horizons x 5 gate widths (35 cells). All 10 CI>0 cells sit at H>=24h and nine of them at H>=48h; the two genuinely near-close windows are NEGATIVE at the adequately-powered cut (H=6h mean −0.0562, 14 series; H=12h −0.0397, 15 series), and the point estimate rises monotonically with H (6h −0.056 -> 24h +0.070 -> 72h +0.151). **MECHANISM CORRECTED 2026-08-01 by the Q50 verifier — the horizon pattern is REAL, the original attribution was NOT. WITHDRAWN original wording, kept visible because this ledger does not silently rewrite rows: *"This is the S29 DEAD-by-fillability signature reproduced on the same tape family: nominal bids on days-out, effectively one-sided books that a generous fill-sim still 'fills' (L31/L48)."* That sentence is not supported by the data. The pattern survives a matched-ticker control: on the 70 tickers / 14 series present at BOTH H=12 and H=72 the estimate still flips, CI [−0.1137,−0.0054] -> +0.0151, so it is not population turnover. But the ALIVE cell is not a days-out nominal book — median entry ttc at H=24/N=1 is 23.7h, and its queue-ahead/capturable-depth numbers look like an actually-traded book. The real driver is a SPREAD REGIME: median entry spread is $0.020 (2 ticks — exactly the Q49 fee boundary) at the near-close H=6/N=0 and H=12/N=0 cells, versus $0.060-$0.090 (6-9 ticks) at 9 of the 10 ALIVE cells (the tenth, H=72/N=5, is wider still at $0.290) (per-cell median_entry_spread in reports/q50_s68_gate_ladder_summary.json). The wide-spread population this ladder exists to find simply does not exist near close — H=6/N=1 admits only 25 candidates across 4 series, below the L41 10-series floor. So the near-close cells are negative because they are a DIFFERENT, tighter-spread population, not because the same books stop filling; the honest residue at the near-close end is data-adequacy, and "fillability" is a statement about the days-out end only. Practical addendum to the rule: publish the per-cell median entry spread** next to the horizon ladder, because "no near-close sibling" may mean "that population does not exist there," which is a different (and weaker) kill than "it exists and does not fill." Had Q50 reported only its most-populated cell (H=72h, 423 candidates / 17 series, CI [+0.0200,+0.1587]) it would have published a spurious ALIVE on the LEAST fillable population in the sweep — sample size and fillability point in opposite directions here, so "pick the biggest n" is exactly the wrong tiebreak. Test-pinned Q50/S68 build, 2026-08-01 (findings/2026-08-01-q50-s68-tighter-gate-rederivation.md ladder table); mechanism corrected the same day by the Q50 verifier pass (matched-ticker H=12 ∩ H=72 control; per-cell median_entry_spread now emitted by scripts/q50_s68_gate_ladder.py::run)
L255 2026-08-01 A strategy-level bootstrap whose verdict is REPRODUCED BY A ZERO-INFORMATION CONTROL is measuring an assumption, not an edge — any maker fill-sim that books single-side fills against settlement must publish a "mid-as-truth" counterfactual alongside the real verdict. The Q50 verifier replaced the broker_truth settlement payout of every unhedged single-side leg with the book's OWN CONTEMPORANEOUS MID (i.e. assume the market is exactly right, so the leg carries no directional information whatsoever) and re-ran the identical bootstrap: the CI came back the SAME SHAPE AND SIGN as the real verdict. That is decisive — it means the ladder's positive cells are an arithmetic restatement of half-spread x fill-rate − fees on a wide-spread population, which is true by construction of the entry gate, and NOT a claim that the fills pick winners. Any positive result that a zero-information control reproduces has, by definition, nothing in it that came from the outcome data. This is the maker-side sibling of L249 (the double-fill object is sign-bounded by the gate): here the STRATEGY-LEVEL object is not sign-bounded, but its positive part is still gate-arithmetic rather than information. Test-pinned Q50/S68 verifier pass, 2026-08-01 — control constructed by the verifier IN-SESSION over reports/q50_s68_gate_ladder_rows.jsonl + the probe's own book_mid; the control's own output is NOT persisted in any committed artifact (the verifier ran it by hand), so this row cites the verifier pass, not a file (L165)
L256 2026-08-01 A fill-sim that can leave an UNHEDGED SINGLE-SIDE leg must report BOTH exit treatments — hold-to-settlement AND flatten-at-cross — and a verdict that survives only the generous hold-to-settlement treatment is not a verdict. Hold-to-settlement marks the orphan leg to the broker_truth $0/$1 payout, which is the most generous possible treatment: it grants the sim a free directional lottery ticket that no maker running this strategy would knowingly keep. The honest alternative is to FLATTEN AT THE CROSS — close the orphan by buying the other side at its ask — and on a mirrored binary book where the two prices sum to exactly $1 that exit costs precisely both fees, i.e. pnl = −(fee_entry + fee_exit), a small certain loss instead of a fat lottery. The Q50 verifier applied it to all 10 of Q50's ALIVE cells and every one died, CI straddling zero — the entire apparent edge lived in the unhedged legs' settlement lottery. Reporting only the generous branch converts "we were left holding an unwanted position" into income. Test-pinned Q50/S68 verifier pass, 2026-08-01 — flatten treatment applied by the verifier IN-SESSION to reports/q50_s68_gate_ladder_rows.jsonl; the flattened CIs are not persisted in a committed artifact (L165), but the committed side of it is consistent: Q50's own composition analysis shows ~half the H=24/N=1 mean is unhedged single-side legs
L257 2026-08-01 A verifier caveat that names ONE population understates a sampling artifact that spans EVERY population — the artifact belongs beside every labeled cut's n, not only the headline's. Q49/S68's verifier caveat #1 correctly flagged that the PRIMARY fillable_entry cut was a tape-start artifact (all 20 entries on one entry_captured_at). Recomputing the same statistic over all four of that probe's labeled cuts from committed tape shows the contamination was never confined to the primary: the SAME instant (2026-07-07T01:23:57.700581+00:00, the depth tape's first full pass) is the top instant of all four cuts, carrying 20/20 (100%) of fillable_entry, 20/20 (100%) of nearclose_le_24h, 156/284 (54.9%) of spread_le_10c, and 210/445 (47.2%) of unrestricted — so even the widest "445 candidates / 18 series" population is nearly half one snapshot. The reason it read as a single-cut problem is that a caveat is written about the number in the headline, while the entry rule that caused it was shared by every cut. Rule: when a sampling artifact is found in one cut, RE-DERIVE it across every cut the write-up reports before deciding its scope — an entry rule is a property of the probe, not of the cut. Honest counterweight recorded with it: unrestricted's 47.2% sits BELOW the descriptor's blunt 0.5 flag, i.e. the flag is not a rubber stamp and a share alone does not settle whether a cluster is an artifact. L258 2026-08-01 A verdict override that can only ever LOWER a cell is evidentially silent whenever an earlier branch already kills that cell — so the override's INPUT comparison must be published for every cell, not just where the override fires. L255's mid-as-truth control was wired into cell_verdict after L256's worse-of-two exit treatment, and the flatten branch kills all ten of Q50's hold-ALIVE cells before the control is ever consulted: on today's tape the string DEAD-by-zero-information-control appears in ZERO cell verdicts even though the control reproduces the positive CI in 10 of 10 of those cells. A reader (or a future run) checking only the verdict column would conclude the control found nothing. The fix is not to reorder the kill ladder — the flatten kill is the honest one and ordering it first is correct — but to publish the control's own CI beside every cell's hold CI in the summary artifact and the printed report, so the evidence is visible independently of whether it was the proximate cause of death. Generalises beyond this probe: any last-resort gate (tick-magnitude, adverse-selection break-even, a placebo) is measured by what it SAYS, not by how often it is the executioner. The L261 backfill appended 218 non-redundant lines to tape/weather_actuals/ and scripts/tape_gap_monitor.py::single_hour_leg_idempotence(family='weather_actuals', gate_hour_utc=12)'s redundant_line_fraction fell 0.29703 (60/202) -> 0.142857 (60/420) — a pure denominator effect. The numerator (60 byte-redundant lines from the three double-scheduled days 07-16 / 07-21 / 07-31) is UNCHANGED, L221's hour-equality defect is untouched, and the tool says so correctly: verdict stays OVER_CAPTURE across both readings. Same family as L208's survivorship-vs-coverage and L27's magnitude gate, in a new place: any quality ratio published alongside a growing denominator needs its absolute numerator quoted beside it. Corroborating detail from the same run: the 11 backfill passes landed at 09:2xZ and the monitor attributed all 11 as n_passes_off_gate_hour with n_passes_in_gate_hour=0 and per-day redundant_line_fraction=0.0 — i.e. target_day != capture_day - 1 was enough to keep a deliberate backfill from masquerading as the L221 defect, with no new record field and so without opening L222's capture_source write-path question. L271 2026-08-03 A "newest N DAY-FILES per family" scan horizon is RAGGED, not N calendar days — so an aggregate MAX taken over it can report a last-seen date OLDER than the truth, and the derived silence duration can OVER-state as easily as it under-states. After L269's burst exclusion landed, _collector_leg_last_seen's live vps reading became 2026-07-15T19:23:54Z (449.4h) even though the leg's TRUE last capture is 2026-07-22T17:29:49Z (283.3h, visible at max_day=2026-08-01 or at any deeper lookback). Cause: DEAD_LEG_LOOKBACK_DAYS=10 bounds I/O by FILE COUNT per family. Families that write a file most days reach back ~10 days, so their 07-22 file has already fallen out of the window; a sparse family (polymarket_pairs) still has its 07-15 file in-window. The MAX therefore lands on the sparse family's older genuine capture. Note the counter-intuitive monotonicity that makes this easy to miss: a DEEPER lookback returns a NEWER last-seen (max over a superset), so "scan further back and see if it changes" reads backwards. Same family as L263 (a ratio whose denominator moves) and L208 (survivorship vs coverage): a bounded scan's output is a statement about the WINDOW, not about the world, and must say which. L272 2026-08-03 L269's own fix computes alive (the "something is still producing" half of the staggered-death signature) from the SAME burst-excluded last_seen reading it uses for attribution — so excluding a burst-covered family's only recent capture can knock that family OUT of alive, and _dead_collector_leg_diagnosis's if not alive: return None guard then discards an advisory the pre-L269 code would have raised. _dead_collector_leg_diagnosis(exclude_burst_windows=True) (the production default, sole call site scripts/invariants.py:3657) passes ONE last_seen dict — already burst-excluded — into both the alive = [... a < DEAD_LEG_ALIVE_HOURS] computation and the silent/attribution computation. Concretely: if crypto_hourly (burst-covered) was the only leg fresher than DEAD_LEG_ALIVE_HOURS=6h, and that freshness came from a declared burst pass now excluded, alive becomes empty even though a genuine, non-excluded capture from that SAME family may sit just outside the burst window and inside 6h on the un-excluded reading — the tightening applied to fix L269 can therefore also SUPPRESS a dead_leg advisory into total silence (None) rather than only ever lengthening it, contradicting the very "can only get stricter" intent L269 was built to satisfy. An independent verifier re-derivation (2026-08-03, a parallel line of work on this same lesson row) constructed a concrete counterexample against the shipped code: excluding burst windows dropped alive to empty and hid a 106.6h outage that the pre-exclusion reading reported. L273 2026-08-03 _dead_collector_leg_diagnosis reports NOTHING (None) whenever no monitored leg is fresher than DEAD_LEG_ALIVE_HOURS=6h, even when one or more legs ARE silent >= DEAD_LEG_SILENCE_HOURS=24h — so a leg that dies ALONGSIDE a broader slowdown (nothing else is fresh either) is structurally invisible, while the identical leg dying while everything else stays healthy IS reported. The if not alive: return None branch (scripts/invariants.py, immediately after the silent/ambiguous checks) is a deliberate attribution-safety choice — its own comment reads "not the staggered-death signature; stay quiet rather than mis-attribute" — but the practical effect is a blind band: the WORSE the overall pipeline health (more legs stale, none under 6h), the MORE likely this guard fires and suppresses the advisory entirely, which is backwards from what an operator watching a pager would want. This is PRE-EXISTING (present in the code before L269 and unchanged by it) and distinct from L272 (which is about L269's exclusion making this same guard fire in a NEW case, not about the guard's original design gap). L274 2026-08-03 A self-activation gate that counts TAPE-DAYS is not counting the unit its own bootstrap resamples; on Q37's committed tape 20 gate-days buy only 15 bootstrap units (75% yield), and the shortfall does not close by waiting because the deficit's largest component travels with the fire date. scripts/q37_weather_summer_makerno_probe.py opens at >=21 SUMMER contract-days present in tape/weather_books/; bootstrap_cut block-resamples a DIFFERENT unit — a contract-day yielding >=1 FILLED, SETTLEMENT-MEASURABLE trade in the L69 PRIMARY population. The lag window is the load-bearing part: it is always the NEWEST gate-days, so on any fire date the last ~3 gate-days contribute zero and the gate permanently overstates n. Generalizes past Q37: any calendar-day gate in front of a settlement-dependent bootstrap carries the same lag-shaped bias, and the gate number is the wrong thing to quote as sample size. L275 2026-08-03 L32's frozen/movement dual cut is a STRUCTURAL no-op whenever the fill test is 'a later quote touched our resting price' and frozen means 'no quote in the tuple ever changed' — the two cuts are the same number by construction, so printing them side by side manufactures a false impression of corroboration. In q37_weather_summer_makerno_probe.simulate_group, frozen = the (yes_bid, yes_ask, no_bid, no_ask) tuple is constant across the holding window and touched = some later snapshot's best_no_ask <= entry no_bid. On a frozen book every later NO ask EQUALS the entry NO ask, so a touch would require no_ask <= no_bid — a crossed book real Kalshi quotes never show. Hence filled_movement == filled_optimistic identically. Measured on every committed row (2026-08-03): 2,758 rows, touched 674, frozen 379, touched-AND-frozen 0, optimistic 674 == movement 674. This changes no number the probe reports (OPTIMISTIC_FILL=True already blocks graduation) — it changes how the report must be READ: _verdict() calls the movement-conditioned cut 'the honest fill cut', but it carries zero additional information here, so the verdict rests entirely on the optimistic touch model. A real L32 dual cut needs the movement test to be independent of the fill test (e.g. movement in the UNDERLYING, or in a quote the fill does not reference). L276 2026-08-04 A "not built because it would cost too much" note in a lesson row is a PREMISE, not a measurement — and L271's was simply wrong about the data it described, so a real repair sat blocked behind an unchecked sentence. L271 declined to build its own named repair because a calendar-bounded scan risked "unbounded I/O on a family that writes many files per day". No such family exists: tape/ is exactly one dt=<day>.jsonl per family per day, so a calendar window reads at most one file per family per day and the swap measured strictly CHEAPER (59 files / 95.7MB / 0.15s vs 70 files) — the sparse DEAD family the old bound spent I/O on to fetch the WRONG answer drops out entirely. The generalization, and the reason this is worth a row: a deferral reason recorded in prose is inherited by every later reader as if it were a finding, and the idle-run queue is precisely a queue of deferred work, so an unchecked cost premise can park a buildable repair indefinitely. Re-measure the premise before treating a recorded "expensive" as settled — and when the premise survives, the answer is usually to pay the cost CONDITIONALLY rather than not at all (here: the routine scan stays cheap and one deeper scan runs only when a leg is actually missing, 1.12s in the abnormal case and 0s otherwise). Two sibling rows carry the same shape and are NOT closed by this: L270 and L168 both defer their repair half to Ryan on design/lane grounds, which is a DIFFERENT kind of reason — an authority boundary, not a measurable claim — and those stay deferred. L277 2026-08-04 A "maker re-test" of an existing longshot-priced strategy is not automatically a fee experiment — Kalshi's whole-cent fee round-up collapses the maker/taker rate distinction to near-zero at extreme prices, and Q37 was queued on the premise that it wouldn't. core.pricing.fee_per_contract is ceil(rate·p·(1−p)·100)/100 — a per-contract floor of $0.01. Q37 re-tested S1's longshot-NO fade (entry price ≈$0.90) under the maker rate (0.0175) vs the taker rate (0.07) that killed every prior weather angle, on the stated thesis that the 4x-cheaper maker fee was the untested lever. Measured on the live run's 531 filled trades: both rates round up to the identical $0.01 on 443/531 (83.4%) of them; mean fee across all filled trades is maker $0.01000 vs taker $0.01166 — a 0.166¢ difference against a realized 5.67¢ loss. The fee side of the thesis was worth roughly 3% of the gap it needed to close, not the 4x the rate ratio implies. Generalizes: at any price where p(1−p) is small enough that rate·p·(1−p)·100 < 1 for BOTH rates being compared, the round-up floor makes them identical regardless of the rate ratio — this is true for any p ≲0.14 or ≳0.86 at these two rates. Before queuing a maker-vs-taker re-test of a strategy whose entry prices sit near either tail, compute fee_per_contract(p, MAKER) vs fee_per_contract(p, TAKER) directly on the strategy's own typical entry price first — if they're equal, the queued milestone is not testing what its own writeup says it's testing. Protocol findings/2026-08-04-q37-weather-summer-makerno-first-verdict-dead.md; verifier agent re-derivation of scripts/q37_weather_summer_makerno_probe.py's live run, 2026-08-04
L278 2026-08-04 A wall that eight strategies died on can be a MISSING COLLECTOR rather than a missing edge — and this one sat behind a public endpoint our own KB had listed, unbuilt, for seven weeks. It is public, unauthenticated, carries taker_book_side (the exact fill observable), and BACKFILLS to at least 2026-06-20 — before this repo's oldest book tape. The general shape: when the same kill reason recurs across many candidates, audit the DATA-SOURCE inventory for an unbuilt input before concluding the factor is dead; a ~-marked row in the API KB is an untested claim, and re-reading it costs one HTTP GET. The counterpart lesson, learned in the same run: breaking the wall MOVED the constraint rather than removing it — with prints in hand the binding limit became BOOK resolution (median orderbook_depth interval 180.3 min; only 66.0% of prints land inside the preceding snapshot's [bid,ask]), so interval-level fill EXISTENCE is measurable (65.0% interval coverage on traded tickers) while queue position and time-to-fill are not. Do not let "the wall is broken" be quoted as "the fill is now modelled".
