L279 2026-08-04 A venue field whose meaning you INFER from its name is an untested assumption, and if it is load-bearing it must be validated against a second, independent tape BEFORE anything is built on it — the sign of the result can hang on it. Q51 milestone 1 read Kalshi /markets/trades's taker_book_side as "the side of the BOOK the taker crossed into" and wrote that reading into the collector docstring, the finding, and the milestone-2 spec. It is inverted: the field names the side the TAKER'S OWN order sat on, so a bid taker is a BUYER that LIFTS a resting offer and an ask taker is a SELLER that HITS a resting bid. Measured against tape/orderbook_depth/ on prints landing within 15 minutes of their reference snapshot, bid prints execute at or ABOVE the best ask 86.8% (n=151) and ask prints at or BELOW the best bid 83.3% (n=30), and the agreement decays monotonically as the join window widens (86.8% -> 84.6% -> 70.4% at <=15min/<=60min/any age) — the decay is what makes it a measurement rather than an anecdote, because a real relationship degrades under added staleness and an artifact does not. The cost of not checking: the SAME tape, read the milestone-1 way, gives a 27.5% fill rate and a mean of -$0.066; read correctly, 65.0% and +$0.045. The point estimate changes SIGN. Nothing protected against this except joining the field to an independent observable — a second code path would not have caught it (it would have inherited the same misreading), which is the precise limit of the redundancy-check fallback used when no verifier is dispatchable. Corollary for collectors: storing a field VERBATIM (as collection/kalshi_trades.py does) confines the blast radius to interpretation and costs zero re-capture; a collector that had DERIVED a fills_a_resting_bid boolean from the misreading would have poisoned the tape itself. Test-pinned Q51 milestone 2, 2026-08-04 (scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py; findings/2026-08-04-q51-maker-fillsim-milestone2.md; correction appended to findings/2026-08-04-q51-trade-print-surface-wall-b-half-broken.md)
L280 2026-08-04 Coverage measured from one side of a join is not coverage. Measure it from BOTH sides and report the smaller number — the gap between them is where the strategy dies. Q51 milestone 1 asked the book-side question ("of the orderbook_depth intervals I hold, how many contain an executed print?") and got 65.0%, which reads as a healthy join. The dual, print-side question ("of the executed prints I hold, how many sit inside a book interval I can price against?") gives 10.1% on the same two tapes (17.2% if adjacent days are admitted), with 82.7% of prints landing AFTER the last snapshot their ticker ever received and only 0.46% carrying a reference quote younger than 15 minutes (median quote age at execution 149.7 min). Both numbers are arithmetically correct; they are not the same measurement, and the usable fraction of the tape is the smaller one. The corollary matters more than the number: a print-side coverage hole is a BOOK-side defect — orderbook_depth ran 10 passes over 2026-08-02..08-04 with a 1,080-minute (18 h) gap starting 08-03T15:56Z, so the highest-volume markets (one NWSL market: 10,156 prints, 6 snapshots, 10,120 prints after the last one) trade through a book blackout. Fixing the trade collector could not have helped. A second corollary: this bounds the evidence behind L279 — its n=151/n=30 orientation slice IS that 0.46%. Also surfaced by the same audit and worth carrying: event_ticker is structurally null on every line of this family (the /markets/trades payload has no such field), so the block-bootstrap unit (L6) is DERIVED from the ticker string and had never been checked — game_of() validated 60/60 against the venue's own event_ticker; and raw_sha256 is a per-QUERY digest (42 distinct across 39,698 lines), not a per-line content hash. Test-pinned IDLE RUN policy (c), 2026-08-04 (scripts/q51_trade_tape_quality.py; reports/q51_trade_tape_quality.json; findings/2026-08-04-q51-trade-tape-quality-deepdive.md)
L281 2026-08-04 Corrects L84. "Per-(entity, day) dedup implemented by reading the day's already-written tape" is concurrency-safe ONLY within one working tree — it does not extend across the unmerged branches this repo's own collectors routinely race on. collection/weather_books.py::_existing_meta_series reads a series list back from tape/weather_books/meta/dt=<day>.jsonl to skip a series already recorded that day; two collector passes each running on their own unmerged tape/hourly-* branch (LOOP-QUEUE.md step 0b) each see an empty or partial file, so BOTH write. Measured on committed tape: dt=2026-07-27.jsonl carries 47 of its 48 (series, group) keys twice — every other of the 19 committed day-files is exactly 1:1. Traced by git log -S to a single commit (a7ee98b, an unrelated idle-run) that union-appended two branch-local files whose rows differ in capture_id/captured_at, so the step-0b sweep's line-level containment check correctly could not (and structurally cannot) collapse them — a duplicate LOGICAL key made of two DISTINCT lines is invisible to any dedup keyed on the line itself. Worse than a harmless repeat: 5 of the 47 duplicate pairs differ in CONTENT (rules_primary/sample_ticker), because the two racing passes sampled different hourly-directional contract instances as their first_market — a consumer reading meta by series on that day gets a value that depends on read order, not a stable fact. This is L170's exact predicted failure class (hyperliquid_funding's branch-local dedup, 2026-07-26), now confirmed in a second family — "worth a grep-check the next time one is audited" (L170) was correct. General rule: any "write once per (entity, period)" claim built on a read-back-and-skip pattern is a WORKING-TREE-LOCAL guarantee, not a repo-wide one, in a pipeline with two staggered collectors and a branch-fallback push path; auditing it means checking committed tape for the logical-key duplicate, not re-reading the source for a synchronization bug that isn't there. L282 2026-08-04 Corrects L84 a third time — the step-0b union-append race is a repo-wide structural risk, not a per-family bug. collection/orderbook_depth.py::run has no read-back dedup at all (it simply appends every captured ticker on every pass), yet it shows the identical defect L281 diagnosed for a family that DOES dedup: dt=2026-07-28.jsonl carries 1,093 byte-identical duplicate (capture_id, ticker) rows — a whole 1,093-ticker pass landed on main twice. Traced via git log --numstat: commit 8130bff ("hourly pass 20260728T065635Z (continued)", 07:06:58Z) introduced the pass fresh (its parent has zero occurrences), and commit c4ed31a ("recover 1,691 stranded lines (hourly-20260728T1004Z)", #223, 13:07:07Z) independently reintroduced the SAME 1,093 rows from a branch that had forked before 8130bff landed — the stranded-branch-sweep's own line-level containment check correctly (by its own contract) saw 1,093 "new" lines that were in fact already on main under a different commit. This is the third family (after hyperliquid_funding/L170 and weather_books-meta/L281) to show the exact same failure from the exact same mechanism — with and without a collector-side dedup guard — which promotes the pattern from "a bug in this collector's dedup" to "a structural property of the step-0b stranded-branch-sweep workflow itself": ANY two commits that each independently believe they are the sole writer of a day-file, and that both get merged, can duplicate a whole pass, regardless of what the collector does. Every duplicate here is byte-identical (unlike L281's 5 content-differing pairs), because this is the SAME capture landing twice, not two DIFFERENT passes racing. One consumer, scripts/s6_maker_firstcut.py::_dedup_by_capture, already defends against exactly this (a capture_id-dedup step whose own docstring names the risk) — evidence the pattern was already suspected before this audit, just not yet confirmed on this family or enforced repo-wide. L283 2026-08-05 A tape family can be enormous, perfectly tagged, zero-defect — and still incapable of answering any question, because breadth without REVISITS is not a panel. tape/universe_sweep/ is the repo's largest family (652 MB, 17 days, full-universe top-of-book *with* yes_bid_size/yes_ask_size, 100% real_ask-tagged, 0 parse errors) and was the obvious candidate to close L280's 10.1% print-side anchor hole. It closes none of it: 0 of 42 print tickers and 0 of 39,698 prints, under every criterion, on the print day and both neighbours. The cause is structural — the collector's pager is cap-bound (MAX_CALLS=20 x PAGE_LIMIT=1000) and the truncated slice ROTATES between passes, so on dt=2026-08-03 two 20,000-row captures produced 40,000 *distinct* tickers with zero overlap. Family-wide: 760,000 lines / 723,235 distinct tickers / 686,470 (94.92%) observed exactly ONCE ever / max 2 observations of any ticker / 38 of 38 captures at the cap. A family whose modal market is seen once can never yield a price change, an interval, or an anchor-then-print join, so 94.92% of the largest tape in the repo is analytically inert for any time-series question. This is a distinct fact from the same cap's already-recorded OPERATIONAL consequence (findings/2026-08-03-universe-sweep-completeness-cap-saturation.md measured a permanently-saturated completeness_ok wired into a high-priority pager); nobody had asked what the cap does to the DATA. General rule: before proposing a family as a join anchor, measure its observations-per-entity histogram, not its size — frac_entities_observed_once is the one number that decides whether it is a panel or a census, and a rotating cap-bound sweep is a census no matter how many bytes it holds. Second half, learned in the same audit: a coverage fraction is only as meaningful as its CRITERION, and the two must be quoted together. Relaxing L280's BRACKET rule (>=2 snapshots, print inside [first,last]) to the rule a resting-maker fill-sim actually needs (>=1 PRIOR quote) moves print coverage 10.1% -> 99.85% — a 10x headline swing that changes nothing real, because only 1.99% of prints carry an anchor younger than 15 minutes under the RELAXED rule (vs L280's 0.46% under the strict one; median anchor age 128.6 min, p90 365.8 min). A relaxed criterion buys coverage, not freshness, and must never be quoted without the freshness ladder beside it. Constructive corollary: adequacy is decided by resample UNITS, not fractions — the 15-min-anchored population is 791 prints across 21 GAME units (L6), 2.1x the L41 MIN_UNITS=10 floor, and same-day / ±1 day / union give the IDENTICAL 791/21, proving the bound is CADENCE and not coverage, breadth or history. Test-pinned IDLE RUN policy (c), 2026-08-05 (research loop, main-context build); scripts/q51_book_anchor_audit.py; reports/q51_book_anchor_audit.json; findings/2026-08-05-q51-book-anchor-cadence-not-breadth.md; supersedes nothing, extends L280
L284 2026-08-05 An acceptance test pinned to a file that one of the project's OWN scheduled commands overwrites is not a pin — L191's "pin to a slice that cannot grow" must be checked against the repo's planned commands, not only against tape append-only-ness. tests/test_q51_maker_fillsim.py's three HARD milestone-2 acceptance cases cite L191 and read tape/q51_settlement_cache/settlement.json through q51_maker_fillsim.run()'s DEFAULT cache_path. The two TAPE day-files they also read genuinely cannot shrink; the settlement cache was never frozen at all — scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py --build-cache, the single command LOOP-QUEUE.md's TIME-GATED Q51 milestone 3 runs on 2026-08-10, rewrites it in place. Measured offline five days early against a synthetic post-re-pull cache (population counts only; the invented results are tagged synthetic and no outcome-dependent number was computed from them): all five pinned quantities move — n_units_games 7→44, n_intervals 20→128, n_covered_intervals 17→62, drops["unsettled"] 145→37, n_fills 26→76 — so the milestone would have turned the pytest -q gate RED on the exact day it fired, with the input that produced the milestone-2 numbers already overwritten and no committed copy left. Two generalizations worth carrying. (1) The blast radius of a mutable pinned input is worst at the moment the pin matters most: the breakage lands together with the new evidence, when the old evidence has just been destroyed. (2) A file-SHAPE readiness check cannot see this — the 2026-08-05 edge-hunter run verified milestone 3 "execute-ready by FILE SHAPE (L25): script present, tests collect 41 tests", which was true and did not imply the tests would still pass after the milestone's own command ran. Readiness for a gated item means *the item's own command sequence leaves the gate green*, not *the files exist*. The general rule this replaces L282's with: the union-append convention (LOOP-QUEUE step 0b) is correct for a STRANDED BRANCH, whose content is disjoint from main by construction, and WRONG as a git pull --rebase conflict resolution, where the local side's overlap with upstream has by definition already been merged — the 13:09Z tree is exactly main's file ++ a stale local copy of the morning pass ++ the new pass, in that order, in all six families. A plain rebase REPLAY is ruled out (it would have produced its own commit; the graph has none). Which runner resolved it cannot be proven from the object graph — the collector's local state is gone — and is deliberately NOT guessed at (the L221/L222 attribution discipline). Live-risk read: the specific route is plausibly closed (cloud runs now push to tape/hourly-* branches, not main), but multi-commit carried-forward passes still reach main (… [continued]/… [final], 2026-08-02) and ops/vps/kalshi-headless-hourly.sh still opens every pass with git pull --rebase -q origin main over a tree that may hold an unpushed tape commit — 8 clean days is evidence of rarity, not of repair. Consumer impact is real but bounded: a re-appended capture inflates every per-line statistic off that file (density, n_prints, pass ratios, bootstrap population size) and can duplicate a bootstrap unit; capture_id-dedup at read time (scripts/s6_maker_firstcut.py::_dedup_by_capture) still defends correctly. L287 2026-08-05 A real_ask tag certifies PROVENANCE, not FILLABILITY — and on a Kalshi threshold ladder the naive coherence screen you would reach for is ~1,700x more optimistic than the executable one. Kalshi mirrors a NO order into the SAME book as a YES order, so the four persisted BBO fields carry only two degrees of freedom: over all committed tape/econ_prints/ (25 files, 2,290 records, 126,841 strikes, --max-day 2026-08-04) yes_ask + no_bid == 1 holds 126,841/126,841 and no_ask + yes_bid == 1 holds 126,841/126,841, zero violations either way. So no_ask/no_bid are derived, and a strike with no resting YES bid reports no_ask == $1.00 while a strike with no resting NO bid reports yes_ask == $1.00 — a quote pinned by the ABSENCE of a bid, not by belief. 44.36% (56,268/126,841) of the family's strikes are one-sided (41.06% no YES bid, 3.07% no NO bid, 0.23% nothing resting); only 32.86% (41,684) are two-sided AND inside a 5c spread (median spread $0.06 but mean $0.3179, p90 $0.98 — L31's shape). All 126,841 carry the same tag, real_ask, and the tape persists NO field separating the two cohorts (the discriminator yes_bid > 0 AND yes_ask < 1 is derivable, but only by a consumer who already knows to derive it — L286's trap one family over). The consequence that matters for a probe: the naive screen (yes_ask must be non-increasing in floor_strike, ADJACENT rungs, the most conservative form) fires on 15,234/116,632 = 13.06% of adjacent pairs and 6,152/10,209 = 60.26% of ladder snapshots, and 80.96% of those hits touch a one-sided rung (two-sided-only rate falls to 5.13%); the EXECUTABLE screen (buy YES(outer)+NO(inner) at both real asks for a guaranteed >=$1, priced through core.pricing.monotonicity_crossing_edge, ALL 849,958 nested pairs — the most generous form) fires 0 times net of taker fees. Only 9 pairs cost under $1.00 gross, and those 9 are just 2 distinct quote states (L221's byte-redundant re-capture inflating one 2026-07-29 state 8x); the best is KXCPIYOY-26SEP-T3.5 YES @ $0.50 + -T3.6 NO @ $0.48 (real_ask) = $0.98 gross, -$0.02 net. A yes_ask ordering violation is NECESSARY but not SUFFICIENT; treat it as evidence of nothing. Side-notes from the same pass, both small: the day file is append-ordered, not time-ordered (3 within-file captured_at inversions, incl. a 5.3h backward jump on dt=2026-07-05 line 16) so a line-by-line replay gets an out-of-order feed; and 3 gdp records carry nowcast.status: not_built, a value today's fetch_nowcast('gdp') cannot produce — a PAYLOAD-SEMANTICS change invisible in schema_version (all 2,290 lines say econ_prints.v1). L289 2026-08-06 A previous_settlement-style status field with a "not yet resolved" branch that omits the payload key entirely (no results, no expiration_value) is easy to mis-audit by checking the VALUE ("is results empty/zero-winner?") instead of the STATUS ("did this record ever claim to be settled?") — the two questions look identical until you check the source. Auditing tape/crypto_hourly/'s settlement completeness, a first pass computed n_yes = sum(v == "yes" for v in ps.get("results", {}).values()) over every previous_settlement record and flagged 116/1,597 (7.3%) as suspicious zero-winner broker_truth payloads — a false alarm, self-caught before publication by reading collection/crypto_hourly.py::fetch_settlement/run(): pending (settlement not fully posted), no_current_group (no ticker to derive a previous hour from) and not_found all correctly omit results/price_source_tag altogether, so .get("results", {}) silently returns {} for all three and a bare n_yes == 0 check cannot tell "0 winners because nothing is settled yet" from "0 winners because the ladder is corrupted." Re-run keyed on the record's own status field instead: 1,483 settled records, 0 exactly-one-winner violations (100% clean, checked via core.settlement.filter_binary_results_map per L52/L155 rather than a bare =="yes" comparison — the audit script itself tripped that advisory on first draft and was fixed the same run), 34 pending / 76 no_current_group / 6 not_found, none of them a data defect. The general rule: any MECE/exhaustive-ladder settlement audit must branch on the collector's own completion signal before touching the payload shape — an absent key and an empty-but-present key are different claims, and only the source code (never the JSON shape alone) tells you which one you're looking at. L291 2026-08-06 A shared event_ticker does NOT prove nested strikes: Kalshi packs MULTIPLE SUBJECTS into one event, and the repo's monotonicity check sorts them by strike as if they were rungs of one ladder — so 100% of what survives the fillability guard is a naked directional bet, not an arb. Replaying all 43,038 committed cross_strike_monotonicity anomalies (dt <= 2026-08-04, 26 capture-days) through the L290 guards leaves 13 records / 6 distinct ticker pairs, and every one pairs two DIFFERENT subjects under one event: two tennis players' game spreads (KXATPGSPREAD-26JUL17COLVAC-VAC2 vs -COL2), two batters' props (KXMLBHIT/HRR/TB-...-KCVPASQUANTINO9-* vs -KCBWITT7-*), three cities' rain markets (KXRAIN-26JUL23-NYC vs -NOLA/-DEN). Corroborated on a second surface: tape/universe_sweep/ titles show event KXATPGSPREAD-26JUL22STRNAV holding both *'Will Navone win at least 2.5 more games than Struff?'* and *'Will Struff win at least 1.5 more games than Navone?'* — opposite-direction claims, both greater-typed. Buying YES(subject A) + NO(subject B) pays a guaranteed >= $1 only under nesting, which is exactly what is absent. Net effect on the repo's oldest live scanner: 43,038 recorded 'anomalies' -> 43,025 unbuyable legs -> 13 cross-subject false positives -> ZERO verified fillable arbs in 26 days. S3/S15 were NOT flipped (a kill is verdict-class; no independent verifier was available in-session) — the rows carry a dated descriptive note and the repair is queued as Q53. L295 2026-08-06 A Kalshi strike LABEL is not the strike VALUE — it is the strike plus a per-family constant offset — so the subject-identity test that repairs L291's nesting premise must compare the market's own text POSITIONALLY against its own strike under a SHARED offset, not against strike equality and not under a global numeric mask. Q53's repair (core/subject_identity.py) proves nesting from the market's own title/subtitle/yes_sub_title, anchored to its own floor_strike/cap_strike; it parses NO ticker suffix (Q1's build note, the KXFEDDECISION '>25bps as 26' trap). Two conditions, both required: the text SKELETON (alphabetic content, numbers removed) must match — which is what actually discriminates, since two subjects differ in WORDS ('Will Navone win ... than Struff?' vs the reverse; 'Wilyer Abreu: 4+ hits...' vs 'Tsung-Che Cheng: 5+ hits...') and no numeric rule can repair an alphabetic difference — AND every differing numeric token must be explainable by a shared offset d with a_token == a_strike + d, b_token == b_strike + d. The offset is REAL and non-zero on committed tape: KXHIGHNY-26JUL16-T96 publishes 97° or above (+1), KXTEMPNYCH-26JUL1522-T81.99 publishes 82° or above (+0.01), KXCPICORE publishes 0.3 for floor_strike 0.3 (0) — so a naive label==strike rule refuses EVERY weather ladder on tape. Positional (not global-mask) comparison is likewise measured, not stylistic: a global 'blank every number equal to the strike' normalizer would blank the 2 in Q2 2026 for a KXGDP rung with floor_strike 2.0 while its sibling rung (0.3) kept it, inventing a false refusal. Both error rates, closed window dt <= 2026-08-04, exhaustive (0 classes sampled), via python scripts/subject_identity_corpus_audit.py --max-day 2026-08-04 (run 2026-08-06, reports/subject_identity_corpus_audit.json): FALSE-REFUSE 0 / 34,334 genuine-ladder monotonicity-shaped pairs (econ_prints 2,348/0; weather_books 31,986/0; crypto_hourly contributes 0 pairs BY CONSTRUCTION — one greater + one less per event, rest between — reported rather than hidden in a total); FALSE-ADMIT 0 / 2,364 pairs a ground-truth player-name regex (independent of the predicate) labels cross-subject, with 0 / 151 false refusals of the genuine within-player ladders sitting in the SAME events (KXMLBHRR-26JUL171335TBBOSG1 holds Abreu's 1+/2+/3+/4+ rungs beside Cheng's; KXATPGSPREAD-26JUL22STRNAV holds both match directions beside Navone's own 2.5/5.5 ladder). Platform-wide over 1,190,426 within-event tape/universe_sweep/ pairs: 932,965 (78.37%) decided-refuse, 15 decided-admit — all 15 verified CORRECT (KXATPGTOTAL total-games rungs of one match) — and 257,446 (21.63%) INDETERMINATE because that family persists no strike fields, a corpus limit stated rather than assumed away (its 24,094 skeletons are dominated by KXMVE* parlays, which carry no strike_type and so never reach the check, and by KXGOLDH/KXSILVERH/KXWTIH genuine ladders). The design change the measurement forced: scoring a whole MECE ladder on the sub-title refuses 880 of 1,591 genuine single-city weather ladders (55.3%), because one city's ladder legitimately carries three sub-title GRAMMARS (88° or below / 95° to 96° / 97° or above) — a sub-title describes the RUNG, only the title describes the SUBJECT — hence two documented field sets (DESCRIPTIVE_FIELDS for same-strike_type pairs, SUBJECT_FIELDS_CROSS_STRIKE_TYPE for the cross-type bracket shape). L297 2026-08-06 git log -S archaeology is silently WRONG in a shallow clone: it reports the clone's truncation boundary as the date a symbol was introduced, and the answer looks completely ordinary. While building Q53's milestone-3 S15 verdict, git log --format='%ad %h %s' --date=short -S'check_cross_event_implication' -- scripts/anomaly_sweep.py returned exactly one commit, 4330d8e (2026-08-02), and git log --diff-filter=A -- config/implication_pairs.yaml agreed — which read as a clean, citable finding ('the check shipped 14 days after its only data source expired, so its zero is chronological'). It was an artifact: git log --oneline | wc -l -> 50, earliest commit date 2026-08-02, and .git/shallow exists — the working copy is a shallow clone, so 4330d8e is simply the oldest commit PRESENT, and scripts/anomaly_sweep.py appears as a 425-line whole-file 'addition' there because that is the graft point, not because it was written then. The tell that caught it BEFORE publication was a cross-source contradiction, not a git check: kb/strategies/00-index.md's S15 row cites a 2026-07-05 live validation, which cannot coexist with a 2026-08-02 introduction. Rule: any claim about WHEN something entered this repo must first establish that the history is deep enough to support it (test -e .git/shallow / git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository), and when it is not, the claim must be re-derived from committed TAPE and config text — which are complete — or dropped. The S15 finding was rewritten to use only tape-derived facts and the retraction is recorded in the finding itself rather than quietly deleted. Measured 2026-08-06 in the Q53 run's working copy. L298 2026-08-06 Supersedes L296's reading-rule half — the guard is BUILT — and the replay that built it found the SAME defect one check over, in S3's own scanners, where nobody had looked. L296 recorded that scripts/anomaly_sweep.py's check 3 reported n_implication_pairs_checked: 0 on 243/243 committed passes (0 pairs CHECKED, not 0 hits over N checks) and left the rule UNENFORCED. Building the enforcement — core/detector_evidence.py, one shared site — required deciding what a hit count MEANS without its denominator, and the answer is four-valued, not three: hits / informative_zero (the only zero readable as absence) / empty_denominator / incoherent (hits over an empty denominator). incoherent is a returned VALUE, not a raise, and the choice is load-bearing in both directions: raising inside a live collector would destroy a pass's tape over a bookkeeping contradiction (L86), and raising in replay would make an audit unable to COUNT the contradictions, which is the one number that matters when you find one. Malformed INPUT (negative/non-integer counts) does raise — a caller bug is a different class from a record's claim. What the replay found, closed window --max-day 2026-08-04, 248 passes / 26 capture-days / 0 malformed lines, via python3 scripts/anomaly_detector_evidence_audit.py: L296's 243 reproduces exactly on an independent code path (243 empty_denominator + 5 counter_absent = 248, the 5 predating the counter reported as their own class per L289, never folded into the zero), and check 3 has 0 passes anywhere in committed history whose zero is readable. The new fact: S3's own two checks are not immune. bracket_arb and cross_strike_monotonicity each show 23 of 248 (9.3%) empty_denominator passes — and all 23 report completeness_ok: true, fetch_error: null and a non-zero n_markets_scanned up to the full 20,000-market cap. A pass that scanned 20,000 markets, evaluated ZERO candidate groups, and looks clean is exactly L296's shape in the check whose zero S3's registry row rests on; 10 of the 23 fall on dt=2026-07-18. Both checks' readable passes are thin too: 2,210 group-checks over 26 days is ~8.9 per pass out of ~20,000 markets scanned, bracket_arb 0 hits over all 2,210, cross_strike_monotonicity 43,038 hits on 137 passes. Side-observation pinned rather than explained away: n_bracket_groups_checked == n_monotonicity_groups_checked on 248/248 passes, which the code does NOT guarantee (an event of three between rungs increments the first and not the second). Does NOT make S3/S15 killable and does not fix check 3's zero: the coverage denominator stays truncation-bound (247/248 markets_truncated, Q55's scanned_tickers_sha256 only accumulates forward), and Q55's new live KXMARMADROUND family is still beyond the 20,000-market cursor, so the expected reading of the next passes is STILL empty_denominator — now labelled as such instead of passing as a clean zero. L299 2026-08-07 An enforcement cell that declares itself unmechanizable is a claim, not a fact — and it is the one claim in the ledger nothing re-checks, because the stale-marker detector only looks at rows that NAME an artifact. L302 2026-08-07 A "no recurrence since X" claim computed over committed tape is conditioned on the pass having successfully PUSHED — and a pass that fails is not independent of a pass that fails to push. The 2026-08-06 crypto_hourly data-quality audit concluded its no_hourly_group_found discovery-gap episode was "a closed July episode, not a live risk" (76 gaps, last day 2026-07-30, "zero August recurrence"). This run's step-0b sweep recovered tape/hourly-20260806T0657Z, whose capture 20260806T065616Z carries current.status == "no_hourly_group_found" on BOTH BTC and ETH with pass_complete: false — an August recurrence whose own tape had been stranded on a push-fallback branch, invisible to the audit. Post-recovery the profile reads 78 gaps / 74 no_hourly_group_found / last_gap_day 2026-08-06. The audit's arithmetic was right for the tape that existed; the INFERENCE was availability-biased, and the bias is not random — a collector host sick enough to fail discovery is the same host likely to fail its push, so the missing observations are exactly the ones that would have refuted the claim. Any "closed episode" / "no recurrence" statement must either follow a step-0b sweep that confirms no stranded branch carries the family, or state the conditioning explicitly. Sibling of L155 (a low count is precision, not recall) and L296 (a zero whose denominator was never checked), applied to the TIME axis. L303 2026-08-07 A capped examples list in a JSON report is a sample, not the population — and "every one is X" is a claim about the population, generalized from the sample without re-checking. Q53 milestone 3's finding (findings/2026-08-06-q53-subject-identity-nesting-repair.md) reported "all 15 platform-wide decided-ADMIT pairs are correct, every one is KXATPGTOTAL" — true on correctness, wrong on family, because reports/subject_identity_corpus_audit.json stores at most 5 examples per reason and all 5 stored happened to be KXATPGTOTAL. Independent enumeration of the actual 15 (not the 5-example cap) found 9 KXATPGTOTAL + 6 KXWNBATOTAL-26JUL22LVWSH (a WNBA total-points ladder). Two sibling defects surfaced by the same independent-verifier pass, same root shape — an incomplete SOURCE read as a complete one (L165's class): the finding's "48 distinct KXWCROUND tickers (07-06..07-14)" doesn't reproduce (the union over that exact window is 36; 48 needs 07-04..07-15) though the qualitative point survives either count; and "the 38-pairs/40-markets validation is not reproducible from committed tape" was flatly false — tape/polymarket_pairs/dt=2026-07-05.jsonl alone reproduces it exactly (40 tickers -> 38 pairs via the family's own regex+rank map), the true and narrower claim being "not reproducible from tape/anomalies/" specifically. That last correction is not neutral: it upgrades the S15 DATA-INADEQUATE verdict from "most likely a truncation artifact" to positively corroborated (the pairs WERE generatable from tape the sweep ran against that exact day, and it still recorded zero checked). General rule: before writing "every one" / "not reproducible" / "all N", re-derive from the corpus itself, not from a report's stored sample of it — and name WHICH corpus a negative reproducibility claim was tested against, since a detector's own write-path tape (tape/anomalies/) and the raw source tape it reads (tape/polymarket_pairs/) are different corpora with different answers. L304 2026-08-07 "The collector is only 3-hourly" is a hypothesis, not an explanation — and on tape/sports_pairs/ it is falsified by 4.09x: 28.55% of games leave the tape BEFORE their own kickoff while passes are still running. Availability-corrected over 1,636 v2 games whose kickoff falls inside the observation window (637 of 2,273 excluded because their kickoff is after the tape's last pass — that correction alone moves the median terminal gap 216.1 → 155.7 min and the p90 4,745 → 400 min, the L302 class), only 163/1,636 = 9.96% have ANY observation within 60 min of kickoff and 52 = 3.18% within 30 min. The falsifiable null — if a game were captured on every pass until kickoff its terminal gap would be ~U(0, C) for C the local cadence — predicts 0.5190 and observes 0.1268 (shortfall 4.09x, REJECTED), even though the null's shape is exactly right where it applies (mean gap/C = 0.5001 vs the U(0,1) reference 0.500 among the 48.6% of games inside one cadence interval; full-sample median gap/C = 1.029). The non-inferential confirmation names the passes instead of arguing from a distribution: 467/1,636 = 28.55% of games had ≥1 pass that captured their OWN series run strictly between their last observation and kickoff without them (median 1 missed pass, max 14; drop-lead LOWER bound p50 15.9 min, p90 53.0 min). L305 2026-08-07 sports_pairs.game_date is parsed from the TICKER and is NOT the UTC kickoff date — it disagrees on 38,804 of 86,520 v2 records (44.85%), always by exactly +1 day, always for a kickoff in UTC hours 00-06. game_date comes from parse_sports_ticker (a US-local/evening calendar date); game_start is Kalshi's UTC occurrence_datetime, added only in sports_pairs.v2 (first record 2026-07-12T21:23:03Z, so 41.16% of the family — 60,516 v1 records — has no game_start at all and any kickoff-relative question is structurally unanswerable there, L296's denominator rule). The offset distribution has exactly two values, 0 (47,716) and +1 (38,804), and the disagreeing hour profile peaks at 02Z (10,880) — i.e. it is a timezone convention, not corruption, which is precisely why it is dangerous: a probe that buckets or joins on game_date as if it were the UTC kickoff day silently mis-dates nearly half the largest tape family by a full day, and any join to a UTC-keyed settlement surface (core/settlement_sources.py, L300) inherits that error without a parse failure to warn it. L308 2026-08-08 A fill rate measured on the all_intervals branch is a property of the SETTLED SUBSET, not of the mechanism — and the two branches' fill rates diverge by 2.27x as the population grows. Q51 milestone 2 published a 65.0% fill rate at n=7 game units. Its settled population on 2026-08-04 was the games PLAYED on 2026-08-03, which are exactly the markets that TRADED on 2026-08-03, so the headline branch measured itself on the most active slice the tape has. Projected outcome-independently over the SAME committed tape to milestone 3's 2026-08-10 population (a fill is decided by BOOK + PRINTS alone; settlement decides only which legs are scored and whether one WON, so every count is fixed today): the headline all_intervals fill rate falls 0.6739 -> 0.2969 (2.27x) while the conditional-on-coverage rate falls only 0.7750 -> 0.6129 (1.26x) — the collapse is a COVERAGE effect (0.870 -> 0.484), not a change in fill behaviour. Because an unfilled leg contributes an exact 0.0, mean_over_all_legs == (n_fills/n_legs) * mean_over_FILLED_legs is an identity, so 180 of 256 legs (70.3%) contribute exactly zero by construction and any per-fill edge is compressed 2.27x BEFORE the L27 one-tick gate is applied to it. The general rule: an unconditional fill rate is not comparable across populations whose coverage differs, and a headline that lands at or below the tick gate after such a compression is not, by itself, evidence about the mechanism — the same arithmetic produces it from an unchanged per-fill edge. Report the conditional rate and the multiplier beside the headline, and never compare two unconditional fill rates measured on differently-covered populations. Corollary measured on the same tape: 76 of the 79 fills the committed tape will ever supply are already in the 08-10 population — waiting to 08-23 adds 74 legs and 3 fills, so a later sweep buys resample units, not fill evidence. L311 2026-08-08 A probe written BEFORE its data gate opens must be structurally unable to compute the answer, or it has already spent the pre-registration it exists to protect. Q54/S79's binding test mandates "pre-register horizon+entry BEFORE returns", and the committed tape can already produce the 8-game answer — so a probe that computed it and then printed INSUFFICIENT DATA would leave whoever later picks the lookback/threshold/band picking them having seen the returns (L41's luckiest-cell failure with extra steps). Cosmetic refusal is not refusal. Four structural properties make it real in scripts/q54_s79_flow_continuation_probe.py: (1) the adequacy function never receives a settlement result — membership comes from settled_ticker_set(), which collapses each result through core.settlement.is_binary_result to the label CLASS, so a caller learns THAT a market settled and never HOW; (2) outcome_map() and score_rows() — the only functions that read an outcome value or compute a return — are unreachable from run() while the gate is shut; (3) the emitted sealed report is walked KEY-by-key for outcome-derived fields (key-level, not a substring scan of the blob: a blob scan flags the gate note's own prose and a guard that over-reports gets disabled, L155); (4) the spec dict is hashed into PREREG_SHA256 and pinned, so tuning-after-seeing-data cannot be a quiet diff. Corollary that makes this cheap rather than pious: a discovery made while the seal holds is outcome-BLIND, so acting on it is design, not tuning — which is how L312 below became a gate on the same day instead of a note. Test-pinned scripts/q54_s79_flow_continuation_probe.py; findings/2026-08-08-q54-s79-preregistered-probe-and-the-sign-variation-hole.md
L312 2026-08-08 A signal whose sign is CONSTANT on the scoreable population is not a conditioning variable — it is a direction, and a CI computed on it is evidence about that direction instead. Q54/S79 ("signed aggressor flow predicts continuation; a taker follows it") enumerates 82 pre-registered entries on the one committed trade day; 67 land on a settled market and all 67 are on the same side, because the 5 NO-side candidates the tape produces belong to two games that have not settled. A verdict computed there would be a verdict about buying YES on sports moneylines, not about signed flow. It is structural, not a one-day accident: L279 measured 31,831 buy prints against 7,867 sell (80/20) on this same tape — retail overwhelmingly BUYS — so a net-flow signal aggregated over 30 minutes is positive nearly always, and more days do not remove that. Q54's Status line named two gate conditions (settled units < 10; no fillable seconds-to-minutes exit book); this is a third, and the one a longer tape is least likely to clear. Two sub-lessons rode along: (a) the gate distance was quoted in the wrong denominator — 9 settled games is not 9 bootstrap units, because a settled game becomes a unit only if it also yields an entry (one of the nine has a print span too short to produce one), so the distance is 2 units, not 1 (same class as the 2026-08-03 Q37 "gate counts the wrong unit" finding and L289/L296); (b) the minority-side floor is 2 units, not 1, because a minority arm concentrated in a single game cannot be block-bootstrapped at all — L41's own logic applied to the SIGNAL rather than to the outcome. L314 2026-08-08 A pagination cap set below a real ticker-day's depth writes a time-ordered PREFIX into tape that carries no partial-coverage marker — for a flow strategy that is worse than missing data. The phase-1 trade-print backfill ran at max_calls=20 (page limit 1000); KXMLBGAME-26JUL061915NYMATL's two outcome tickers each hit the cap on 2026-07-07, committing 40,000 prints of a measured 52,937 (true depth 25,405 + 27,532, needing 26 and 28 calls). The truncated=True / completeness_ok=False signal is honest but lives in the PASS SUMMARY, not on the tape line, so a later signed-flow join reads a prefix of the day as the day — and because pagination is time-ordered the distortion is systematic, not noise. The rule: for any append-only family whose records do not self-describe their window's completeness, size the page cap against the MEASURED worst case (not the typical case) before the pull, and re-pull to cursor exhaustion rather than leaving a prefix committed. L315 2026-08-08 A byte-capped backfill's SELECTION RULE becomes the consuming probe's sampling frame — commit the manifest before the probe runs, or the frame gets written after the numbers are seen. Phase 1 could afford 17 of 328 identified settled sports games, so which 17 is a research decision, not an ops detail: ordering is a round-robin over series (a plain sorted() prefix would have been one league's alphabet, i.e. a single-competition sample presented as cross-sport), games are pulled whole or not at all (a half-pulled game is a silently biased unit), and the stop is a declared byte cap enforced on measured on-disk bytes. The realized sample is outcome-BLIND (only "does a declared settlement family resolve this ticker", never the result) but NOT random: it skews to the alphabetically-first game of each league in the earliest days, and to games heavy enough to survive whole-game atomicity. The rule: when a data pull is capped, the cap, the ordering and the atomicity unit are part of the eventual CI's provenance — record them as a coverage manifest in the same commit as the tape, before any scoring code touches it. L316 2026-08-08 A one-sided max_day window does NOT freeze a pin against a family that grows BACKWARD. L140's time-bomb discipline says a test pinning real tape numbers must close its window; scripts/kalshi_trades_ticker_inventory.py did that with max_day="2026-08-03", which is airtight for a family that only accretes newer days. tape/kalshi_trades/ has no scheduled writer (L313), so the way it actually grows is a BACKFILL: the 2026-08-08 phase-1 pull added dt=2026-07-07 … dt=2026-07-12, every one of them OLDER than the frozen max_day and therefore inside a window that had been closed on purpose. Two L292 acceptance pins (n_days == 1 and days == [M1_DAY], n_series == 20) went red on tape that was added correctly — the assertions were right and the WINDOW was wrong. The rule: freeze a tape window on BOTH ends, or on neither. max_day alone encodes an unstated assumption that collection is forward-only, and any family whose writer is a manual/backfill pull violates it the first time someone runs the pull. Repair: symmetric min_day added to _family_files/trade_tape_inventory/the CLI and the L292 pins re-frozen at min_day == max_day == M1_DAY — the pinned NUMBERS are unchanged, only the window is now closed on both sides. L317 2026-08-09 A stranded-tape sweep that matches branch NAMES, not branch CONTENT, is only as complete as its list of names — and the collector's actual fallback path outgrew that list without anyone updating it. The kalshi-collector Routine's trigger config carries outcomes.git_repository.git_info.branches: ["claude/determined-goodall"] — a CCR session-outcome branch (auto-suffixed per session) that the collector lands on whenever BOTH its push to main and its own manual tape/hourly-<ts>Z fallback fail. Nobody had ever swept that branch family. Enumerating every remote branch by commit date (not by name-pattern) found the single most recent commit on the ENTIRE remote sitting on claude/determined-goodall-5llz9l, newer than anything on main or on any tape/hourly-*/tape/burst-* ref. A content-level diff (each candidate's own added lines vs main's CURRENT file content, not vs the commit's parent — squash-merged research branches show hundreds of commits "ahead" of main by raw ancestry alone, which is noise, not stranded tape) found 4 commits / 7,114 real capture lines genuinely missing (2026-08-06 04:12 through 2026-08-08 16:05, across weather_books/crypto_hourly/polymarket_macro_pairs/sports_pairs/hyperliquid_funding/orderbook_depth/perp_tape) that every prior "sweep found zero new lines" run had never seen, because it was checking the wrong branches, not because there was nothing to find. Recovering that data also repaired the accuracy of the pre-existing (L117/L129) COLLECTOR HEALTH ADVISORY: before recovery, main's newest visible cloud-leg capture was 23.3h old (one bad sample from tripping the same >=24h dead-leg bucket the long-silent vps leg was already in); after, it reads 8.5h, the true figure. The rule: a stranded-fallback sweep keyed on branch NAME is only as good as its enumeration of every place a fallback can land — when a new fallback mechanism appears (a new CCR session-outcome pattern, a renamed Routine, a new retry path), the sweep silently stops covering it and reports false-clean forever. Prefer enumerating branches by commit recency / content diff over maintaining a fixed name-pattern allowlist, or at minimum re-audit the allowlist whenever a Routine's job_config changes. L318 2026-08-09 A venue's rate BASELINE (interest floor, dead-band anchor, etc.) is a third state beside "clamped to zero" and "free-floating" — a cross-venue join must count both legs' constant-valued windows before quoting a window count as independent information. tape/hyperliquid_funding/'s second data-quality pass found Hyperliquid's hourly funding rate pinned to exactly its 0.01%-per-8h interest baseline (1.25e-05/hr) in 52.78% (BTC) / 55.84% (ETH) of 1601 committed hours, in long autocorrelated runs (median 2-3h, longest 269-270h). Joined against Kalshi's own 0.0 clamp (scripts/q42_crossvenue_funding_join.py's 8h windows), 28.3% (BTC) / 35.9% (ETH) of the 198 joined windows/asset have BOTH legs pinned simultaneously, and across all 127 such windows the differential takes exactly one distinct value — zero variance, i.e. arithmetic on two constants, not a measurement. "Never exactly zero" (Q42's own premise) and "an independent observation" are different claims; a pinned hour tells you nothing a neighboring pinned hour didn't already say. Walking records in capture order and flagging any whose start_ms sits BEHIND the newest hour an earlier-captured record already archived directly proves that pass's working tree was missing its predecessor's write — measured live, 13 of 79 incremental records per coin (16.46%), re-emitting between 1 and 15 hours of already-covered ground each time (0 value conflicts on any re-emitted hour — the race inflates counts, it does not corrupt values). L320 2026-08-09 A hardcoded historical cross-check constant embedded in a live, still-growing-population script silently converts honest population growth into a false alarm. scripts/q42_crossvenue_funding_join.py carries PART1_BTC_ZERO_FRACTION = 0.669 (tol 0.05) as a sanity check against Q42 part 1's original population. The join is genuinely healthy and un-frozen (198 windows/asset, 0 partial-excluded) — but its population has grown honestly since that constant was set, and the joined BTC zero-fraction has drifted to 0.7222 (|Δ|=0.0532 > 0.05 tol), so the hardcoded check now reads False on correct, growing data. L321 2026-08-09 A minority-side (sign-variation) gate that counts units NOT exclusive to that side can open with zero independently-resamplable minority blocks — exactly the population it exists to require. Q54's sealed S79 probe gates on min_minority_side_units=2 (L312) before scoring, and the gate opened for the first time with units_per_side: {no: 2, yes: 24} out of n_units=24 total. An independent verifier re-check (two-agent rule, Q54's first outcome-dependent execution) noticed the arithmetic doesn't add up as a partition: both NO-side units are ALSO among the 24 YES-side units, because a "unit" here is a GAME (L6's blocking key) and a single game can contain both a YES entry and a NO entry. So no game-block is exclusively minority-side — the block bootstrap can never resample a minority-only configuration, which is the entire point L312's floor was meant to guarantee (an ALIVE reading conditioned on genuine sign variation, not on 2 stray minority rows sitting inside otherwise-majority blocks). This did not change the S79 verdict here (headline DEAD-by-CI on its own terms), but it means the gate as coded is weaker than its own docstring claims, and a future candidate could clear min_minority_side_units while still having a bootstrap that is, in the units that matter, unable to see the minority side at all. Rule: a minority-side/sign-variation floor must count units EXCLUSIVE to that side (a block containing only minority-sign entries), not units that merely contain at least one minority-sign entry alongside majority ones. UNENFORCED reports/q54_s79_flow_continuation.json; Q54 two-agent verifier re-check (research loop, 2026-08-09); relates to L6 (blocking key), L41 (unit floor), L312 (the sign-variation gate this refines)
L322 2026-08-09 n_units from a pooled (not equal-weighted) block bootstrap overstates independence when unit sizes are ragged — report Kish effective n beside it. Q54's S79 verdict pools 133 individual entries into 24 game-blocks of wildly uneven size (23, 17, 12, 12, 11, 11, 8, 5, 4, 4, 3, and ten blocks of size ≤2 down to 1) and block-bootstraps the POOLED mean (each entry weighted equally, not each game weighted equally) — the standard and correct choice for L6's blocking discipline, but it means the "24 vs the L41 floor of 10" headline reads as 2.4× headroom when the actual precision is closer to an 11.6-unit sample: Kish's effective sample size (Σn_i)² / Σ(n_i²) for these block sizes ≈ 11.6, i.e. 1.16× the floor. The CI width the probe reports ([−0.272,+0.152], roughly 2.6× an iid-assumption half-width) is honest and already reflects this — the bootstrap itself doesn't lie — but a HEADLINE that only quotes n_units next to min_units invites a reader (or a future prober skimming the JSON) to think the adequacy margin is larger than it is. Distinct from L41 (which sets the floor) and from L221 (byte-redundant re-capture inflating a DIFFERENT count): this is about a floor comparison silently changing meaning under unequal-weight pooling, not about padding either number. Rule: whenever core.bootstrap.block_bootstrap pools entries within unequally-sized blocks, report Kish effective n (or an equivalent design-effect note) alongside raw n_units in any report/finding that will be read as an adequacy signal. Test-pinned reports/q54_s79_flow_continuation.json; Q54 two-agent verifier re-check (research loop, 2026-08-09); relates to L41 (unit floor), L6 (blocking key), sibling to L221 (a different failure mode of "N looks bigger than it is")
L323 2026-08-09 14,724 exact-timestamp trade-print ties on committed tape, 4,605 of them with DIFFERING prices, are resolved by file read order today — an accidental, not a declared, tie-break rule. While independently re-deriving Q54's S79 entry-candidate population from raw tape/kalshi_trades/ prints, the verifier found the "first print at/after decision instant t" rule (first_agreeing_print in scripts/q54_s79_flow_continuation_probe.py) has to break ties whenever two or more prints share an identical (ticker, created_time) key — which happens 14,724 times in the committed backfill, 4,605 of them with genuinely different yes_price between the tied prints. Today's behavior is deterministic (committed JSONL files are stably ordered, so re-running reproduces byte-identical results — confirmed independently) but the ordering is an ARTIFACT of how the tape was written, not a rule anyone chose; 76 of Q54's 148 entry prices change under an adversarial (reversed) tie-break, though the headline DEAD-by-CI verdict is unaffected either way. This is a live-fire instance of the same class as L27/L165: a number that is reproducible is not automatically a number that is PRINCIPLED — "reproducible because the file happens to be sorted this way" is different from "reproducible because the rule names an explicit key." Rule: any probe resolving simultaneous-timestamp ties in trade/print tape should tie-break on an explicit field (e.g. trade_id, if monotonic within a capture) rather than relying on incidental file order, and should say so in the probe's own docstring next to its other pre-registered choices. L325 2026-08-10 Freezing a mutable artifact for ONE consumer does not freeze it for the others — a mutable-artifact repair owes a repo-wide READER audit, not a per-file fix. And reports/ is as mutable as tape/. L284 (2026-08-05) found that Q51 milestone 3's own firing command (q51_maker_fillsim.py --build-cache, which overwrites tape/q51_settlement_cache/settlement.json in place) would turn pytest -q RED on the day it fired, and repaired it correctly: it froze settlement-m2-2026-08-04.json, repointed the three pinned cases in tests/test_q51_maker_fillsim.py at the snapshot with an explicit cache_path=, and added a sha256 identity pin so the snapshot could not be swapped. It then VERIFIED the repair by simulating the re-pull — 42/42 green. On 2026-08-10 the milestone fired and the suite went red anyway: 6 acceptance cases across 4 SIBLING modules were reading the same mutable artifact and had never been audited — tests/test_q51_m3_preflight.py twice via P.run(with_hazard=False), which defaults to the LIVE cache (under the re-pulled cache 48 of 60 markets' close_time moves EARLIER to the actual finalize instant, the close-day table collapses 11 rows → 8, and the "57 units is the terminal row" pin fails), and tests/test_q51_m3_fill_projection.py once via calibration_vs_milestone_2(), whose comparand defaulted to the live reports/q51_maker_fillsim.json — the exact file step (iii) of the same recipe rewrites — so it silently began comparing the 08-04 projection against the 08-10 RESULT and returned MIXED instead of over-inclusive; tests/test_q51_maker_fillsim_rederive.py once via R.rederive(), which defaults to the live reports/q51_maker_fillsim_rows.jsonl (40 rows -> 294); and tests/test_settlement_sources.py TWICE, where L300's own acceptance pin ("the declared settlement registry resolves 9 of the 42 traded tickers, 9 GAME units, below the L41 floor of 10") reads the same live cache and now resolves 32 — a module that has nothing to do with Q51's fill-sim and was written by a different run, which is exactly why a per-file repair could never have found it. Both sibling modules were built AFTER L284's repair, by runs that had read L284 and adopted its frozen-snapshot vocabulary (M2_CACHE, FROZEN_M2_CACHE) while still leaving a default path to the live artifact. Two generalisations: (a) the question a mutable-artifact repair must answer is *"who else reads this?"* (grep the artifact path across tests/ and scripts/), never *"is the file I found red now green?"*; (b) the repair vocabulary was cache-shaped and the second leak was a REPORT — any artifact a repo's own planned command rewrites is unpinnable, so reports/*.json used as a test comparand needs the same freeze-and-hash treatment tape/ gets (L191 "pin to a slice that cannot grow" is about MUTABILITY, not about being tape). Repair, non-weakening (no assertion relaxed, deleted or reordered): the two preflight cases pass cache_path=P.FROZEN_M2_CACHE explicitly; calibration_vs_milestone_2's default comparand is now the frozen reports/q51_maker_fillsim-m2-2026-08-04.json (sha256 745c4eb7…83c24, recovered byte-identically from the committed tree), pinned by hash AND by a source-text assertion that the default can never point back at the live report; the rederive acceptance case reads a newly frozen reports/q51_maker_fillsim_rows-m2-2026-08-04.jsonl (sha256 780b1a7d...5ca50), also hash-pinned; and L300's pin is re-anchored by substituting the frozen snapshot into the source registry with dataclasses.replace(path_glob=...), reproducing its published counts EXACTLY (9/9). Every repaired module keeps a live-path case (SHAPE-only, or a directional measurement of the new state) so no live path is silently abandoned. Side-effect worth recording separately: the same re-pull moved L300's settlement-resolvability gate from 9 GAME units (below the L41 floor) to 32 (above it) — a data gate a prior run measured as CLOSED is now OPEN as a by-product of an unrelated milestone; it revives nothing (S79 fired DEAD-by-CI on 2026-08-09) but is pinned directionally so the next run does not rediscover it. Test-pinned findings/2026-08-10-q51-m3-admissible-null-verdict.md §5; L284; L191
L326 2026-08-10 A block bootstrap's n_units can clear the L41 floor on units that contain no information at all — report the INFORMATIVE unit count (units with ≥1 non-zero leg) beside it. Q51 milestone 3's headline all_intervals branch reports 51 game units against the L41 floor of 10, which reads as 5x headroom. But 230 of its 294 legs (78.2%) are UNFILLED and contribute an EXACT 0.0 by construction, and 27 of the 51 units consist entirely of such legs — they are perfectly resamplable and perfectly uninformative, contributing an identical zero to every one of the 10,000 resamples. The information-bearing count is 24, and it is already sitting in the report under a different branch's name (conditional_on_fill.n_units_games == 24) without anyone having connected the two. The verdict survives here (24 > 10, and the CI straddles zero either way, so nothing is overturned) — this is a REPORTING rule, not a re-verdict. Distinct from L322's Kish effective n, which this case defeats: Kish over legs is 49.91 of 51 because the unit SIZES are near-uniform (2–6 legs each) — an all-zero unit is perfectly "sized" and wholly uninformative, so a size-based effective-n correction cannot see this at all. The general shape: whenever a design admits a structural zero (an unfilled maker leg, an untriggered signal, a no-trade day), n_units counts the DESIGN's units while the evidence lives only in the units where the structural zero did not fire — quote both, and gate on the second. Test-pinned findings/2026-08-10-q51-m3-admissible-null-verdict.md §3 qualifier 2; L322; L41
L327 2026-08-10 A join's adequacy lives in the JOINED CELL, never in either leg's marginal count — and when one leg's settlement is EMBEDDED in another family's capture record, the join's yield is set by that family's CADENCE, not by its row count. S81 was registered (Q21 round #26, verifier-attacked) on marginals measured leg-by-leg: Hyperliquid funding "regime contrast pin 848 / sub-baseline 607 / negative 179" and "215 BTC settlement events / ~338 regime runs ≫ 10-unit floor". Both marginals are real. The JOINED informative cell — the population the probe actually resamples — is 19 entries / 15 fillable / 8 fillable regime runs / Kish effective n 4.79 — 19 of the 136 total sub_baseline+negative snapshots in the full funding census (14% of that captured-informative population; just 3.3% of the full 574-entry joinable universe — two different denominators, do not conflate) and BELOW the plain L41 floor of 10 before any stricter condition is applied. The mechanism is specific and generalises: crypto_hourly's settlement is one of L300's three EMBEDDED families — each record's previous_settlement reports ONLY the event that closed immediately before that capture, so an entry snapshot is settlement-joinable only if ANOTHER capture lands in the following hour. Starting ~2026-07-15 the collector's capture-pass cadence fell from ~46–50/day to ~2–8/day (reaching that floor ~07-23, one relapse day 07-29 at 27) — an ~8x collapse in one consistent unit — and joinable entries/day collapsed with it (last joinable entry 2026-08-05). Measured counterfactual on the same tape: had every captured snapshot been settlement-paired, the same cell would hold 136 entries/94 runs, of which 105 fillable entries over 77 fillable runs, Kish 58.3 — 5.8x the floor. So the blocker was never the funding tape or the idea; it is a bounded settlement PULL (the Q52/Q54 phase-1 shape), and calling it a calendar wait would have routed a future run at the wrong wall (the L300/L313 failure mode). Rule: an idea-stage adequacy claim must quote the joined-cell count produced by the SAME predicate the probe will use, per cell, with the blocking unit already applied — never the product or the minimum of two marginals. Corollary for embedded-settlement families: their effective join yield is P(a capture lands in the settlement window), which is a cadence property, so a cadence regression silently becomes a POPULATION regression in every downstream probe. Test-pinned findings/2026-08-10-q56-s81-join-cell-adequacy.md; scripts/q56_s81_funding_regime_settlement_probe.py::population_report; reports/q56_s81_funding_regime_settlement.json; relates to L300 (embedded settlement families), L318/L324 (regime-run blocking), L322/L326 (effective vs nominal n), L41 (the floor being compared against)
L328 2026-08-10 There is no scalar "book cadence" — tape/orderbook_depth/'s intra-ticker revisit interval is BIMODAL, and the repo's two contradictory figures (~29 min vs ~3h) are the p25 and the p75 of the SAME distribution. Measured over all 34 committed depth day-files (Q56/S80 milestone): on the 87 TRADED sports GAME tickers the pooled consecutive-snapshot gap is p25 28.72 min / median 31.33 min / p75 179.54 min / p90 360.39 min; on all 108,668 depth tickers it is p25 31.09 / median 31.99 / p75 179.36 / p90 360.14. The ~31-min mode is a revisit WITHIN one collector burst; the ~180-min mode is the gap BETWEEN bursts. So the Q21 round-#26 verifier's "~29 min on traded sports tickers" and the graveyard's "the ~3-hour book cadence" (quoted as a blanket maker-fill blocker in scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py's own gate text and inherited by L68/L131's death sentence) are BOTH correct and neither refutes the other — quoting either as *the* cadence is a category error, and quoting the ~3h one as a blocker silently converts a p75 into a floor. Rule: report the revisit-interval QUANTILES for the specific population a probe will trade, never a single number, and state the population in the same breath. Second, separable fact from the same table (the L283 read-across): across ALL depth tickers the median is 1 snapshot ever (only 46,709 of 108,668 tickers are ever seen twice), so orderbook_depth is L283-shaped — a census, not a panel — *in aggregate*; but the traded-sports subset IS a genuine panel (87/87 tickers with a book, median 28 snapshots each). L283's warning is about a family; whether it binds a given probe is a property of that probe's POPULATION and must be measured, not inherited. Test-pinned findings/2026-08-10-q56-s80-print-vwap-overshoot-maker-fade.md §4; scripts/q56_s80_print_vwap_overshoot_maker_fade.py::cadence_report → reports/q56_s80_print_vwap_overshoot_maker_fade.json cadence_reconciliation; reconciles L283 (scope) and the ~3h figure in scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py
L329 2026-08-10 A large ex-post print-VWAP-minus-settlement overshoot is NOT a maker edge: decompose it into static_gross_at_vwap vs realized_gross_at_fill, because the second can be ZERO while the first is +7.5¢ — and the difference is adverse selection, not fees. Q56/S80 measured, on 74 queue-aware maker fills whose evidence is broker_truth executed prints: static gross at the ticker's own full-sample print VWAP +$0.07508, realized gross at our actual real_bid resting price −$0.00095, i.e. an adverse-selection cost of +$0.07603 that consumed 101.3% of the static edge BEFORE the flat $0.01 maker fee was charged at all (net −$0.01095; corrected 2026-08-10 by the independent verifier — 0.07602780845007773/0.07508186250413179, the original 100.1% was an arithmetic slip). The direction of the arithmetic is what makes this decisive: a resting bid is STRICTLY CHEAPER than the average print, so under random fills the realized gross must EXCEED the static gross — any shortfall is pure selection, and it cannot be argued away as an entry-price artifact. Two consequences. (1) At idea-registration time, an "overshoot vs fee" gate computed from prints (or from any ex-post average price) is NOT a proxy for a maker edge and must not be registered as one; the only honest gate is a queue-aware fill-sim's own realized gross. (2) The same probe shows the diagnostic cuts both ways and is therefore not just a maker-pessimism prior: on its OTHER leg the adverse-selection cost is −$0.03026 (genuine price improvement — the fills we got were better than the average print), and that leg still lost, because ITS defect was a wrong-signed mechanism. Reporting both numbers separates "the signal is backwards" from "the signal is right but unfillable at a profit", which a bare P&L cannot. Family: L39 (queue-blind proxies over-credit the income leg), L48 (a turnover proxy rules a cell OUT, never IN), S1/Q37's break-even-vs-realized-on-fills hit-rate identity (9.02% vs 14.69%), S23. Test-pinned findings/2026-08-10-q56-s80-print-vwap-overshoot-maker-fade.md §3; scripts/q56_s80_print_vwap_overshoot_maker_fade.py::adverse_selection_decomposition → reports/q56_s80_print_vwap_overshoot_maker_fade.json (adverse_selection_registered_leg, mirror_leg_DESCRIPTIVE_ONLY.adverse_selection)
L330 2026-08-11 An EMBEDDED settlement family's missing coverage is a bookkeeping gap, not lost data — check the public endpoint before accepting a calendar wait. Q56/S81's gate was shut because crypto_hourly's settlement is embedded (each capture reports only the event that closed immediately before it), so when the capture cadence fell ~8x in mid-July the joinable population fell with it, leaving 282 of 856 entry legs unresolvable and the informative cell at 8 fillable runs against a floor of 10. The 08-10 finding's own estimate of the wait was ~2 weeks to the L41 floor and ~10+ weeks to the Kish floor. The actual repair took one bounded, read-only pull: 282/282 fetched, completeness 1.0000, 282/282 carrying a binary result — Kalshi's public unauthenticated /markets/{ticker} still serves finalized results for hourly crypto brackets that closed five weeks earlier. Generalization: capture cadence determines what an embedded family RECORDED, never what the venue still SERVES; before a probe is parked on a calendar gate, ask whether the missing half is a pull rather than a wait. Corollary for the backfill's own design: its selection rule must be EXHAUSTIVE and declared before any result is read (all unjoinable tickers, every cell, fillable and not), because a backfill that chose which missing settlements to fetch could bias a sealed probe's population without touching one byte of the probe. Test-pinned findings/2026-08-11-q56-s81-settlement-backfill-and-first-firing.md §1-2; scripts/q56_s81_settlement_backfill.py (docstring = the declared rule)
L331 2026-08-11 A data-adequacy finding's "if every snapshot were paired" counterfactual is a falsifiable FORECAST — record it as one, then score it. L327 (2026-08-10) computed, from the same tape, that a fully-paired S81 informative cell would hold 105 fillable entries over 77 fillable regime runs, Kish 58.3. After the 2026-08-11 backfill the realized cell is 105 / 77 / 58.333… — exact on all three (the counterfactual's TOTAL informative figures, 136 entries / 94 runs, realize as 137 / 95 because two more entry snapshots were captured between the runs; the forecast is exact on precisely the fillable quantities the gate is defined over, and the one-unit drift is capture growth, recorded rather than rounded away). That agreement is worth more than the convenience: it is the strongest available confirmation that the diagnosis behind it (*the wall is the JOIN, not the funding tape*) was correct rather than merely plausible, and it converts a data-adequacy note from a description into a tested prediction. The inverse is the real value: had the realized cell come in materially SHORT of the counterfactual, the diagnosis would have been falsified and the missing mechanism would have to be found BEFORE any CI was quoted. So: every adequacy verdict that projects a post-repair population should state the projection as a numbered prediction, and the run that performs the repair should score it explicitly before reading a single outcome. Family: L308 (a projected fill rate vs the realized one), L274/L275 (gate counts the wrong unit). Protocol findings/2026-08-11-q56-s81-settlement-backfill-and-first-firing.md §2 vs findings/2026-08-10-q56-s81-join-cell-adequacy.md ("Counterfactual, measured on the same tape")
L332 2026-08-11 Declaring a new settlement family is a GLOBAL change: any test asserting "X is unresolvable" is really asserting a property of the registry at a moment in time. Adding q56_settlement_cache as the TENTH declared source in core/settlement_sources.py turned a fixed-slice acceptance pin red (test_acceptance_the_join_loses_most_of_the_informative_cell_to_settlement_pairing, assert 0 >= 1) even though the property it encodes — that the EMBEDDED crypto_hourly.previous_settlement pairing loses most of the cell — was unchanged and still true. The non-weakening repair is to scope the measurement's INPUT to the source whose property it encodes (resolve_market_results(..., sources=[crypto_hourly]), the same source-filter device tests/test_settlement_sources.py::_frozen_m2_sources already uses for the Q51 cache), keep the original assertion byte-for-byte, and measure the new full-registry state in a NEW case rather than absorbing it into the old one. L335 2026-08-11 trade_id IS an adequate explicit tie-break key for Kalshi trade prints — and it is NOT a chronological one. Both halves have to be stated together, or the "fix" over-claims. L323 left its own repair conditional on an unchecked assumption: "tie-break on an explicit field (e.g. trade_id, *if monotonic within a capture*)". Measured over the whole committed tape/kalshi_trades/ (213,488 broker_truth prints, 6 days): trade_id is present on 213,488/213,488, has 0 global collisions, and fails to totally order 0 of the 25,781 exact-timestamp (ticker, created_time) tie groups — the key EXISTS, which is the fact the repair depends on. But concordance between trade_id order and clock order, over 109,950 consecutive untied pairs, is 0.500282 — a coin flip; the ids' UUID version nibbles are ~uniform over {4,5,6,7}, so they are not time-ordered UUIDv7s and encode no clock. So adopting (created_time, trade_id) buys DECLAREDNESS and reproducibility — the probe stops depending on how the tape happened to be written — and buys NOTHING about which trade actually executed first. A probe that switches keys must say the first thing and must not imply the second. Scale of what this governs: 48.46% of admitted prints sit inside a tie group, 7,999 groups disagree on yes_price (spread p50 1c, p90 4c, max 61c), largest tie group 303 prints. Test-pinned IDLE RUN policy (a), 2026-08-11 (research loop, main-context build — no worker-agent tool was available in this firing; same posture recorded for L282); scripts/trade_print_tiebreak_audit.py; extends L323
L336 2026-08-11 An order statistic that tests a property of key K, computed over a sequence sorted BY K, is biased by its own sort — the max-of-k artifact. Building L335's chronological-concordance check, the first version sorted each ticker's prints by (created_time, trade_id) and walked consecutive pairs whose timestamps strictly increase. On a key since proven random it read 0.412, not 0.500 — and the shortfall was entirely manufactured by the estimator: sorting a tie group by trade_id puts that group's MAXIMUM id last, and a max-of-k draw then loses its comparison against the next (unconditioned) id more often than chance. Restricting the statistic to prints that are the SOLE print at their timestamp removes the conditioning, and the SAME tape reads 0.500282 over 109,950 pairs. The dangerous property is that 0.412 looks like a finding — "weakly anti-chronological" is a sentence someone would have written down. Rule: never compute a statistic about key K over a sequence K itself ordered; restrict to units whose position K did not touch, and say in the function's own docstring which units those are and why. Test-pinned IDLE RUN policy (a), 2026-08-11 (research loop, main-context build; caught in-build, before any number left the session); scripts/trade_print_tiebreak_audit.py::chronological_concordance METHOD NOTE; relates to L27/L165 (reproducible is not principled)
L337 2026-08-11 A maker fill-sim that credits a fill on any qualifying executed print in a window, with no accounting for the SIZE resting ahead of the order at its own price, produces an UPPER BOUND on both fill rate and mean edge — and the gap can be an order of magnitude. Q51 milestone 3's headline (scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py) reads 64/294 fills (21.77%) and mean +$0.010068 by crediting a fill whenever ANY print in the interval crosses the resting price, regardless of size. An independent verifier (two-agent re-check) measured actual queue depth from the SAME orderbook_depth ladders the probe already reads: median resting size at the entry price was 500.74 contracts vs a median filling print of 8.9 — only 6.2% of the 64 credited fills have a single print larger than the queue ahead. Under the MOST GENEROUS queue-aware rule available (crediting the entire interval's cumulative qualifying volume, not one print, as clearing the queue), only 25/64 fills survive: fill rate collapses to 8.50%, mean to +$0.000714, CI [-$0.010387, +$0.012823] — still straddles zero, still fails the tick gate, 14 informative units (still > the L41 floor of 10). This particular verdict is unaffected (the bias runs toward the null), but the point estimates 21.77%/+$0.010068 are upper bounds by a ~14x/~2.5x factor, not measurements, and the original writeup stated the omission ("no queue-position number is computed") without stating its direction or size. Protocol findings/2026-08-10-q51-m3-admissible-null-verdict.md (Verifier disposition, 2026-08-11); scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py; extends L48/L250 (departure-proxy fill rules) to print-based-but-still-size-blind rules
L338 2026-08-11 A field-orientation or monotonicity claim supported by "the effect strengthens/decays as the join window widens" must name the POPULATION it was measured on — the same tape, same field, same join logic can show the OPPOSITE trend on a different slice. Q51's taker_book_side orientation correction cites an 86.8%→84.6%→70.4% monotonic decay across ≤15min/≤60min/any-age join windows as evidence the field relationship is real, not an artifact — an independent verifier reproduced this EXACTLY inside the probe's 60-ticker sports-only population. But on the FULL day tape (all 2,713 depth tickers), the bid side's agreement rate moves the OTHER way: 62.99% → 66.9% → 69.6%, RISING with staleness. The directional conclusion (a "bid" print lifts the ask, not hits the bid) still holds overwhelmingly on both populations (63.0% vs 34.8% full-tape at ≤15min), so this doesn't overturn the verdict — but "decays monotonically, which is exactly the degradation a true relationship shows" is a claim scoped to the sports universe, and reads uncorrected as a claim about the tape in general. UNENFORCED findings/2026-08-10-q51-m3-admissible-null-verdict.md (Verifier disposition, 2026-08-11); scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py module docstring
L339 2026-08-12 "The MOST GENEROUS rule available" is a falsifiable superlative, not a hedge — if a strictly more generous rule exists on the same data under a DIFFERENT family of assumption, the superlative is simply false, even though the chosen rule is still the most generous WITHIN its own family. L337's queue-aware fill-sim (scripts/q51_queue_aware_fillsim.py) credits a fill when the interval's cumulative qualifying print volume clears the resting size at the order's own price — and both L337's cell and the module's own docstring called this "the MOST GENEROUS queue-aware rule available." An independent verifier found a strictly more generous rule on the identical tape: PRICE-PRIORITY credit — crediting any leg with ≥1 qualifying print strictly THROUGH the resting price, on the theory that the entire level in front must have cleared first for that print to trade. That rule credits 32/294 fills, not the shipped rule's 25/294. Neither rule is wrong; they encode different physical assumptions (queue-depth-clearing vs. price-time priority), and the shipped rule is genuinely the most generous member of its OWN family (cumulative-volume-vs-resting-size). But "most generous available" as written implies a search over ALL possible rules, which never happened. Not verdict-moving here (the more generous rule is also more negative on this tape — mean −$0.006938, CI still straddling zero — so the correction strengthens rather than undermines the discount finding), but a future case could differ. Rule: a generosity superlative must name the FAMILY it maximizes over in the same clause ("most generous rule of the X family"), never left bare, or a reader treats a family-local maximum as a global upper bound on the discount. Protocol 2026-08-12 verifier dispatch on the L337 follow-on build, /tmp/.../scratchpad/attack3.py (32/294 price-priority reproduction); scripts/q51_queue_aware_fillsim.py module docstring (corrected same run)
L340 2026-08-12 A discount reported as two "Nx" factors travels with a real risk of the factors landing next to the WRONG quantity — cite each factor bound to its quantity in the same clause, never as a bare ordered pair. L337's cell wrote "the point estimates 21.77%/+$0.010068 are upper bounds by a ~14x/~2.5x factor" — ambiguous but decodable in order (rate first, mean second: rate 21.77%→8.50% is a 2.56x ratio; mean +$0.010068→+$0.000714 is a 14.1x ratio). When the L337 follow-on module's docstring restated this as prose ("the gap ... was ~14x on the fill count-derived rate and ~2.5x on the mean"), the factors were bound to the WRONG quantities — swapped. An independent verifier caught it by recomputing both ratios directly from the script's own printed output (fill_rate_retained_fraction=0.390625 → 2.56x; mean_retained_fraction=0.070946 → 14.1x) rather than trusting the prose. Not verdict-moving (no number or CI changed, only which English sentence described which ratio) but exactly the kind of error that compounds silently across restatements — this run's own L337 cell had ALREADY carried the ambiguous ordered-pair form that made the swap possible one hop later. Same class as L165 (a live claim needs a committed, checkable artifact) one step further: even a committed artifact's own PROSE about its numbers needs the same scrutiny as the numbers themselves. Protocol 2026-08-12 verifier dispatch on the L337 follow-on build; scripts/q51_queue_aware_fillsim.py module docstring (corrected same run, both factors now stated with their quantity in the same clause: "~2.56x on the fill rate ... and ~14.1x on the mean")
L341 2026-08-12 A test that pins exact values read out of a reports/ artifact some probe REGENERATES is red-on-correct-data waiting to happen — freeze a dated snapshot and pin THAT. L325's mutable-artifact rule, one directory over, and the first instance found by a run that tripped it itself. While checking Q54's eligibility, this run re-fired scripts/q54_s79_flow_continuation_probe.py — sealed, unmodified, and deliberately SELF-ACTIVATING over every committed trade day (it globs tape/kalshi_trades/dt=*.jsonl rather than pinning a DAY constant).
