Neither artifact was wrong; the PIN was. Two properties make this a class, not an incident: (a) reports/ is as mutable as tape/ (L325's own words) and a self-activating probe guarantees it will move; (b) the pinning test and the regenerating script live in different lanes, so nobody editing either one sees the coupling. Repair pattern (same as L284/L325's frozen settlement cache): copy the verdict-bearing artifact to <name>-YYYY-MM-DD.json, pin it with a sha256 identity check so the comparand cannot be swapped, and let the live file move — plus a direction-only test (n_units_now >= n_units_frozen) so a SHRINKING population is still caught. Today's re-derivation is itself corroborating, not verdict-class: the larger population reproduces the same headline (mean −$0.0771, 95% block-bootstrap-by-game CI [−$0.2190, +$0.0692], broker_truth entry + settlement, DEAD-by-CI), and the kalshi-edge-hunter's independent verifier quoted the identical 45/−0.077/[−0.219,+0.069] hours earlier in findings/2026-08-12-q21-round28-idea-gen.md. L342 2026-08-12 A sign-variation floor measured on the wrong count does not self-heal as the population grows — it degrades, because the touching count grows with the majority arm while the exclusive count can stay pinned at zero. L321 recorded Q54/S79's minority-side gate opening at exactly its floor (2 touching units of 24, 0 of them exclusively minority-side) and read as a knife-edge event. Re-measured 2026-08-12 through the sealed probe's own outcome-blind path after the trade-print backfill grew the population from 1 to 6 committed days: units 24 -> 45, minority-side TOUCHING units 2 -> 6 (now 3x the floor of 2, i.e. the gate looks progressively HEALTHIER), while EXCLUSIVELY-minority units are still 0 and all 6 minority units are mixed blocks. The mechanism is structural, not a small sample: at Kalshi's 80/20 retail buy skew (L279) a NO-side entry almost always lands in a game that also produced YES entries, so every new day adds minority-touching blocks and no minority-only block. Consequence for anyone reading an adequacy headline: minority_side_units rising away from its floor is not evidence the gate is getting safer, and 'wait for more tape' is not a remedy for this defect class — only the counting rule is. Sibling to L322 (a floor comparison silently changing meaning under unequal-weight pooling) and to L320 (a frozen historical constant drifting against an honestly growing population): three different ways an adequacy number moves without the adequacy moving. Test-pinned IDLE RUN policy (a), 2026-08-12 (research loop, main-context build — no Task/subagent tool exists in this harness, the L287/L288/L290/L291/L295 precedent; DESCRIPTIVE, no verdict, no registry flip, S79 stays DEAD); scripts/q54_minority_exclusivity_audit.py; core/bootstrap.py::minority_side_unit_census; extends L321, relates to L279/L312/L320/L322
L343 2026-08-12 A stale-constant false alarm can silently SELF-HEAL, and a cleared alarm is not evidence the defect was fixed — the same check, on the same unedited code, returned False on 2026-08-09 and True on 2026-08-12 purely because the population grew past it and back inside tolerance. L320 recorded scripts/q42_crossvenue_funding_join.py's PART1_BTC_ZERO_FRACTION = 0.669 (tol 0.05) reading False against a joined BTC zero-fraction that had drifted to 0.7222 on 198 windows/asset. Re-run 3 days later on 210 windows/asset: 0.7048, |Δ|=0.0358, the boolean reads True again — the statistic wandered BACK inside the tolerance band. Two consequences. (a) Whoever reads such a check gets a verdict determined by their run DATE, so neither its False nor its True carries information about the thing it purports to test; a reproducible number is still not a principled one (the L27/L165/L323 family). (b) The obvious repair — re-pin the constant to the fresher reading — would have been WRONG here, because the drift is NON-MONOTONE; re-pinning trades a false alarm today for a false all-clear later. The correct repair is to stop comparing against the frozen number at all: report the drift with its sign and magnitude, state in the record itself that it is not a gate, and re-derive any live bound from the CURRENT population each run. Corollary for the loop: when an audit finds a red check, RE-MEASURE it before writing the repair — an alarm that has since cleared on its own is the strongest evidence that it was never a gate. L344 2026-08-13 Two duplicate-capture detectors with adjacent scopes leave a seam, and the seam holds the worst class: same capture_id, same captured_at, same logical row, DIFFERENT payload — and the obvious key to check it with is provably unsafe. This repo had three duplicate-capture defects and three detectors: L285's repo-wide census sees only BYTE-IDENTICAL lines (its own docstring says so); L218's collision detector is candidate-gated on n_distinct_captured_at > 1, so it cannot see a repeat stamped at ONE instant; L282's (capture_id, ticker) check covers one family. The uncovered class is one pass emitting one item twice with two contradictory answers — no consumer can pick, and any per-row statistic (population size, a bootstrap unit's weight) inflates silently with no other trace. First census over all committed tape (429 files / 2,024,482 rows / 19 capture families): 0 instances — while the SAME pass reproduced both known incidents digit-for-digit (L285's 1,358 byte-identical rows on dt=2026-07-28 split 1,093/228/17/16/2/2 across six families; L218's 7 collided groups across three), so the surface is now fully mapped and fully accounted for. The load-bearing half is what NOT to reuse: the first attempt keyed on tape_gap_monitor.ITEM_IDENTITY_FIELDS, the coarse tuple L218 already uses. That key is sound for L218 *because* L218 also demands two distinct captured_at; without that requirement it false-positived 97 files, incl. 145,855 'extra rows' in one kalshi_trades day (every print of a ticker in one pass shares ('ticker', ...); the real identity is trade_id), plus polymarket_cpi_pairs (many buckets per series) and 137 false pairs in sports_history (two schemas in one family, so every ESPN row collapsed onto event_ticker=None). A guessed row identity is worse than no check. Invariant findings/2026-08-13-tape-duplicate-surface-census.md; census + detector this run over committed tape/ (read-only, no network); extends L285 (corrects L282, corrects L84) and L218 (disposes L210) without superseding either — each detector keeps exactly one class.
L345 2026-08-13 core.settlement_sources.resolve_market_results defaults root="tape" — a RELATIVE path — so a script that anchors every other path via an absolute repo-root constant but leaves this one on the default silently returns a 0-resolved report at EXIT CODE 0 from any other working directory, indistinguishable from a genuine data gate. Caught live by an independent verifier round on the first draft of the Q52/S78 split-feasibility audit: cd /tmp && python3 /home/user/kalshi-headless/scripts/q52_s78_split_feasibility_audit.py printed "0 resolved" and n_train_units: 0 / n_holdout_units: 0 with no error and no warning — the exact opposite of the repo-root run's real answer (34/29). The module had already anchored TRADE_TAPE_GLOB/DEPTH_TAPE_GLOB on an absolute REPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))) constant, so the mismatch was easy to introduce (only one call site left on the shared default) and easy to miss (both code paths "work", one just returns silently-wrong zeros). Repaired by adding DEFAULT_SETTLEMENT_ROOT = os.path.join(REPO, "tape") and passing it explicitly. L346 2026-08-13 A correction that cites "see field X in the output" as its evidence is itself a checkable claim — grep the named field before shipping, or the citation can point at a block that structurally cannot show what's claimed, recreating the exact failure the correction exists to fix, one layer up. Round 1 of a verifier pass on the Q52/S78 split-feasibility audit found the script's headline overclaimed unimodality and dropped Q52's own "≤4-cell" qualifier (see the finding); the FIRST fix's docstring said the era-boundary date was "the date this run's own per-day capture_id counts (see book_cadence_by_era in the output) visibly step down at" — but book_cadence_by_era is keyed by TRADE day, which has a 3-week hole straddling the boundary itself (2026-07-12 → 08-03), so it could not show a step at all. Repaired by adding a field (era_boundary_evidence, scanning every raw tape-family day file near the boundary independent of which days had a trade) that the docstring's claim is checkable against, rather than trusting the citation. L347 2026-08-13 An independent re-derivation must restate the THRESHOLD's epsilon convention, not just its threshold — on a cent-quoted venue an exact >= 0.03 and an epsilon-tolerant >= 0.03 - 1e-9 disagree on real data, and the disagreement can be invisible in the totals. The Q52/S78 redundancy leg (from-scratch second implementation, no shared code) reproduced the sealed probe's split, admitted cells, candidate count, fill count and unit count exactly — but assigned 163 cheap and 96 rich TRAIN prints to a different cell, and reported 6 exclusively-minority holdout units where the probe reported 5, flipping which side was the minority. Cause: 0.71 - 0.68 == 0.029999999999999916 in binary floating point, so an exact comparison calls a genuine three-cent spread TIGHT. Two properties made it nearly undetectable: the per-cell errors were exactly compensating (both implementations totalled 120,437 scored prints), and because the admitted cells were DIAGONAL while the two price buckets inside one snapshot are complementary, every flipped interval swapped one candidate out and its opposite-side twin in — so the candidate and fill COUNTS matched while the per-side census did not. A redundancy check that compares only headline totals would have returned a clean agreement over a real classification defect. Restate the epsilon, and diff the per-cell/per-side breakdown, not just the totals. L348 2026-08-13 "Make every call site pass an explicit root=" is the WRONG enforcement for a relative-path default: explicitness is not the property that matters, ANCHORING is — and the fix belongs at the single shared declaration site, where it also repairs SEALED probes that may not be edited. L345's own proposed candidate check was "every resolve_market_results( call site either passes an explicit root= or is demonstrably run with cwd == repo root". Measured against the actual pre-repair tree by live-firing the built gate: 7 of the 10 cwd-fragile call sites already passed an explicit root= and would have passed that check — because the value they pass is their own parameter defaulting to the relative constant (scripts/q54_s79_flow_continuation_probe.py does this at both of its sites; scripts/settlement_coverage_audit.py at three). Only 3 of 10 omitted root= at all. Two consequences. (a) A checker for this class must classify the VALUE that reaches the parameter (following constants, parameter defaults, from x import CONST re-exports and module.CONST reads), not the SYNTAX of the call. (b) Repairing the one shared declaration site instead of N call sites fixed all 10 at once without editing a single byte of the three verdict-bearing probes — q54_s79 (SEALED mid-verdict, L311), q56_s80 (dead ✗, closed) and q56_s81 (admissible NULL) — which L309/L311 forbid touching; and it makes the ratchet self-enforcing, since reverting that one constant turns all 11 sites red at once. Corollary for the repo's whole enforcement queue: an UNENFORCED row's own suggested candidate is a hypothesis, not a spec — measure the surface it claims to cover before building it. Numbered L348, not L347: this row was ALSO first minted as L347 (the next free number as of origin/main 4003c0a, before this branch was rebased) and collided a second time the same day with the row immediately above, which had already claimed L347 for an unrelated finding after PR #364 took L345/L346 first. L349 2026-08-13 Corrects L338's mechanism: when two readings of the same tape disagree, the factor named in the writeup is not necessarily the factor that moved the number — run the factorial before naming a cause. L338 recorded that a taker_book_side orientation-agreement trend decays (86.8% -> 84.6% -> 70.4%) in scripts/q51_maker_fillsim.py's 60-ticker sports universe but RISES (62.99% -> 66.9% -> 69.6%) on "the FULL day tape (all 2,713 depth tickers)", and concluded the corroboration argument is POPULATION-scoped. Both series reproduce exactly, but the two readings differed on TWO factors at once and only one of them binds. Measured as a 2x2 (population x join rule): switching the 60-ticker sports sample for all 2,713 depth tickers changes not one admitted-print count and not one agreement rate, on either taker_book_side, under either join — the 4 print-carrying tickers outside the probe universe are crypto markets with ONE depth snapshot each and zero prints after it, so they are structurally unmeasurable either way. The entire flip is the JOIN RULE: the probe requires a print to sit strictly inside a CONSECUTIVE snapshot pair (t_i, t_{i+1}], which discards every print after a ticker's last snapshot; attaching each print to the nearest PRECEDING quote instead admits 29,964 bid-side prints instead of 3,532 (>=4x at every window) and reverses the direction. L338's remedy would therefore have sent a future run to scope POPULATIONS while the unstated JOIN RULE kept producing the same disagreement. The directional conclusion both rows were arguing about is untouched: a bid taker prints at or above the ask in >55% of every cell of the grid, under both joins. Numbered L349, not L347: this row was first minted as L347 (the next free number as of origin/main 4003c0a, before this branch was rebased), and collided a THIRD time the same day — PR #364 had already taken L345/L346, PR #365's redundancy finding then took L347, and PR #366's settlement-root fix was renumbered to L348 during its own rebase. Renumbered to L349 by the orchestrating session during this branch's rebase — see kb/00-LOG.md's matching concurrency note. L350 2026-08-14 A tool that can GENERATE a safe plan but cannot CHECK an arbitrary one leaves the check where the failure already happened — in a human's head — and its own generator can be quietly wrong in a direction nobody re-measures. Building L164's deferred multi-instant half exposed two defects in the already-shipped, verifier-reviewed single-instant --protect, neither of which any test could have caught because no checker existed: (1) it grows CHUNK 1 regardless of where the protected instant falls, so an instant in a later chunk inflates the first chunk to reach it — measured [43, 15, 15, 15, 12] where the caller asked for 15-tick chunks, i.e. 2.9x the requested worst-case data loss from the tool whose sole purpose is bounding that loss; and (2) its implicit safety test was ONE-SIDED (it only ever pushes a seam later, so an instant landing just AFTER a seam was equally unprotected and equally unreported). Both survived a verifier round on the original PR because the review checked the FOMC instance, where the instant sits in chunk 1 and the two forms agree exactly. Rule: when a tool emits a plan that must satisfy a stated property, ship the property as a separate CHECKER over an arbitrary plan — not only as a generator that promises to satisfy it — and run the generator's own output back through the checker. The generator and checker being independent code paths is what makes their agreement information rather than tautology; here it also made the committed hand-written FOMC recipe verifiable after the fact (it passes, including for the 18:30:00Z presser it was never checked against). Test-pinned this run; findings/2026-08-14-l164-multi-instant-seam-check.md; scripts/burst_chunk_plan.py; scripts/l164_seam_rederive.py; reports/l164_seam_check.json; extends L164
L351 2026-08-14 A triage ratchet that only triggers on DIRECT contact turns its own registry into a laundering hop: the modules it has already triaged become imports that carry the hazard onward, unasked. L323's tie-break ratchet (inv_trade_print_tiebreak_triage) fired on two things — a kalshi_trades tape reference, or an import of the ONE shared q51_maker_fillsim loader — and its own enforcement cell recorded the gap honestly as a residual: *"a future module consuming prints only through some OTHER triaged helper inherits its disposition without being asked."* That residual was not hypothetical: scripts/q54_minority_exclusivity_audit.py names neither the tape nor the loader, imports the SEALED Q54 probe, reaches prints through P.load_all_prints -> P.entry_candidates -> first_agreeing_print, and was publishing L321's headline counts on an undeclared file-order tie-break — the measurement half of one lesson row silently exposed to another. The repair is structural and self-widening: derive the trigger set FROM THE REGISTRY (every entry whose disposition is not N/A becomes an import that obliges the importer to declare itself), so adding a triage entry ENLARGES the net instead of closing it. Rule: when a ratchet allowlists modules by hazard, its trigger must include "imports something already allowlisted as hazardous", or the allowlist is also an escape hatch. Invariant scripts/invariants.py::inv_trade_print_tiebreak_triage (transitive half, this run); findings/2026-08-14-l323-transitive-print-consumers.md
L352 2026-08-14 An inherited tie-break is a real exposure and still not automatically a moved number — but only a measurement can tell you which, and the measurement is worthless unless the perturbation is shown to REACH the population. Q54's sealed probe selects ONE print per decision instant (first_agreeing_print), and the selected print's yes_price sets both entry_price and price-band admission, so the order of exact-timestamp ties is load-bearing by construction. Measured 2026-08-14 over all committed trade days via python3 scripts/q54_minority_exclusivity_audit.py --sensitivity: 48.47% of the 213,431 eligible sports prints sit in a tie (25,777 groups, 7,998 disagreeing on yes_price), and under a REVERSED tie order 61 of the 221 entry rows (27.6%) fill against a different trade_id and 20 (9.0%) get a different entry_price — identical counts under an explicit trade_id key. Yet every L321 headline field is byte-identical across all three orderings: 221/214 candidates, 45 units, touching {no:6, yes:45}, exclusive {no:0, yes:39}, gate-touching OPEN / gate-exclusive SHUT. So L321's published numbers are order-robust on this population, NON-VACUOUSLY (L249/L250: the perturbation demonstrably reached 27.6% of the fill identities before the headline refused to move). Report the reach beside the invariance or the invariance is unreadable. L353 2026-08-15 if ts.hour == N has a THIRD failure mode nothing measured: the gate hour can be one the scheduler never STARTS a pass in — a permanent property of the (constant, realized schedule) pair, invisible to every missing-day check. L354 2026-08-15 A cadence question answered off the WRONG leg of a multi-leg pass inverts: a pass that starts at :54 reaches its seventh leg in the NEXT clock hour, so "which hours does the collector run in?" has opposite answers depending on which family you ask. Building L353's reachability check, tape/perp_tape/ (leg #7 of hourly_pass.run()) shows 11 of 74 pass instants at 10Z on the identical frozen 20-day slice where sports_pairs (leg #1) shows 0 of 103 — the SAME passes, one clock hour apart, because the collector starts at ~:54 and perp stamps ~:04. Reading reachability off the late leg concludes "10Z fires 11 times, the gate is fine" and the frozen family stays invisible. Generalizes L222's caller-attribution problem to any pass-timing statistic: state WHICH leg's timestamps you measured and WHERE that leg sits in the pass, or the statistic is unanchored. The residual bias of the fixed rule is worth naming too, and is one-directional: a witness family written by some caller OTHER than hourly_pass can only ADD hours, so a first-leg witness under-reports unreachability and can never manufacture an alarm. L355 2026-08-15 A pre-registered adequacy floor stated on an AGGREGATE unit can be vacuous at the granularity the mechanism actually acts on — and the repair is to add the CONDITIONED measurement beside the sealed floor, never to re-tune it. The same run then proved the sharper half: a class-wide statistic quoted beside a conditioned count is itself the error. The 2026-08-15 depth-substrate census pre-registered MIN_SNAPSHOTS_PER_UNIT=2 on the bootstrap unit (the event — the correct unit for a CI, L6). But a 188-bracket crypto hour clears "the unit has >=2 snapshots" while an individual bracket can carry exactly one, and a resting maker order lives on ONE leg: on real tape 160 of the 418 probe-ready crypto units have every leg single. The floor was left untouched and a POST-HOC observability block added. The first cut then made the second error in the same file — it printed the CLASS-WIDE per-leg cell (median 1.0 snapshots/leg, 38.25% with a forward interval, over 101,060 legs) directly beside the 418 probe-ready units, a population that excludes 40,186 of those legs; conditioned on the ready units the same measurement reads median 2.0 and 57.58% — the OPPOSITE direction. An independent verifier caught it before merge. L356 2026-08-15 A fill-sim substrate can be limited by RESOLUTION rather than by absence — and the two look identical until observability is measured conditioned on the scoreable population. On tape/orderbook_depth/ (110,632 tickers / 465,776 snapshots / 39 days) the crypto ladders are BOTH labeled and observable: 258 of 418 probe-ready event-hours have every leg observed >=2 times across 12 distinct days, clearing the census's own pre-registered floors — but their median forward gap is 31.5 min (p25 31.3 / p75 31.9), so a queue-aware maker fill-sim on them carries ~2 forward intervals per leg at half-hour resolution, coarse for a queue model whose events are second-scale. The sports books run at the same cadence (median 31.1 min) and are blocked by something else entirely: only 224 of 3,551 units are labeled and every labeled sports unit predates 2026-07-16, the settlement_ledger freeze, with 198,086 snapshots across 3,152 units captured since then that nothing can score. L357 2026-08-15 A helper that honestly returns None for an undefined statistic is only as honest as its CALLER — q43's lead-lag headline mapped a None rho to the sentinel -1.0 and then compared >=, so TWO undefined legs compared EQUAL and the left operand won by argument order. pearson()'s own docstring promises "an undefined correlation is honestly None, never a fabricated 0.0"; one function up, dominant = "perp_leads" if _abs(perp_leads) >= _abs(binary_leads) else "binary_leads" threw that away. On real committed tape this run it printed dominant lead direction (by abs-rho): perp_leads from perp_leads n=2 rho=None / binary_leads n=2 rho=None — the L-speed thesis's entire directional premise, decided by nothing. The same run measured WHY the legs are that thin, and it is the second half of the lesson: Q43's gate counts the wrong unit. The item is gated on >=7 perp_tape DAY-FILES (30 present, open since 07-23) with an advisory on CAPTURES/day (27 of 30 days thin), but its future CI resamples the MARKET-HOUR (L6) — and the joined population is 100 snapshots over 83 market-hours (1.2 per unit) from 356 perp BBO rows vs 1,693 binary snapshots (1,593 dropped for no perp inside the +/-300s join window), yielding 12 market-hours with any change-pair and 14/2/2 pairs on the three legs. Leg 2 is capped independently: 12 fee-clearing dislocations, all 12 depth-UNMEASURABLE, 0 executable — "not checkable", never "checked and fillable" (L216). A calendar gate that is open while the bootstrap unit is empty reads as ANALYSIS for three weeks (2026-07-25 -> 2026-08-15) with nobody re-measuring. Same defect class as Q37's 2026-08-03 "the gate counts the wrong UNIT", one item over. L358 2026-08-15 A registry that publishes its own recall limit does not thereby close it — and the blind spot it names is where the next source will be. core/settlement_sources.py states in its docstring that undeclared_settlement_dirs() matches DIRECTORY NAMES and *"structurally CANNOT detect a family that hides settlement inside another family's record schema"*, and notes that 3 of its 10 declared sources are exactly that shape. That honest disclosure sat in the module for 8 days and every run read the zero it returns as a clean queue. The first FIELD-level scan of the committed tree (31 families, 2,135,008 lines, 0 malformed) found two undeclared capture families carrying 367 net-new binary labels the sanctioned resolver cannot see (tape/sports_history/ 341, tape/sports_history_s7/ 291, minus 4 already resolvable) — plus a third, tape/sports_clv_s7/ (167), correctly excluded as a DERIVED artefact this repo computed itself, because declaring a derived family launders our own number back in as broker truth. Where a cross-check exists the undeclared sources agree with broker truth on 7/7 tickers, so this is recall, not conflict. The general rule: a published limit is a POINTER AT WORK, not a discharge of it — when a guard's own docstring names the shape it cannot see, that shape is a standing audit item, and the complement (here: a field-level detector) is the deliverable. L359 2026-08-15 The label famine is a CADENCE property, not a coverage property: every sports family we already collect carries the exchange's settled-market schema and we have never once observed it filled in, because no leg ever re-polls a ticker after expiry. Measured on the committed tree: tape/sports_pairs/ has 31,016 captured market objects with result populated on ZERO of them and status == "active" on all 31,016 across 13,404 files; tape/universe_sweep/ has 1,100,000 records, 1,807 with status == "closed", and settled/finalized/determined on ZERO — the full-universe sweep has watched markets stop trading 1,807 times and never seen one resolve. This reframes the 2026-08-15 depth-census conclusion (L356) at the mechanism level: the fix is not "collect more families" but "re-poll tickers we already know, after they expire", which is a materially cheaper collector change than a new settlement family. It also bounds the cheap hypothesis it was built to test — 367 net-new labels move the sports price substrate from 5.6% to 6.0% resolvable (659 -> 697 of 11,663 legs) and the maker-fill depth substrate by 4 event units out of 4,171 — so the famine is REAL ABSENCE and cannot be repaired from tape already held. A label count is not evidence: quote what it lands on, or it overstates what any probe could score. L360 2026-08-16 A field named like a schedule can be an OUTCOME field: Kalshi's close_time is a placeholder while a market is open and is REWRITTEN to the actual close instant at settlement — always earlier, median 51.9h, up to 13.9 days — and it is perfectly stable in both surrounding regimes, which is what makes it look trustworthy. Measured on committed tape over all 8 settlement-cache blobs (1,689 paired observations / 533 distinct tickers), classified by the SETTLEMENT STATE of each row rather than by the timestamps under test: settled_to_settled 1,588 observations, 0 changed; open_to_open 3, 0 changed; open_to_settled 98, 96 changed, 96 EARLIER, 0 later. Live control on a different family and collector (tape/universe_sweep/, 1,100,000 records): 36,765 tickers re-observed, 0 close_time changes while open. Granularity corroborates the mechanism — pre-close 20/60 sit on a round hour and 10/60 are second-precise; post-close 58/60 are second-precise. CONSEQUENCE, and the half that bites: using the settled close_time to SELECT pre-close rows (captured_at < close_time) is correct, but using it as a FEATURE — ttc_seconds, ttc_hours, "the last snapshot before close", a close-DATE bucket — is a LOOK-AHEAD of a median 51.9h, because the live observable was the placeholder. scripts/q51_m3_fill_projection.py states the falsified premise out loud (close_time is *"a SCHEDULE field, never an outcome"*, :51/:118-119) and reads it from a deliberately FROZEN pre-settlement cache, on which 47 of 60 tickers (78.3%) change close DATE; q26/q28 build ttc features the same way. No verdict flips and that is DIRECTION, not luck: all affected probes are DEAD or admissible-null, and a look-ahead only inflates. Generalize: before trusting a cached field as immutable, check it in the regime CHANGE, not inside either regime — stability measured only within a state is precision evidence, never recall (L155). L361 2026-08-16 resolve_market_results's first-BINARY-wins precedence makes a label CONFLICT structurally invisible: the losing label is discarded silently and the resolver reports full confidence. Only cache-vs-cache comparison sees it, which nothing did for the 8 committed blobs until the 2026-08-16 close_time audit. 2 · Strategy registry — hypotheses the codebase investigated and closed

A distinct class of "problem": each S-candidate is a falsifiable money-making hypothesis. The prime directive lets one graduate only on a bootstrapped CI > 0 at real fillable asks. Most die there — a dead strategy recorded honestly is a success, not a failure. 23 dead, 5 still collecting, 0 live.

3 · How recent logs are read & acted on

The findings and lessons above aren't passive notes — an autonomous cloud loop system reads them every few hours and moves problems up the enforcement gradient. This is the read→act machinery.

kalshi-collector hourly · Haiku
Runs python -m collection.hourly_pass — appends L2 tape. Nothing else, ever. Push to main fails intermittently → strands tape on tape/hourly-* branches (L17).
kalshi-research-loop every 3h · Sonnet 5
The doer. Steps 0a→9 of LOOP-QUEUE.md: history-integrity check → claim-check open PRs → step 0b stranded-tape sweep → execute ONE queue milestone → gates (pytest + invariants.py --full) → append to kb/00-LOG.md → PR + self-merge. Idle-run policy: "sweep only" is invalid — an idle run must convert an UNENFORCED lesson into an invariant, prep a gated probe, or run a data-quality deep-dive.
kalshi-edge-hunter nightly 04:15Z · Opus 4.8
The thinking seat. Reads the last 24h of kb/00-LOG.md + new findings/, then: (1) adversarially re-checks one load-bearing number per finding — a failure opens a GitHub issue + Priority:high phone note; (2) replenishes the queue (Q21 idea-gen) if <2 eligible items; (3) pre-builds scripts for probes whose data-gate opens within 72h. Ends with a plain-English ntfy brief.
kb-distiller agent · on demand
The compounder. Turns verified findings + worker lesson-candidates into new ledger rows, and escalates UNENFORCED rows into invariants/tests. This is the mechanism that empties the orange column above.
kalshi-weekly-retro Sun 12:00Z · Opus 4.8
Diffs live routines vs ops/ROUTINES.md, flags stuck PRs >5d, verifies the daily briefs actually flowed (a silent day is Priority:high), reports stranded-branch trend.
ntfy-watch hourly
Relays every leg's phone note to Ryan's device over a secret ntfy topic — the human-in-the-loop channel for anything needing a decision.

Two guardrails on acting. (a) The two-agent verdict rule: no registry flip / bootstrap CI / kill decision commits without an independent verifier agent re-run confirming it — unconfirmed verdicts are PROVISIONAL and flip nothing. (b) Structural stop: a cloud run can never place a trade — the gate is missing credentials (paper tier only), not behavior. Two operational scars are baked into step 0: cloud sessions cannot git push origin main (L4, permission boundary) and main was silently rewound to a 6-day-old checkpoint on 2026-07-08 (step 0a integrity check exists because of it).

Self-contained · no external requests · regenerate with python scripts/gen_problems_dashboard.py. Problem definitions are exactly the repo's own: a lesson is a row in the ledger; a dead strategy is a falsified hypothesis; an operational incident is whichever lesson/log entry recorded it. Nothing here is hand-authored — all rows are parsed from the append-only registries.
